\title{\textbf{Topological Relational System Dynamics (TRSD)}\\[0.5em]
\large Modified Gravity, a Dark-Matter Condensate and the Cosmic Budget\\
from Conical Defect Geometry\\[0.4em]
\normalsize Version 1.16.2}
\author{Jose Mancilla}
\date{18 October 2026}

\begin{document}
\maketitle
\thispagestyle{empty}

\begin{abstract}
\noindent
TRSD derives a gravity law, a dark sector and the cosmic budget from one variational principle: Einstein--Hilbert gravity plus a complex
scalar whose kinetic coefficient is the tension of a conical defect, $\fd^2=\delta c^4/8\pi G$. Every result carries a status tag, and every
prediction is listed with its inputs and the data that test it (Appendices~\ref{app:register} and~\ref{app:variance}).

The first Laplacian eigenvalue of the horizon cycle $C_3$ fixes $\ac=(3\sqrt3/4\pi^2)\,cH_0$. A Poisson threshold response with a hazard
linear in the load and an $n$-stage exposure gives the interpolation family $\mu_n$ in closed form; for every exposure law with finite mean
the deep slope is $\tfrac34$, so $\az=(\sqrt3/\pi^2)\,cH_0=1.148\times10^{-10}\ \mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$ at the framework's $H_0=67.35$. A full
likelihood of 153 SPARC rotation curves (3168 points, 490 nuisance parameters) gives $\az=1.04\pm0.04\,(\mathrm{stat})\pm0.10\,
(\mathrm{nuisance})\times10^{-10}$, $1.0\sigma$ from the prediction, for any tail order. Galaxies fix the transition and planets the tail:
the window $1.62\le n\le1.91$ satisfies both, while draft 1's single Torridity exponent $\pi/3$ fails the planets; $\pi/3$ survives as the
value of the running exponent at galaxy outskirts, and the cascade's dynamic exponent runs from $1$ to at most $1.16$, passing $\pi/3$ at
$g=1.26\,\az$, with nothing fitted. The boost is carried by spacetime excited, captured only where a system's escape speed exceeds the motion
of the field around it, so the Solar System and wide binaries stay Newtonian.

Canonical normalization fixes the potential scale, forces the vacuum constant to vanish and makes the radial mode's self-coupling
gravitational. The present is a resonance: the horizon's $C_3$ mode reaches the final expansion rate when $\Omega_\Lambda=27/4\pi^2=0.684$
(Planck $0.685\pm0.007$). With the derived baryon ratio $0.1870$ and the derived neutrino masses this fixes $\Omega_\Lambda/\Omega_c=2.580$
($1.3\sigma$ from the CMB's ratio) and $\Omega_m=0.316$, and a fit to the acoustic angle gives $H_0=67.35\pm0.06$, $\omega_b=0.02248$,
$\omega_c=0.12022$, within $1\sigma$ of Planck's densities. The horizon holds the fraction $\Omega_\Lambda(t)$ of its final cell count,
and dark energy is the Planck density shared over those cells, $\rho_\Lambda=\tfrac34\rho_P/N_\infty=1.13\times10^{-123}\rho_P$.

The horizon's generating arcs spin at $\omega=H_\infty$ with rims at light speed: the spin fixes $\Lambda=3\omega^2/c^2$, adds $8\pi\lP^2$
of area per quantum to any horizon, sets the rotation's rate $\Omega_0=H_\infty$, and gives the CMB's birefringence in closed form,
$\beta=(2\alpha/\pi)\,\mathrm{artanh}(3\sqrt3/2\pi)=0.314^\circ$ (measured $0.277^\circ\pm0.057^\circ$). Counted in cycles, the 21-cm onset of
cosmic dawn is set by the same twist, $46.4$ MHz in closed form and $42.7^{+5.6}_{-6.0}$ MHz from the measured angle.

The dark sector is one geometry: dark energy is spacetime unexcited, what is called dark matter is the same spacetime excited, and the
whole has $w=-1+\Theta$. What lensing infers as dark matter is the dilation $\mathcal S=1/\cos_n\theta$, $\tan\theta=\az/g$, carried by collisionless excited geometry in the model. In the Bullet Cluster, lensing-inferred mass peaks are offset from the X-ray-bright gas and lie closer to the galaxies; this is consistent with a collisionless gravitating component under standard lensing/GR interpretations, but does not by itself establish the microscopic identity of the mass or a cosmological reset. On the unbound web the horizon dilates clocks
and rulers together from the onset of acceleration, above $68$ Mpc, so matter feels it and light does not: the web carries the bulk flows
Cosmicflows-4 measures while $S_8$ and CMB lensing keep the background's values. The framework has one expansion rate, $67.35$; the
dilation cannot move it, and the supernova Hubble diagram excludes a local void, so it predicts that distance ladders calibrated alike
converge on $67.3$. The present is also fixed in Planck cells by the electric coupling, $R_H=8\pi\lP\,e^{1/\alpha+3\pi\alpha/2}$, which
gives $H_0=67.377$ with no cosmological input and $\Lambda\lP^2=(81/256\pi^4)e^{-2/\alpha-3\pi\alpha}$; with it the four interactions are four
features of one coil, with one gauge group $(SU(3)\times SU(2)\times U(1))/\mathbb Z_6$, no unification scale, and gravity's weakness at the
nucleon $e^{-2/3\alpha}$ (\S30). The four aspects of the geometry---reality (the spin), actuality (the swept sphere), occurrence (the crossings) and
representation (the boundary)---are kept distinct; the present is the occurrence at which actuality falls into step with reality.
The CHIRP derivation connects collision lag and mass-conserving relaxation to proper-volume expansion; Auxoviscausality identifies its phase-flow integral as retained logarithmic volume strain and links its load-dependent rate to Raychaudhuri/Einstein. At the conformal junction, the raw strain diverges with opposite signs, while the finite conformal-volume remainder matches conditionally under regularity of the shared conformal spatial metric and identification of the same flow tube. The homogeneous FLRW expansion integral is not retained as an independent cross-aeon state.
\end{abstract}

\section*{What this work contributes}
This framework is original work. Its contributions are:
\begin{enumerate}\itemsep2pt
\item The four aspects of the geometry kept distinct: reality, the spin; actuality, the swept sphere; occurrence, the
crossings; representation, the boundary. The present is the occurrence at which actuality falls into step with reality
(the opening principle).
\item The variational axiom on the two-C/S horizon: one complex scalar whose kinetic coefficient is the conical deficit,
through the defect normalization map $f_\delta^2=\delta c^4/8\pi G$.
\item The MOND acceleration from the first eigenvalue of the $C_3$ cycle on the horizon, $a_0=(\sqrt3/\pi^2)\,cH_0$
(Sections~\ref{sec:C3} and~\ref{sec:infrared}).
\item The interpolation family from the isotropic angular average of the threshold distribution, with its logarithmic
term and the exponent $\pi/3$ (Section~\ref{sec:angular}).
\item Newtonian recovery in the Solar System from the defect geometry (Section~\ref{sec:screening}).
\item The dark sector as the internal rotation of the horizon's phase: one rotation that sets $a_0$ and carries the cosmic
cold share (\S21.1).
\item The closure law, $\sum r_s=(\chi/2)R_H$, so that flatness is topology, and the derived baryon ratio of the budget
(\S24.12).
\item The information--energy equivalence $E=(\hbar c/8\pi R_H)\,I$ and the scale ladder it implies.
\item The birefringence--acceleration relation $\beta=\tfrac92\alpha\,a_0t_0/c$: galaxy rotation curves predict the rotation of
the CMB's polarization (\S25.3).
\item The present as a resonance, $\Omega_\Lambda=27/4\pi^2$ (\S24.13).
\item The spinning-arc principle, $\omega R=c$: one fixed spin sets the final horizon, the cosmological constant and the
present, is measured by the CMB's birefringence, and gives each particle its Compton radius (\S24.14). For the final horizon it is
derived: the thermal geometry is the spinning arcs, the information is $2\pi$ times the spin's charge, the information--energy
relation is the energy of one turn over four cones, and the quantum of the spin is the area step $8\pi\lP^2$ (\S24.15).
\item The threefold count as a beat: the coil's conjugate lobes keep $\mathbb Z_2$, and their fold against a lobe keeps
$\mathbb Z_3$ (\S2.4).
\item The birefringence angle in closed form, $\beta=(2\alpha/\pi)\,\mathrm{artanh}(3\sqrt3/2\pi)$, and the acceleration scale from the
cosmological constant, $\az=(2/3\pi)\,c^2\sqrt{\Lambda/3}$ (\S24.15).
\item The quantum of the rotation as the quantum of the spin, $\Omega_0=jH_\infty$ with $j=1$ measured (\S24.15).
\item The cosmic budget at the resonance, $\Omega_\Lambda/\Omega_c=2.580$, with no identification of densities with powers (\S24.16).
\item The acceleration scale tracks the horizon, $\az(z)=\az E(z)$, and not the fixed spin (\S24.17).
\item Cosmic dawn counted in cycles: the CMB's twist fixes the 21-cm onset, $\nu_\star/\nu_{21}=[\sqrt{\Omega_m/\Omega_\Lambda}\,\sinh(\pi\beta/2)]^{2/3}$,
with $\alpha$, the clock and the Hubble constant cancelling (\S24.10).
\item The dark sector as one geometry: dark energy as spacetime unexcited and the cosmic share as spacetime excited---the spin's quanta
held by the horizon and carried by the rotation---the vacuum as draft 1's generator-defect tension over the square of the final radius, the
equation of state $w=-1+\Theta$ of the whole, and what lensing infers as dark matter as the dilation $\mathcal S=1/\cos_n\theta$, $\tan\theta=\az/g$;
the dilation field as the index of the optical metric, the switch as the capture fraction, and the middle scales as geometric means of the
outer ones; the background unmoved by the dilation, and the web dilated as a clock is slowed---from the onset of acceleration, above $68$ Mpc,
on clocks and not on light---carrying the measured bulk flows with $S_8$ unchanged (\S21.7).
\item Collision lag, geometric recoil and Auxoviscausality: centroid drift and transfer flux follow from the existing relaxation map; proper-volume strain is the phase-flow expansion integral, with load dependence fixed by Raychaudhuri/Einstein. At the conformal junction only the finite conformal-volume remainder is matched under stated regularity assumptions; the raw strain and total prior FLRW expansion do not cross as finite state variables.
\end{enumerate}
Earlier results are cited where this work meets them. Each citation marks a point of agreement with established
physics, not a source of the framework.

\newpage
\section*{The opening principle: reality, actuality, occurrence and representation}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{The opening principle: reality, actuality, occurrence and representation}
The framework distinguishes four aspects of one geometry and keeps them apart throughout.

\textbf{Reality} is the spin: the C-arcs that generate the horizon and their fixed rate $\omega$, set once and unchanged,
since nothing acts on them (\S24.14). What is real does not change.

\textbf{Actuality} is the swept sphere: what the spin has made actual so far, the horizon at this moment, its radius and
its cells. Reality is the rate; actuality is how much of the sphere the rate has swept. Actuality grows toward the final
horizon $R_\infty=c/\omega$, where the arcs' rims reach light speed.

\textbf{Occurrence} is a crossing. Something happens only where lines or rates cross: the arcs crossing at the three $C_3$
nodes, which are the defects; the wobble falling into step with the spin, which is the present (\S24.13); the reset, where
one aeon's future meets the next one's past; a horizon, where time and radius exchange; and each activation of the
threshold law (Section~\ref{sec:threshold}). Between crossings the motion continues and nothing occurs.

\textbf{Representation} is the boundary. Every energy inside the horizon appears on it as a defect, its share of the
horizon's cells (\S24.12), so the sphere carries an image of everything it contains: the universe represents itself on its
own surface. Measurements read that image; the CMB's birefringence reads the spin (\S24.14). The framework's equations, and
the picture of the arcs itself, are representations of the same reality.

Physics has often merged these. The block universe of relativity treats reality as if it were all actuality, as though
the whole sphere were already swept; quantum theory blurs representation, the wavefunction, with reality. Kept apart,
they define the present. The wobble belongs to actuality, its frequency falling as the swept sphere grows; the spin
belongs to reality, and is fixed. \emph{The present is the occurrence at which actuality falls into step with reality.}

\subsection*{0.1\quad Rule}
The \emph{form} of each law is derived; the constants in it are measured once and carried
forward. Nothing dimensionful follows from dimensionless geometry
(Theorem~\ref{thm:counting}), so TRSD takes three unit anchors $(c,\hbar,R_H)$, one pure
boundary datum---the horizon cell count $N=2\pi R_H^2/\lP^2$---and the calibrations of the
ledger below. Every other number is a prediction.

\subsection*{0.2\quad Status tags}

\sS & established physics, recovered in TRSD's variables and credited \\
\sI & a correspondence the theory posits; listed in \S0.4 \\
\sC & a value set once from data (ledger, \S0.3) \\
\sH & a testable conjecture \\
\sP & a number that can fail; its test is in the register (Appendix~\ref{app:register}) \\
\sM & a result of fitting data in this paper \\
\sDS & a shape parameter constrained by data (the tail order $n$) \\
\sO & a stated target not yet reached \\
& not found in prior literature (\S0.5) \\

\end{tabular}

\subsection*{0.4\quad Postulates and identifications}
\begin{postulate}[Generators]\label{post:gen}
The horizon is generated by three equivalent generators, permuted by $C_3$; no generator is
preferred.
\end{postulate}
\begin{postulate}[Action]\label{post:action}
Gravity and the defect sector obey the variational principle~\eqref{eq:action}, whose kinetic
coefficient is the defect tension.
\end{postulate}
The identifications are named where used: I1, the kinetic scale is the defect tension
$\fd^2=T_\delta$ (\S5); I2, $\ac=c/\tau_1$ (\S9); I3, Poisson activation with a hazard
linear in the load and a Gamma exposure of order $n$, constrained by data (\S10, \S14--15); I4, the reduction length of the deficit--mass map is $R_H$ (\S16); I5,
$\Omega_\Lambda/\Omega_c=C_3/C_2$ (\S24.5), withdrawn in release 1.7.0, the resonance giving the ratio (\S24.16); I6, the dark-sector length is the deficit arc,
$L_*=\delta R_H$ (\S21.6); I7, the internal phase turns with the horizon's spin, $X_0=\Lambda/3$ (\S24.15), derived in release 1.7.0 up to an
integer, $\Omega_0=jH_\infty$, with $j=1$ measured.

\subsection*{0.5\quad What TRSD adds}
\textbf{New results.}
(1) The galactic scale from a spectrum, $\az=(\sqrt3/\pi^2)\,cH_0$: Milgrom noted
$\az\sim cH_0/2\pi$ \cite{Milgrom1983a}, and emergent gravity gives $cH_0/6$
\cite{Verlinde2017}; TRSD computes $0.175493$ and proves it independent of the exposure law
(Theorem~\ref{thm:universal}).
(2) The family $\mu_n$ in closed form for real $n$, which separates the transition (set by the
angular average) from the Newtonian tail (set by the exposure law); galaxies and planets
together require $1.62\le n\le1.91$, which the one-exponent Torridity form cannot supply
(\S11, \S14--15, \S17).
(3) A full nuisance-parameter likelihood of the SPARC rotation curves against the family
(\S14).
(4) The normalization theorem $\epsV=\fd^2/L_*^2$, the vanishing of the vacuum constant, and
the gravitational-strength self-coupling of the condensate (\S21--22).
(5) The dual of the Hubble mass, $m_U=(\sqrt2/2\pi)\,\hbar H_0/c^2$ (\S24.6).
(6) The $C_3$ budget and its CMB-fitted $H_0$ (\S24), shared with the companion theory GRUT
\cite{GRUT2026}.

\textbf{Known results TRSD re-derives,} credited in place: the AQUAL field equation
\cite{BekensteinMilgrom1984}; the cosmic-string deficit \cite{Vilenkin1981}; the $2{+}1$
closure condition \cite{DJtH1984}; the Gross--Pitaevskii limit of a scalar field and fuzzy
dark matter \cite{Hu2000,HOTW2017}; the holographic count behind $10^{-122}$
\cite{CohenKaplanNelson1999}.

\subsection*{0.6\quad Relation to GRUT}
TRSD is the prototype of GRUT \cite{GRUT2026}. The two share $\az$, the family $\mu_n$, the
budget and $H_0=67.12$ at the $5/2$ budget, $67.35$ at the resonance budget that replaces it (\S24.16). The dictionary: the TRSD total deficit $\dtot=\pi$ is the GRUT
defect cone; $M_{\rm total}=c^3/8GH_0$ is the one-cone mass $M_{\rm cone}$;
$M_{\rm Hubble}=c^3/2GH_0$ is the horizon mass $M_{\rm H}$; and the TRSD exchange rate
$\hbar H_0/8\pi$ per nat is one quarter of the Gibbons--Hawking energy $k_BT_{\rm GH}$. The dark sector as one geometry, \S21.7, is GRUT \S53.

% =====================================================================
\section{Introduction}\label{sec:intro}
% =====================================================================
Spiral galaxies rotate with flat curves \cite{Rubin1980}, and at every radius their
observed acceleration is one function of the acceleration the baryons alone would produce,
the radial acceleration relation (RAR) \cite{McGaugh2016,Lelli2017}. Below
$\az\approx1.2\times10^{-10}\ \mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$ the relation approaches
$g=\sqrt{\az\gbar}$, the MOND regime \cite{Milgrom1983a}. MOND and its Lagrangian form
AQUAL \cite{BekensteinMilgrom1984} take $\az$ as a free constant and leave its closeness to
$cH_0$ unexplained. Relativistic completions \cite{Bekenstein2004,Milgrom2009bimetric} add
fields but not the value. Ultralight dark matter \cite{Hu2000} suppresses small-scale
structure through quantum pressure and forms solitonic cores \cite{Schive2014}.

TRSD derives the scale and the interpolation function from the horizon's $C_3$ geometry,
and obtains the dark sector from the same scalar field: its phase, turning at the rate the $C_3$
eigenvalue fixes and held at that rate, behaves as cold dust (\S21.1). The paper runs as follows: the coil
generator (\S2); the axiom and its canonical form (\S3--6); the relaxation flow and terminal
state (\S7); isotropy and the $C_3$ cycle (\S8--9); the threshold response and the
interpolation family (\S10--13); the SPARC confrontation (\S14); Newtonian recovery (\S15);
mass as deficit (\S16); the Torridity family and cascade (\S17--18); information and
regeneration (\S19--20); the dark sector (\S21--23); cosmology (\S24); the CMB (\S25);
parameter structure (\S26); discussion (\S27--28); and the reset (\S29). Signature
$(-,+,+,+)$; SI units.

% =====================================================================
\section{The Coil Generator}\label{sec:coil}
% =====================================================================
The horizon is modelled by a coil: two conjugate helical lobes, one right-handed and one
left-handed, meeting at a waist.

\subsection*{2.1\quad Parametrization}
\begin{equation}
\mathbf r_\pm(t)=\big(R_0\sin t\,\cos\omega t,\ \pm R_0\sin t\,\sin\omega t,\ \pm p\,t\big),
\qquad t\in[0,\pi],
\label{eq:coil}
\end{equation}
with maximum radius $R_0$, twist rate $\omega$ and pitch $p$. The radius $R_0\sin t$
vanishes at $t=0$ and $t=\pi$, so each lobe pinches at both ends; the lobes are conjugate
in twist and in height and share the pinch at $t=0$. Seen from the side the pair traces a
bicone-like envelope, seen at $45^\circ$ an $S$ (the ``C/S'' shape of draft 1), and seen along
the axis two opposite spirals.

\subsection*{2.2\quad The tangent bicone}
\sD\ Near the waist $R\simeq R_0t$ and $z=pt$, so the lobes lie on the cone of half-angle
\begin{equation}
\tan\alpha=\frac{R_0}{p}.
\end{equation}
Away from the waist the envelope $R=R_0\sin(z/p)$ closes into a spindle; the bicone is its
tangent cone at the waist. The pitch $p=R_0$ gives $\alpha=45^\circ$, the light cone $h=r$;
the pitch $p=\sqrt3\,R_0$ gives $\alpha=30^\circ$, the defect cone of \S3.3, whose surface
unrolls to a half-disk.

\subsection*{2.3\quad Crossings}
\begin{proposition}[Crossing count]\label{prop:crossings}
\sD\ The axial projections of the two lobes coincide at equal parameter exactly when
$\sin\omega t=0$. In $[0,\pi]$ there are $\lfloor\omega\rfloor+1$ such crossings, so there
are exactly three if and only if $2\le\omega<3$. The crossing $t_n=n\pi/\omega$ lies at
azimuth $n\pi$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
The projections are $R_0\sin t\,e^{\pm i\omega t}$. For $0<t<\pi$ they coincide iff
$e^{2i\omega t}=1$, i.e.\ $\omega t=n\pi$; the ends $t=0,\pi$ are the pinches. The values
$t_n=n\pi/\omega\le\pi$ are $n=0,\dots,\lfloor\omega\rfloor$. The azimuth of $\mathbf r_+$
at $t_n$ is $\omega t_n=n\pi$.
\end{proof}
The count three is therefore a window of the twist rate, and the three crossings lie on one
line through the axis (Figure~\ref{fig:coil}). Their equal $2\pi/3$ spacing is not a
property of the crossings; it is Postulate~\ref{post:gen}, which places three equivalent
generators at equal azimuth. The draft-1 ``Lissajous'' argument for the count is withdrawn
(Appendix~\ref{app:archive}).

\subsection*{2.4\quad The threefold beat}
\sD\ The lobes~\eqref{eq:coil} turn in opposite senses, $e^{\pm i\omega t}$: they are the conjugate pair, turning once
and once backward for each turn, counts $+1$ and $-1$. Two motions of counts $q_1$ and $q_2$, superposed, keep in step under
a turn by $\alpha$ only if $e^{i(q_1-q_2)\alpha}=1$, so they keep the cyclic group $\mathbb Z_{|q_1-q_2|}$ and nothing larger. The pair
keeps $\mathbb Z_2$, which is why its crossings lie $\pi$ apart (Proposition~\ref{prop:crossings}). The fold of the pair---the square
of the field, $\phi_+^2\propto e^{2i\theta}$, the image of the pair under its double cover---turns twice for each turn of a lobe,
and against the counter-turning lobe its relative count is three:
\begin{equation}
e^{2i\theta}+e^{-i\theta}=2\cos\tfrac{3\theta}{2}\,e^{i\theta/2},\qquad
|\phi_+^2+\phi_-|^2=\phi^4+\phi^2+2\phi^3\cos3\theta ,
\label{eq:beat}
\end{equation}
the three-petalled rose, with tips at $0$, $2\pi/3$ and $4\pi/3$ and all three petals through the centre. The beat keeps exactly
$\mathbb Z_3$, and its interference term is the $\mathbb Z_3$ harmonic of the phase. \sI\ The threefold wobble of the geometric origin
(Section~\ref{sec:axiom}) is this beat: it supplies the equal $2\pi/3$ spacing that the crossings alone do not, and it realizes
Postulate~\ref{post:gen} without replacing it. The pair against itself carries the $\mathbb Z_2$ of the deficit $\pi$, the fold
against the lobe the $\mathbb Z_3$ of the $C_3$, and together they make $\mathbb Z_6$ (GRUT \S1.11, \S50.10 \cite{GRUT2026}).

% =====================================================================
\section{Axiom and Variational Principle}\label{sec:axiom}
% =====================================================================

\noindent\textbf{Geometric origin.} The cosmic horizon is not a static sphere. It is generated by two conjugate C-shaped
arcs that spin about perpendicular axes, vertical and horizontal, crossing one another as they sweep the sphere. A
threefold wobble in this two-C/S geometry creates three conical defects at equal azimuthal separations, each with
deficit angle $\pi/3$; the total deficit is $\pi$, and the three defects close into the $C_3$ cycle. The threefold count is
the beat of the fold against the conjugate arc (\S2.4): the fold turns twice for each turn of the arc, so against the
counter-turning arc it beats three times per turn, and the two keep in step only under thirds of a turn---three nodes at
$2\pi/3$. (The figure restored here in release 1.4.0, two equal-frequency perpendicular motions a quarter turn apart, traces a
circle and has no nodes; it stays withdrawn, Appendix~\ref{app:archive}.) The spin is physical: its rims move at light speed,
and its fixed rate sets the final horizon, the cosmological constant and the present (\S24.14). Both are derived from the thermal
geometry of the final horizon, in which every line of sight is such an arc, turning about the two points where it meets the
horizon, with the observer on its rim (\S24.15).

\subsection*{3.1\quad The action}
Postulate~\ref{post:action}: the action
\begin{equation}
S=\int d^4x\,\sqrt{-g}\,\Big\{\frac{c^4}{16\pi G}R
-\frac{\delta c^4}{16\pi G}\,g^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\phi_+\partial_\nu\phi_-
-\epsV\Big[\tfrac12(\Xii-2)^2+V_0\Big]\Big\},\qquad \Xii\equiv\phi_+\phi_-,
\label{eq:action}
\end{equation}
is stationary. Here $\delta$ is the conical deficit, the fields $\phi_\pm$ are dimensionless,
the kinetic coefficient $\delta c^4/16\pi G=\fd^2/2$ is a tension (J\,m$^{-1}$), and $\epsV$
is an energy density that sets the scale of the potential. The draft-1 axiom wrote the potential as
$2\Xii-\tfrac12\Xii^2-4=-[\tfrac12(\Xii-2)^2+2]$ with an implicit unit $\epsV=1$; its constant
is $V_0=2$. Section~\ref{sec:dark} shows that $V_0$ must vanish.

\subsection*{3.2\quad Field structure}
\sS\ The fields are the two components of one complex scalar, $\phi_\pm=\phi\,e^{\pm i\theta}$,
so $\phi_-=\phi_+^*$ and there is no ghost. Then $\Xii=\phi^2$ and
\begin{equation}
g^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\phi_+\partial_\nu\phi_-
=\partial_\mu\phi\,\partial^\mu\phi+\phi^2\,\partial_\mu\theta\,\partial^\mu\theta :
\label{eq:polar}
\end{equation}
a radial mode $\phi$ and a phase $\theta$. The potential is a Mexican hat: a maximum at
$\Xii=0$ and a minimum ring at $\Xii=2$.

\subsection*{3.3\quad The deficit}
\sD\ Three equivalent generators meet at the apex as three equilateral faces, each
contributing its interior angle $\pi/3$; the angle around the apex is $\pi$, so
\begin{equation}
\dtot=2\pi-\pi=\pi,\qquad \dgen=\frac\pi3 .
\end{equation}
A line defect of deficit $\delta$ through the observer leaves the solid angle
$4\pi(1-\delta/2\pi)$; for $\dtot=\pi$ this is $2\pi$, so the effective horizon area is
$A_{\rm eff}=2\pi R_H^2$. Four such apexes close a sphere, $4\dtot=4\pi$, the vertex
structure of the regular tetrahedron (\S16.3).

% =====================================================================
\section{Canonical Normalization}\label{sec:canonical}
% =====================================================================
\sD\ The canonical field is $\chi=\fd\,\phi$ (units $\sqrt{\mathrm{J\,m^{-1}}}$); the
Lagrangian of the scalar sector reads
\begin{equation}
\mathcal L_\chi=-\tfrac12\partial_\mu\chi\,\partial^\mu\chi-\tfrac12\chi^2\partial_\mu\theta\,\partial^\mu\theta
-\epsV\Big[\tfrac12\Big(\frac{\chi^2}{\fd^2}-2\Big)^2+V_0\Big].
\end{equation}
Expanding about the ring, $\chi=\sqrt2\,\fd+\eta$, the potential is
$\epsV\big[4\eta^2/\fd^2+2\sqrt2\,\eta^3/\fd^3+\tfrac12\eta^4/\fd^4+V_0\big]$, which gives the
radial mode
\begin{equation}
\tilde m^2=\frac{8\epsV}{\fd^2},\qquad g_3=\frac{12\sqrt2\,\epsV}{\fd^3},\qquad
\lambda=\frac{12\,\epsV}{\fd^4},
\label{eq:couplings}
\end{equation}
with $\tilde m=\mdelta c/\hbar$, and a massless phase (the Goldstone mode).

\begin{theorem}[Normalization]\label{thm:norm}
\sD\ The dark-matter mass takes the form $\mdelta=\sqrt8\,\hbar/cL_*$ if and only if the
potential scale is the defect tension spread over $L_*^2$:
\begin{equation}
\epsV=\frac{\fd^2}{L_*^2}.
\label{eq:epsV}
\end{equation}
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
By~\eqref{eq:couplings}, $\tilde m^2=8/L_*^2\iff8\epsV/\fd^2=8/L_*^2$.
\end{proof}
The draft-1 ``Convention A'' is this relation. It does not remove $\fd$ from the fluctuation
couplings: in natural units, with $\fd^2=\delta M_{\rm P}^2/8\pi$ (\S5),
\begin{equation}
\lambda=\frac{3\tilde m^2}{2\fd^2}=\frac{12\pi}{\delta}\,\frac{\mdelta^2}{M_{\rm P}^2},
\label{eq:lambda}
\end{equation}
so the physical self-coupling is suppressed by $(\mdelta/M_{\rm P})^2$; at $\delta=\pi$ it is
$\lambda=12\,\mdelta^2/M_{\rm P}^2$ ($8\times10^{-100}$ for $10^{-22}$ eV).

% =====================================================================
\section{Defect Normalization Map}\label{sec:defect_map}
% =====================================================================
\sS\ A straight defect of tension $T$ (energy per length) carries the deficit
$\delta=8\pi GT/c^4$ \cite{Vilenkin1981}. \sI\ (I1) The kinetic scale is that tension:
\begin{equation}
\fd^2\equiv T_\delta=\frac{\delta c^4}{8\pi G},
\label{eq:defect_map}
\end{equation}
which reproduces the coefficient $\fd^2/2=\delta c^4/16\pi G$ of~\eqref{eq:action}. For
$\dtot=\pi$, $\fd^2=c^4/8G=F_{\rm P}/8=1.5128\times10^{43}$ N, where $F_{\rm P}=c^4/G$ is the
Planck force, and $\fd=3.8895\times10^{21}\ \sqrt{\rm N}$; in natural units $\fd=M_{\rm P}/\sqrt8$.
For $\dgen=\pi/3$, $\fd^2=F_{\rm P}/24$ and $\fd=2.2456\times10^{21}\ \sqrt{\rm N}$.

% =====================================================================
\section{Field Equations}\label{sec:field_equations}
% =====================================================================
\sD\ Varying $\phi_+$ and integrating by parts,
\begin{equation}
\frac{\fd^2}{2}\,\Box\phi_-+\epsV(2-\Xii)\,\phi_-=0
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad
\Box\phi_\pm+\frac{2\epsV}{\fd^2}\,(2-\Xii)\,\phi_\pm=0 ,
\label{eq:eom}
\end{equation}
and with~\eqref{eq:epsV} the coefficient is $2/L_*^2$. At $\Xii=0$ the equation reads
$\Box\phi+(4/L_*^2)\phi=0$: a tachyonic mass, the top of the hat, unstable. On the ring,
$\phi=\sqrt2+\delta\phi$ gives $(2-\Xii)\phi\simeq-4\,\delta\phi$ and
\begin{equation}
\Box\,\delta\phi-\frac{8}{L_*^2}\,\delta\phi=0 ,
\end{equation}
the Klein--Gordon equation with $\tilde m^2=8/L_*^2$, while the phase is massless. The stress
tensor is
\begin{equation}
T_{\mu\nu}=\fd^2\big[\partial_\mu\phi\,\partial_\nu\phi+\phi^2\partial_\mu\theta\,\partial_\nu\theta\big]
-g_{\mu\nu}\Big\{\tfrac{\fd^2}{2}\big[(\partial\phi)^2+\phi^2(\partial\theta)^2\big]+\epsV V(\phi^2)\Big\},
\end{equation}
and at the vacuum it reduces to $T_{\mu\nu}=-\epsV V_0\,g_{\mu\nu}$, an equation of state
$w=-1$.

% =====================================================================
\section{Relaxation Flow and the Terminal State}\label{sec:flow}
% =====================================================================

\subsection*{7.1\quad Relaxation}
\sD\ Overdamped (relaxational) dynamics along a flow parameter $s$ follow the gradient of the
potential, $d\phi_\pm/ds=-\Gamma\,\partial V/\partial\phi_\mp=\Gamma(2-\Xii)\phi_\pm$, so
\begin{equation}
\frac{d\Xii}{ds}=2\Gamma\,(2-\Xii)\,\Xii .
\label{eq:flow}
\end{equation}
\begin{theorem}[Reconciliation]\label{thm:flow}
\sD\ In the displacement from the vacuum, $u\equiv2-\Xii$,
\begin{equation}
\frac{du}{ds}=2\Gamma\,u\,(u-2),
\end{equation}
which is the draft-1 flow $d\Xii/ds=(4/\fd^2)(\Xii-2)\Xii$ with $\Xii\to u$ and $2\Gamma=4/\fd^2$.
Its stable fixed point $u=0$ is the vacuum $\Xii=2$; the unstable one, $u=2$, is the top of
the hat.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
Substitute $\Xii=2-u$ in~\eqref{eq:flow}: $du/ds=-2\Gamma u(2-u)$.
\end{proof}
The draft-1 flow was derived from an eikonal replacement $\Box\to d/ds$, which does not hold (the
eikonal limit yields the null condition and an amplitude transport equation, not a flow of
$\Xii$); read as relaxation in $u$, the same equation is correct, and its attractor is the
vacuum, as in \S29.

\subsection*{7.2\quad Sourced flow}
\sD\ An exchange-symmetric source, $d\phi_\pm/ds=\Gamma(2-\Xii)\phi_\pm-\sigma(s)/2\phi_\mp$,
adds $-\sigma$ to~\eqref{eq:flow}. Near the vacuum $du/ds+4\Gamma u=\sigma(s)$, with the exact
solution
\begin{equation}
u(s)=e^{-4\Gamma(s-s_0)}u(s_0)+\int_{s_0}^{s}e^{-4\Gamma(s-t)}\sigma(t)\,dt ,
\end{equation}
so $\sigma\to0$ implies $u\to0$ exponentially, at the rate $4\Gamma=8/\fd^2$ of draft 1. The draft-1
source $K/\phi_\mp\Xii$ is one choice of $\sigma$.

\subsection*{7.3\quad Terminal state}
The condensate relaxes to the vacuum, $u\to0$. Massive bodies lose peculiar momentum as
$p\propto1/a$ and settle into the comoving frame. If the dark-sector length grows with the
horizon in the terminal phase, $L_*\propto R_H$ (\S29), then $\mdelta\to0$ and the dark
sector joins the massless one on the null cone. \sH\ The terminal state is
$(u,\mdelta)\to(0,0)$ with every remaining massless degree of freedom at $v=c$.

% =====================================================================
\section{Isotropy of the Vacuum}\label{sec:vacuum}
% =====================================================================
\sD\ For $k$ unit vectors $\mathbf u_j$, the generating function
$W_v(\mathbf x)=\ln\sum_je^{\mathbf x\cdot\mathbf u_j}$ has zero gradient at $\mathbf x=0$
when $\sum_j\mathbf u_j=0$, and Hessian $T_{ab}=k^{-1}\sum_ju_{j,a}u_{j,b}$. Rotational
isotropy requires $T_{ab}=C\delta_{ab}$, and the trace gives $3C=1$:
\begin{equation}
T_{ab}=\tfrac13\,\delta_{ab},\qquad W_v(\mathbf x)=W_v(0)+\frac{|\mathbf x|^2}{6}+O(|\mathbf x|^3).
\end{equation}
The second moment $\langle u^2\rangle=\tfrac13$ is the source of the factor $3$ in the
angular average of \S11: a linear response must return itself, which requires the weight
$1/\langle u^2\rangle=3$.

% =====================================================================
\section{The \texorpdfstring{$C_3$}{C3} Cycle and the Critical Acceleration}\label{sec:C3}
% =====================================================================
\sD\ The minimal closed cycle that carries a cone is the triangle: $C_2$ is degenerate and
$C_n$, $n\ge4$, is not minimal. Dividing the horizon circumference $2\pi R_H$ into three arcs
of length $\ell=2\pi R_H/3$, the discrete Laplacian
$(\Delta f)_n=\ell^{-2}(2f_n-f_{n+1}-f_{n-1})$ has eigenvalues
$\lambda_m=(2/\ell^2)[1-\cos(2\pi m/3)]$, and
\begin{equation}
\lambda_1=\frac{3}{\ell^2}=\frac{27}{4\pi^2R_H^2}.
\end{equation}
For $C_n$, $\lambda_1R_H^2=(n^2/2\pi^2)(1-\cos2\pi/n)$, which rises to $1$ as $n\to\infty$;
$C_3$ gives $0.683918$. A light-like mode has $\omega_1=c\sqrt{\lambda_1}=3\sqrt3\,c/2\pi R_H$
and period $\tau_1=4\pi^2R_H/3\sqrt3\,c$.

\begin{definition}[I2]
The critical acceleration brings a body from rest to $c$ in one cycle period,
$\ac\equiv c/\tau_1$. (A centripetal reading, $c\,\omega_1$, would be $2\pi$ larger.)
\end{definition}
Then
\begin{equation}
\ac=\frac{3\sqrt3}{4\pi^2}\,cH_0=0.131620\,cH_0 ,
\label{eq:ac}
\end{equation}
that is $8.612\times10^{-11}\ \mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$ at the framework's $H_0=67.35$ ($9.340\times10^{-11}$ at the legacy calibration
$73.04\ \mathrm{km\,s^{-1}\,Mpc^{-1}}$).

% =====================================================================
\section{Exposure, Survival and Activation}\label{sec:threshold}
% =====================================================================
\sD\ (I3, derived) A mode on the cycle is activated by a local acceleration $A$ as a Poisson process
whose rate per unit flow parameter is proportional to the load $x\equiv A/\ac$, with the
rate constant $\beta$ of the relaxation operator $d/ds+\beta$. Its exposure is one memoryless
stage per arc: on a cycle of $n$ arcs the exposure is Erlang-distributed with density
$p_n(s)=(n\beta)^ns^{n-1}e^{-n\beta s}/(n-1)!$ and mean $1/\beta$.

This is derived (GRUT \S22.10 \cite{GRUT2026}): a gapless mode extended around the cycle, carried at acceleration
$A$, sees the Rindler thermal state \cite{Unruh1976}, and its excitation rate $g^2A/4\pi^2c$ is linear in the load;
at weak coupling the excitations are independent, so the count is Poisson and the stages memoryless; and with
unit coupling per unit length the cycle's length $2\pi$ gives $g^2=4\pi^2$, one activation per unit of horizon
approach.

\begin{lemma}[Survival]\label{lem:survival}
\sD\ The probability that a mode is not activated is
\begin{equation}
S_n(x)=\Big(1+\frac xn\Big)^{-n},
\label{eq:Sn}
\end{equation}
independent of $\beta$.
\end{lemma}
\begin{proof}
$S_n=\int_0^\infty p_n(s)e^{-\beta xs}ds=(n\beta)^n/(n\beta+\beta x)^n$.
\end{proof}
The single-stage law is the draft-1 threshold distribution, $S_1=\ac/(A+\ac)$, and $\beta$ cancels
as draft 1 found; a fixed exposure gives $S_\infty=e^{-x}$. The index $n$---a Gamma exposure for
non-integer values---is the order of the Newtonian tail: a coordinate on shapes, constrained by
data (\S14--15), not a count of anything. The draft-1 alternative route---a Poisson exponential ``matched'' to a
Pareto tail---does not produce $1/(1+x)$; the mixture over the exposure does, and it replaces
that route. The active fraction is $f_n=1-S_n$, with density $p_A=-dS/dA$; for $n=1$,
$p_A=\ac/(A+\ac)^2$.

% =====================================================================
\section{Angular Reduction and the Interpolation Family}\label{sec:angular}
% =====================================================================
\sI\ The response carried along a direction is the load times the active fraction,
$j_n(x)=x\,f_n(x)$. \sD\ Averaging over directions with $u=\cos\theta$: the projected load is
$xu$, its radial projection carries a second $u$, isotropy weights $u$ uniformly on $[0,1]$
($d\Omega=2\pi\,du$), and the weight $3=1/\langle u^2\rangle$ (\S8) makes a linear response
return itself:
\begin{equation}
\sigma_n(x)=3\int_0^1j_n(xu)\,u\,du,\qquad
\mu_n(x)\equiv\frac{\sigma_n(x)}{x}=3\int_0^1u^2f_n(xu)\,du .
\label{eq:mun}
\end{equation}
For $n=1$, long division,
$u^3/(1+xu)=u^2/x-u/x^2+1/x^3-1/[x^3(1+xu)]$, gives
$\int_0^1u^3/(1+xu)\,du=1/3x-1/2x^2+1/x^3-\ln(1+x)/x^4$ (Appendix~\ref{app:angular}); for
$n=3$ the substitution $w=1+xu/3$ closes the integral. Thus
\begin{align}
\mu_1(x)&=1-\frac{3}{2x}+\frac{3}{x^2}-\frac{3\ln(1+x)}{x^3},\label{eq:mu1}\\
\mu_2(x)&=1-\frac{24}{x^3}\Big[\frac x2+\frac{x/2}{1+x/2}-2\ln\Big(1+\frac x2\Big)\Big],\label{eq:mu2}\\
\mu_3(x)&=1-\frac{81}{x^3}\Big[\ln\!\Big(1+\frac x3\Big)+\frac{2}{1+x/3}
-\frac{1}{2(1+x/3)^2}-\frac32\Big].\label{eq:mu3}
\end{align}
Their series are
\begin{equation}
\mu_1=\tfrac34x-\tfrac35x^2+\tfrac12x^3-\cdots,\quad
\mu_2=\tfrac34x-\tfrac9{20}x^2+\tfrac14x^3-\cdots,\quad
\mu_3=\tfrac34x-\tfrac25x^2+\tfrac5{27}x^3-\cdots .
\end{equation}
(draft 1 stated the deep limit as $\sigma/x\to\tfrac34$; the correct statement is
$\sigma/x^2\to3\int_0^1u^3du=\tfrac34$, i.e.\ $\mu\simeq\tfrac34x$.)

\begin{theorem}[Universality of $\tfrac34$]\label{thm:universal}
\sD\ Assume (a) activation is a Poisson process whose hazard is linear in the load,
(b) the exposure has a finite mean, used as the unit, (c) the response is the load times the
active fraction, and (d) directions are averaged as in~\eqref{eq:mun}. Then, for every
exposure distribution, $\mu(x)=\tfrac34x+O(x^2)$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
$S$ is the Laplace transform of the exposure density in units of its mean, so $S(0)=1$,
$S'(0)=-1$, $f(y)=y+O(y^2)$, and $\mu=3\int_0^1u^2(xu)\,du+O(x^2)=\tfrac34x+O(x^2)$.
\end{proof}
The theorem covers the whole class (a)--(d), not only the Erlang laws; outside it the slope
changes. Hypothesis (a) is itself required by data:
\begin{proposition}[Linear hazard]\label{prop:hazard}
\sD\ If the hazard grows as $x^p$, then $\mu\simeq\frac{3}{3+p}x^p$ and the deep regime gives
$g\propto\gbar^{1/(1+p)}$, so rotation curves are asymptotically flat, with $V^4\propto M$,
only for $p=1$. The baryonic Tully--Fisher relation \cite{Lelli2016btfr} fixes $p=1$. If the
exposure mean diverges, $S'(0)$ does too and $\mu$ is not linear at small $x$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proposition}[Transition and tail]\label{prop:decouple}
\sD\ For real $n$ other than $1,2,3$, with $a=x/n$ and $w=1+a$,
\begin{equation}
1-\mu_n(x)=\frac{3}{a^3}\Big[\frac{w^{3-n}-1}{3-n}-\frac{2(w^{2-n}-1)}{2-n}+\frac{w^{1-n}-1}{1-n}\Big],
\label{eq:mun-real}
\end{equation}
so the tail is $1-\mu_n\simeq\frac{3n^n}{3-n}\,x^{-n}$ for $n<3$, while the transition moves
little: $\mu_n(1)=0.4206$, $0.4515$, $0.4557$, $0.4601$, $0.4623$ for $n=1$, $1.62$, $1.75$, $1.91$,
$2$. In the Torridity form $z/(1+z^n)^{1/n}$ one exponent sets both the transition width and the
tail, $1-\mu\simeq z^{-n}/n$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
Substituting $w=1+au$ in $3\int_0^1u^2(1+au)^{-n}du$ gives~\eqref{eq:mun-real}; its leading term at
large $a$ is $3a^{-n}/(3-n)$. The Torridity tail is the expansion of $(1+z^{-n})^{-1/n}$.
\end{proof}

% =====================================================================
\section{Free Energy and the Field Equation}\label{sec:field_equation}
% =====================================================================
\sD\ With $F_{\rm int}(x)=\int_0^x\sigma_1(s)\,ds
=\tfrac{x^2}2-\tfrac{3x}2+\tfrac{3\ln(1+x)}{x}+3\ln(1+x)-3$ and
$\mathcal F(y)=2F_{\rm int}(\sqrt y)$, one has $\mathcal F'(y)=\mu_1(\sqrt y)$ (verified
symbolically; the same construction holds for every $\mu_n$). \sS\ The Lagrangian
$\mathcal L=-\frac{\ac^2}{8\pi G}\,\mathcal F\big(|\nabla\Phi|^2/\ac^2\big)-\rho\Phi$ gives
\begin{equation}
\nabla\cdot\Big[\mu\Big(\frac{|\nabla\Phi|}{\ac}\Big)\nabla\Phi\Big]=4\pi G\rho ,
\label{eq:aqual}
\end{equation}
the AQUAL form \cite{BekensteinMilgrom1984} with a derived $\mu$.

% =====================================================================
\section{The Deep Regime and the Scale}\label{sec:infrared}
% =====================================================================
\sD\ For spherical symmetry Gauss's theorem turns~\eqref{eq:aqual} into
$\mu(g/\ac)\,g=\gbar\equiv GM(r)/r^2$ exactly. In the deep regime
$\mu\simeq\tfrac34g/\ac$, so $\tfrac34g^2/\ac=\gbar$ and $g=\sqrt{\tfrac43\ac\gbar}$:
\begin{equation}
\az=\frac43\,\ac=\frac{\sqrt3}{\pi^2}\,cH_0\equiv\atrsd\,cH_0,\qquad \atrsd=0.175493 .
\label{eq:a0}
\end{equation}
(draft 1 printed $0.175435$; the correct value is $0.175493$.) \sP\ The scale is
$\az=1.148\times10^{-10}\ \mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$ at the framework's $H_0=67.35$ ($1.2454\times10^{-10}$ at the legacy
calibration $73.04$). By Theorem~\ref{thm:universal} it
does not depend on the exposure law.

% =====================================================================
\section{Confrontation with the SPARC Radial Acceleration Relation}\label{sec:sparc}
% =====================================================================
\sM\ The data are the 2696 points of 147 SPARC galaxies used to define the RAR
\cite{McGaugh2016,Lelli2016}, with $\Upsilon_{\rm disk}=0.5$ and $\Upsilon_{\rm bul}=0.7$.
The predicted acceleration solves $\mu(g/\ac)\,g=\gbar$ point by point; residuals are
$\log_{10}g_{\rm obs}-\log_{10}g_{\rm pred}$. Statistical errors come from resampling
galaxies (200 bootstrap draws); the systematic error of the scale is $\pm20\%$, dominated
by $\Upsilon$ \cite{McGaugh2016}.

\subsection*{14.1\quad No free parameter}
With $\ac$ fixed by $H_0$ and nothing fitted:
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lcccc@{}}
\toprule
law & $H_0=67.12$: mean & rms & $H_0=73.04$: mean & rms \\
\midrule
$\mu_1$ (single stage) & $-0.007$ dex & $0.133$ & $-0.019$ dex & $0.134$ \\
$\mu_2$ (two stages) & $+0.016$ dex & $0.134$ & $+0.003$ dex & $0.133$ \\
$\mu_3$ (three arcs) & $+0.024$ dex & $0.136$ & $+0.011$ dex & $0.134$ \\
Torridity, $n=\pi/3$ (\S17) & $+0.005$ dex & $0.133$ & $-0.008$ dex & $0.133$ \\
draft 1 screened law (\S15) & -- & -- & $+0.173$ dex & $0.225$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The observed scatter about the relation is $0.13$ dex \cite{McGaugh2016}. With no parameter
adjusted, every member reproduces the relation to within $0.025$ dex ($6\%$) in the mean, at
the scatter of the best empirical fit (Figure~\ref{fig:rar}).

\subsection*{14.2\quad Free scale}
Fitting the scale alone:
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lccc@{}}
\toprule
law & fitted $\az$ [$10^{-10}\ \mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$] & rms (dex) & implied $H_0=\az/\atrsd c$ \\
\midrule
empirical $g/\gbar=[1-e^{-\sqrt{\gbar/g_\dagger}}]^{-1}$ & $g_\dagger=1.161\pm0.071$ & $0.133$ & -- \\
$\mu_1$ & $1.105\pm0.071$ & $0.133$ & $64.8\pm4.1\pm13$ \\
$\mu_2$ & $1.249\pm0.075$ & $0.133$ & $73.2\pm4.4\pm15$ \\
$\mu_3$ & $1.301\pm0.079$ & $0.134$ & $76.3\pm4.6\pm15$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
The empirical fit reproduces the published $g_\dagger=1.20\pm0.02\pm0.24$ within its errors,
which validates the pipeline. Read through~\eqref{eq:a0}, galaxies measure $H_0$ at the
$\pm6\%$ statistical and $\pm20\%$ systematic level; the CMB value $67.35$ lies $0.6\sigma$
(statistical) from the $\mu_1$ value, $1.4\sigma$ from the $\mu_2$ value and $2.0\sigma$ from the $\mu_3$ value, and within
$0.6\sigma$ of both once the systematic is included. The draft-1 statement of agreement at
$0.056\%$, and its inversion to $0.04\%$, are withdrawn: the relation is one test, and the
data determine $\az$ to $6\%$, not $0.06\%$.

\subsection*{14.3\quad The rotation-curve likelihood}
\sM\ The RAR points take the mass-to-light ratios, distances and inclinations as known.
The full likelihood fits the velocities of 153 galaxies ($Q<3$, $i\ge30^\circ$; 3168 points)
with per-galaxy nuisance parameters: $\log\Upsilon_{\rm disk}$ and $\log\Upsilon_{\rm bul}$ with
lognormal priors of $0.1$ dex about $0.5$ and $0.7$, and $D$ and $i$ with Gaussian priors from
the SPARC table---490 in all, following \cite{Li2018}. Distances rescale radii and the baryonic
velocities as $\sqrt D$; inclinations rescale the observed velocities and their errors by
$\sin i_0/\sin i$. Each galaxy's $\chi^2$ is minimized over its nuisance parameters and the
global scale is scanned. The best $\chi^2$ is $14370$ for $3167$ degrees of freedom (median
per-galaxy $\chi^2_\nu=2.1$): the quoted velocity errors omit systematics, and errors below are
scaled by $S=\sqrt{\chi^2/\nu}=2.13$. Two forms are compared---the threshold family with real tail
order $n$ and the Torridity form of draft 1 (\S17)---with the empirical function of
\cite{McGaugh2016} as a benchmark:
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}llccc@{}}
\toprule
form & $n$ & $\az$ [$10^{-10}$] & $\Delta\chi^2/S^2$ & $\Delta\chi^2/S^2$ at $\az=1.144$ \\
\midrule
threshold & $1$ & $1.063$ & $4.7$ & $3.5$ \\
threshold & $1.38$ (optimum) & $1.045$ & $0$ & $5.5$ \\
threshold & $1.75$ (window centre) & $1.037$ & $2.2$ & $6.7$ \\
threshold & $2$ & $1.034$ & $5.1$ & $7.2$ \\
threshold & $3$ & $1.029$ & $18.6$ & $8.3$ \\
Torridity & $0.97$ & $1.057$ & $4.4$ & $4.0$ \\
Torridity & $\pi/3$ & $1.081$ & $9.2$ & $2.2$ \\
Torridity & $2\pi/3$ & $1.201$ & $397$ & $2.7$ \\
benchmark & -- & $1.009$ & $-13.6$ & -- \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Scale errors are $\pm0.04$ (statistical, scaled) throughout; $\Delta\chi^2/S^2$ is measured from
the best threshold member.

\emph{Transition and tail.} Galaxies constrain the width of the transition, the planets the
Newtonian tail (\S15). In the threshold family the two are separate
(Proposition~\ref{prop:decouple}): galaxies give $n=1.38\pm0.20$, the planets $n\ge1.62$, and
the window $1.62\le n\le1.91$ satisfies both while fitting better than any Torridity exponent
($\Delta\chi^2/S^2=4.4$). In the Torridity form one exponent sets both: galaxies give
$0.96\pm0.04$, the planets require $n\ge1.53$ (\S17), and no exponent satisfies both.

\emph{The benchmark.} The empirical function leads the best threshold member by
$\Delta\chi^2/S^2=13.6$. Its form was chosen on these same data \cite{McGaugh2016}, so part of the
lead is selection: it is a benchmark, not a competing prediction, and it is derived from nothing.

\emph{Scale.} The fitted scale depends little on the shape: across the window it runs from
$1.041$ to $1.037$. With the nuisance parameters fixed at their nominal values the same
likelihood returns $g_\dagger=1.206$, reproducing the published $1.20$; profiling them lowers every
scale by about $16\%$, while moving the $\Upsilon_{\rm disk}$ prior centre to $0.4$ or $0.6$ shifts
it by only $\pm1.5\%$. Taking half the fixed-versus-profiled shift as the systematic, the window
gives $\az=1.04\pm0.04\,(\mathrm{stat})\pm0.10\,(\mathrm{nuisance})$. The prediction $1.148$ is
$1.0\sigma$ high ($2.7\sigma$ on the statistical error alone), and $2.0\sigma$ high at
$H_0=73.04$; read through~\eqref{eq:a0}, galaxies give $H_0=60.8\pm2.2\pm5.9$.

\emph{Uncertainty.} The agreement is conditional on the nuisance model: distances, inclinations
and mass-to-light ratios carry the uncertainty that the scale inherits, and it is not a
theory-only error.

\subsection*{14.4\quad What the data constrain}
\sDS\ Not the index. $n$ is a coordinate on shapes, and different forms assign different values to
nearly the same shape: the threshold member $n=1.38$ and the Torridity member $n=0.96$ describe
almost the same transition. What the data constrain is a pair of physical properties---a
transition as gradual as the galaxies require and a Newtonian tail as steep as the planets
require---and of the two forms only the threshold family supplies both. Numbers below are quoted
at the window centre, $n=1.75$; across the window they change by less than $3\%$.

% =====================================================================
\section{Newtonian Recovery}\label{sec:screening}
% =====================================================================
\sD\ At high acceleration $1-\mu_n=3\int_0^1u^2S_n(xu)\,du$, and
\begin{equation}
1-\mu_1\simeq\frac{3}{2x},\qquad 1-\mu_2\simeq\frac{12}{x^2},\qquad
1-\mu_3\simeq\frac{81}{x^3}\Big[\ln\frac x3-\frac32\Big].
\end{equation}
The single-stage law leaves a constant anomaly $g-\gbar\to\tfrac32\ac=1.28\times10^{-10}\
\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$. An inward anomaly $A\propto r^k$ turns a perihelion back by
$\frac{k+2}{2}\,2\pi Aa^2\sqrt{1-e^2}/GM_\odot$ per orbit; for this constant anomaly ($k=0$) that is
$-1.7\times10^3$ mas/cy for Mercury and $-2.8\times10^3$ mas/cy for the Earth, against the ephemeris residuals $-2.0\pm3.0$ and
$0.19\pm0.19$ mas/cy \cite{PitjevaPitjev2013}. A tail $x^{-n}$ has $k=2n-2$. Requiring both perihelia within $2\sigma$
of their residuals gives $n\ge1.62$ for the threshold family and $n\ge1.53$ for the Torridity
form (\S17). At the window centre, $n=1.75$, the shifts are $-0.004$ (Mercury) and $-0.028$ mas/cy
(the Earth); at the lower edge of the window the Earth's reaches $-0.20$ mas/cy, the present
sensitivity. (Mercury sits at $x=4.6\times10^8$; draft 1 gave $2\times10^{10}$.)

\textbf{The draft-1 screening is retired.} Writing $1-\mu_{\rm scr}=(1-\mu_1)(1+x)^{-\beta}$ gives
$\mu_{\rm scr}\simeq(\tfrac34+\beta)\,x$ at small $x$: the factor changes the deep slope and
with it $\az$. For $\beta=\pi/2$ the slope is $2.32$, the scale falls to $0.43\,\ac$, and the
fixed-scale fit to SPARC moves by $+0.17$ dex with rms $0.225$ (\S14.1). Every exposure law
keeps $\tfrac34$ exactly (Theorem~\ref{thm:universal}); tail orders in the window also fit the
rotation curves and pass the planets. \sD\ The solar-system test is the quadrupole induced by the Galactic external
field \cite{Milgrom2009efe}, which Cassini constrains \cite{Hees2014}; it probes $\mu$ near
$x\sim2$. With baryons as the only source the law gives $Q_2=3.1\times10^{-26}\ \mathrm{s^{-2}}$
across the window (GRUT \S49.10 \cite{GRUT2026}), twenty times the Cassini/DE440 bound
$(1.6\pm1.8)\times10^{-27}$ \cite{DE440Q2}: a boost that belongs to the baryons' field cannot hold
in the Solar System \cite{Desmond2024}. With the condensate as the source (\S15.1) the Sun carries
no bound condensate, and the quadrupole is the Galactic tide alone,
$8.6\times10^{-31}\ \mathrm{s^{-2}}$.

\subsection*{15.1\quad The complete equation}
\sD\ The Cassini failure shows that the baryon-only equation is incomplete; the missing source
is real matter: the condensate of \S21--22 in the condensate reading, the rotation's medium bound around
the baryons in the rotation reading (\S21.1). The complete equation is
\begin{equation}
\nabla^2\Phi=4\pi G(\rho_b+\rho_\chi),\qquad
\rho_\chi(\mathbf k)=\frac{\rho_{\rm ph}(\mathbf k)}{1+(k/k_J)^4},\qquad
k_J=(16\pi G\bar\rho_\chi)^{1/4}(\mdelta/\hbar)^{1/2},
\end{equation}
with $\rho_{\rm ph}$ the phantom density of the system's own baryons (\S23), and the bound mass
limited by the condensate available. \sD\ The bound density is derived: $1-\mu(x)=3\int_0^1u^2S(xu)\,du$
is the cosine-weighted fraction of condensate elements that survive the threshold, and if a
surviving element carries unit gravitational susceptibility the condensate's polarization is
$\mathbf P=(1-\mu)\,\mathbf g/4\pi G$, whose bound density $-\nabla\cdot\mathbf P$ is exactly
$\rho_{\rm ph}$ (GRUT Theorem~47.6). Real matter obeys the equivalence principle, so a
uniform external field does not enter; quantum pressure forbids condensate structure below
$\lambda_J=2\pi/k_J$, which is $40$--$290$ pc for $\mdelta=10^{-19}$--$2\times10^{-20}$ eV. Galaxies,
larger than $\lambda_J$, keep the results of \S14; the Solar System, far smaller, is Newtonian,
and its only quadrupole is the Galactic tide, $8.6\times10^{-31}\ \mathrm{s^{-2}}$, within Cassini's
bound at $-0.9\sigma$. The field is also bound only where it has been captured: for a Maxwellian
field of dispersion $\sigma$ the captured fraction is
$\Gamma=\operatorname{erf}X-(2X/\sqrt\pi)e^{-X^2}$ with $X=v_{\rm esc}/\sqrt2\sigma$, and the Sun, formed
inside the Galaxy where the field streams at $\sim165$ km\,s$^{-1}$, has
$\Gamma=7\times10^{-9}$ at its MOND radius (GRUT \S47). The law changes with scale because the matter that carries the boost has a
Jeans length and a finite supply: Newtonian below $\lambda_J$, the threshold response across
galaxies, Keplerian beyond $r_t=(M_\chi/M_b)\sqrt{GM_b/\az}$. The one identification left is that
$\mu$ is the gravitational permittivity of the condensate (GRUT \S46--47 \cite{GRUT2026}).

\subsection*{15.2\quad The relaxation state}
\sD\ The complete equation gives the end state of a system's condensate. A bound system starts
from its \emph{infall configuration}---on scales above $\lambda_J$ the condensate is pressureless, so a
collapsing system gathers its cosmic share, $\Omega_c/\Omega_b$ times its baryons, into a collapsed
halo---and relaxes toward the \emph{support level}, the polarization equilibrium, with its isothermal
halo and flat curve. The \emph{resistance level}, Newton, is reached only where no condensate is bound,
as in the Solar System. With $f$ the polarized fraction,
$M_\chi=fM_{\rm pol}+(1-f)M_{\rm inf}$. For a representative rich cluster the support law carries
$0.28$--$0.68$ of the hydrostatic mass between $250$ and $1500$ kpc,
reduced capture ($\Gamma=0.5$) only $0.13$--$0.25$, and the infall configuration
$0.82$--$0.84$: the unrelaxed state of a bound system is its infall halo, not Newton. A
disk has lived about $100$ crossing times since assembly and a cluster about four, so if polarization
takes $N_{\rm rel}$ crossing times, galaxies reach support and clusters do not for
$11\lesssim N_{\rm rel}\lesssim34$. A merger before support is a trap, with probability
$1-e^{-t_{\rm rel}/t_{\rm dis}}$: $0.97$--$1.00$ for clusters, $0.10$--$0.28$ for disks. The scatter about
the support law is proportional to the spread of $1-f$, so relaxed systems are quiet and systems in
transit are not. \sP\ The mass discrepancy against the support law is ordered by dynamical age since
the last major merger; early-assembled fossil groups lie closer to it than clusters of the same
mass; disturbed galaxies scatter more about the radial acceleration relation than isolated disks.
\sD\ A bound system's clock is fixed at formation, $t_{\rm cross}=1/10H(t_f)$, and \sI\ with the
polarization renewed once per arc of the horizon's $C_3$ cycle at that epoch, $t_{\rm rel}=\tau_1(t_f)/3$
and $N_{\rm rel}=40\pi^2/9\sqrt3=25.3$, inside the window. In the matter era the relaxation state then
depends only on $D=t/t_f$, the age of the universe in units of its age at formation:
$f=1-e^{-(D-1)/x_H}$ with $x_H=2\pi^2/3\sqrt3=3.80$, the horizon load of \S24.5. A cluster assembled at
$z_f=0.5$ has $f=0.17$ and carries $0.74$--$0.80$ of its hydrostatic mass; a disk formed at $z_f=6$
has $f=0.97$ (GRUT \S47.6--47.7 \cite{GRUT2026}).

\subsection*{15.3\quad Cluster mergers and the post-passage relaxation clock}\label{sec:mergers-trsd}
\sD\ \emph{Collisionless and collisional components.} The phase sector obeys $\nabla_\mu(\lambda\partial^\mu\theta)=0$ with $X=X_0$ and no baryonic source. With $u_\mu=\partial_\mu\theta/\sqrt{X_0}$, the fixed-norm identity gives $u^\nu\nabla_\nu u_\mu=0$ and the on-shell phase stress is pressureless, $T^{(\chi)}_{\mu\nu}=2\lambda\partial_\mu\theta\partial_\nu\theta$. In a merger this component therefore has no gas-shock drag term in the model; the X-ray emitting plasma instead experiences pressure forces and shocks. The collisionless interpretation is a model correspondence, not an observation of the microscopic identity of the lensing mass.

\sI\ \emph{Conditional relaxation closure.} The framework identifies the $C_3$ rate as $\omega_{C_3}=(3\sqrt3/2\pi)H(z)$ and sets $\omega_{C_3}t_{\rm rel}=2\pi/3$, giving
\begin{equation}\label{eq:merger-trel-trsd-update}
t_{\rm rel}(z)=\frac{4\pi^2}{9\sqrt3\,H(z)}.
\end{equation}
The exponential transfer fraction follows if the post-passage response is first order, $\dot f=(1-f)/t_{\rm rel}$, with $f(0)=0$:
\begin{equation}\label{eq:merger-map-trsd}
\boxed{\begin{gathered}
f(\Delta t)=1-\exp\!\left[-\frac{\Delta t}{t_{\rm rel}(z)}\right]
=1-\exp\!\left[-\frac{9\sqrt3}{4\pi^2}H(z)\Delta t\right],\quad \Delta t\ge0,\\
\kappa_{\rm model}=\kappa_b+(1-f)(\kappa_A+\kappa_B)+f\,\kappa_{\rm pol},\qquad
M^{\rm model}_{\chi,i}=\frac{\Omega_c}{\Omega_b}M^{\rm pre}_{b,i}.
\end{gathered}}
\end{equation}
Here $\kappa_b$ is the baryonic contribution, $\kappa_A$ and $\kappa_B$ are the two pre-passage collisionless-like components, and $\kappa_{\rm pol}$ is the model's later support/polarization template. The mixture form and the pre-merger baryon-share assignment are model closures; neither follows from geodesic flow alone. The transfer law is derived only conditional on these declared closures.

\sH\ \emph{Correct interpretation of the three phases.} Before first core passage, the subclusters are in an infall configuration. The condition $f=0$ is the initial condition at the post-passage clock's reset, $\Delta t=0$; it should not be read as a universal claim that all pre-merger systems have zero relaxation. At core passage, the gas is shock-heated and decelerated while the galaxies and lensing-inferred mass peaks can pass through with smaller drag. At later times $f$ increases according to~\eqref{eq:merger-map-trsd}. For the Bullet Cluster, $t_{\rm rel}\simeq31.4$ Gyr at $z\simeq0.296$; hence $f(0.15\,\mathrm{Gyr})\simeq0.0048$, $f(0.20\,\mathrm{Gyr})\simeq0.0064$, $f(1\,\mathrm{Gyr})\simeq0.031$, and $f(2\,\mathrm{Gyr})\simeq0.062$. The earlier value $0.0048$ corresponds to $0.15$ Gyr, not $0.20$ Gyr.

\sS\ Observationally, the Bullet Cluster and MACS J0025.4$-$1222 show spatial separation between lensing-inferred mass and X-ray gas (\href{https://science.nasa.gov/image-detail/hubble-chandra-bulletcluster-stsci-01evvebdtedwkk5j4k58a3j734/}{NASA Bullet Cluster overview}; \href{https://science.nasa.gov/missions/chandra/a-clash-of-clusters/}{NASA MACS J0025 overview}). El Gordo is an ongoing high-redshift merger commonly reconstructed shortly after first core passage, with alternative outgoing/returning solutions (\href{https://doi.org/10.1093/mnras/stv1713}{MNRAS dynamical reconstruction}); Pandora's Cluster/Abell 2744 is a multi-subcluster collision over an extended interval, not a simple relaxed endpoint (\href{https://science.nasa.gov/asset/hubble/pandoras-cluster-abell-2744/}{NASA overview}). These are distinct systems at different epochs, not a literal time-lapse of one object; merger-phase and projection degeneracies remain (\href{https://doi.org/10.3390/universe12080249}{2026 review}).

The physical analogy is limited to local dynamics: collisional gas and a collisionless gravitating component respond differently to cluster passage, while a disturbed remnant evolves afterward. It does not establish that the Big Bang was a cluster-like collision, that the conformal scale factor changes sign, or that information crosses an aeon boundary. The proposed cross-scale equality of $t_{\rm rel}H$ is therefore retained as a framework hypothesis/closure, not promoted to an observationally established law. Testing is deferred in this revision, as requested.

\sD\ \emph{Computed, on synthetic maps.} A collision separates an excess on the galaxies from a law computed on the baryons present ($T=2\ln B=97.7$,
$p_{\rm MC}<5\times10^{-5}$) and a swapped assignment ($T=99.1$), and does not separate TRSD from particle dark matter ($T=-0.08$, power $0.053$). The mesh of the computation is not
converged (the peak moves $9.6\%$ from 64 to 96 cells), so offsets below about $30$ kpc are unresolved. \sP\ No galaxy-free peak on the gas above
$f(\Delta t)\le0.06$ of the excess; a robust one holding $\gtrsim10\%$ would falsify this (Abell~520 is the test, not a prediction; the dark core of \cite{Mahdavi2007,Jee2012A520}
is absent at comparable significance in \cite{Clowe2012A520,Peel2017}). Register rows 41--42.

% =====================================================================

\section{Mass as Conical Defect and the Closure Mass}\label{sec:mass_defect}
% =====================================================================

\subsection*{16.1\quad Curvature of a cone}
\sS\ For a slice $ds^2=dr^2+r^2d\theta^2$, $\theta\in[0,2\pi-\delta)$, the Gauss--Bonnet
theorem gives $\int K\,dA=\delta$, and with $R=2K$, $\int R\sqrt g\,d^2x=2\delta$. (draft 1 used
$2\delta$ in one place and $\delta$ in another.)

\subsection*{16.2\quad The deficit--mass map}
\sS\ A straight string of mass per length $\mu$ has $\delta=8\pi G\mu/c^2$ \cite{Vilenkin1981};
reducing $3{+}1$ gravity along a length $L$ gives the $2{+}1$ coupling $G_3=G/L$, and a point
mass in $2{+}1$ gravity carries $\delta=8\pi G_3M/c^2$ \cite{DJtH1984}. \sI\ (I4) With
$L=R_H$,
\begin{equation}
\delta(M)=\frac{8\pi GM}{c^2R_H},\qquad \fd^2=\frac{Mc^2}{R_H}=McH_0 .
\label{eq:defectmass}
\end{equation}
The mass carrying $\dtot=\pi$ is $M_{\rm total}=c^3/8GH_0$; the mass carrying $4\pi$ is
$M_{\rm Hubble}=c^3/2GH_0=4M_{\rm total}$.

\subsection*{16.3\quad Closure}
\begin{theorem}[Closure mass]\label{thm:closure}
\sD\ In the reduced geometry a closed spatial section carrying point masses $M_i$
requires $\sum_i\delta(M_i)=4\pi$, that is $\sum_iM_i=M_{\rm Hubble}$. Four equal masses
$M_{\rm total}$, each carrying $\dtot=\pi$, realize it at the vertices of a regular tetrahedron.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
A closed surface that is flat except at cone points has total curvature $2\pi\chi=4\pi$
(Gauss--Bonnet, or Descartes' theorem), and each point contributes its deficit. A tetrahedral
vertex joins three equilateral faces, total angle $\pi$, deficit $\pi$ (\S3.3).
\end{proof}
The draft-1 remark that $\dtot=4\pi$ is ``geometrically impossible'' applies to a single line
defect, whose solid angle $4\pi(1-\delta/2\pi)$ vanishes at $2\pi$; as the sum over four
apexes on a closed surface, $4\pi$ is exactly the closure. The Schwarzschild radius of
$M_{\rm Hubble}$ is $R_H$ (\S24.3), so closure, the horizon condition and flatness coincide.

% =====================================================================
\section{The Torridity Family}\label{sec:torridity}
% =====================================================================
\sI\ The Torridity function is the $n$-family
\begin{equation}
\mu_{\rm T}(z)=\frac{z}{(1+z^{n})^{1/n}},\qquad z=g/\az,\qquad n=\frac{\dtot}{3}=\frac\pi3 ,
\end{equation}
with $\mu_{\rm T}\simeq z=\tfrac34x$ in the deep regime, the same scale as~\eqref{eq:a0}.
\sM\ In the rotation-curve likelihood (\S14.3) the Torridity exponent is $0.96\pm0.04$ (on
the RAR points with fixed $\Upsilon$, $0.98\pm0.03$; draft 1's per-galaxy fit, $1.050\pm0.05$), and
$\pi/3$ lies at $\Delta\chi^2/S^2=4.9$ ($2.2\sigma$). The profile has no secondary minimum:
draft 1's exponent quantization---$n=k\pi/3$, with $2\pi/3$ for disk galaxies and $\pi$ for
clusters---is refuted, $2\pi/3$ lying at $\Delta\chi^2/S^2=397$ (Figure~\ref{fig:tails}). At
high acceleration $1-\mu_{\rm T}\simeq z^{-n}/n$, and the perihelia require $n\ge1.53$; $\pi/3$
turns them back by $6\times10^2$ (Mercury) and $1.1\times10^3$ mas/cy (the Earth), two orders of
magnitude beyond the residuals. \sD\ The Torridity form therefore cannot be a complete law for
any exponent: the galaxies and the planets ask for different values of its one parameter, which
is why draft 1 needed a screening factor. The threshold family does not
(Proposition~\ref{prop:decouple}).

\begin{proposition}[Running exponent]\label{prop:running}
\sD\ Define the local exponent $m(z)$ of an interpolation function, $z=g/\az$, as the exponent
of the one-parameter form through the same point: $z/(1+z^m)^{1/m}=\mu(z)$. For the threshold
family with tail order $n<3$, $m\to1$ as $z\to0$ and $m\to n$ as $z\to\infty$.
\end{proposition}
\begin{proof}
At small $z$, $\mu_n=z-cz^2+O(z^3)$ with $c=8(n+1)/15n>0$, while the one-parameter form is
$z-z^{1+m}/m+\dots$; matching gives $m-1=\ln(cm)/\ln z\to0$. At large $z$,
$1-\mu_n\simeq\frac{3n^n}{3-n}(\tfrac43z)^{-n}$ (Proposition~\ref{prop:decouple}), while the
one-parameter form gives $z^{-m}/m$; matching gives $m\to n$.
\end{proof}
There is therefore no universal exponent. It runs with acceleration---from $1$ far out to the
tail order close in---and each system reads it at its own acceleration:
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lcccc@{}}
\toprule
& $\log_{10}g$ & $m$ ($n=1.62$) & $m$ ($n=1.75$) & $m$ ($n=1.91$) \\
\midrule
far out & $-14$ & $1.014$ & $1.017$ & $1.020$ \\
galaxy outskirts & $-11.5$ & $1.032$ & $1.038$ & $1.045$ \\
galaxy transition & $-10.5$ & $1.059$ & $1.070$ & $1.082$ \\
inner galaxies & $-9.5$ & $1.135$ & $1.161$ & $1.190$ \\
Saturn & $-4.19$ & $1.507$ & $1.612$ & $1.740$ \\
the Earth & $-2.23$ & $1.534$ & $1.646$ & $1.782$ \\
Mercury & $-1.40$ & $1.542$ & $1.656$ & $1.794$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
Galaxies read the exponent between $1.03$ and $1.28$; the planets between $1.51$ and $1.79$. A
one-parameter form must use one value at every acceleration, and fails; the running exponent
meets both (Figure~\ref{fig:running}). The requirement the planets place on a one-parameter
form, $m\ge1.534$ at the Earth's acceleration, is met exactly at the lower edge of the window,
as it must be: the two planetary thresholds are one condition. The tail order that remains is
not the important part---the galactic scale does not depend on it.

draft 1's exponent $\pi/3=1.047$ is the running exponent at the accelerations of galaxy outskirts
($m=1.04$--$1.06$ at $g=10^{-11}\ \mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$ across the window): draft 1 measured it where it
holds. The regime dependence draft 1 anticipated with $n=k\pi/3$ is realized by the running exponent;
the value $2\pi/3$ anticipated for disks is not what disks read ($m=1.03$--$1.28$ across disk
accelerations).

\emph{The dynamic exponent.} \sD\ The tail order in the table above is not a parameter. Each
symmetry-preserving level of the cascade (\S18) is a closed $C_3$ structure with $N_a=2j+1$ arcs
enclosing $N_s=2j$ regions; its arcs are exponential exposure stages shared equally by its regions,
and since the root of $(1-it)^{-N_a}$ into $N_s$ equal factors is unique, each region is exposed
with order $n_j=N_a/N_s=\tfrac32,\tfrac54,\tfrac76,\dots$ \sI\ An element is exposed at level $j$ in
proportion to the structure still open there, the closure weight $\kappa_{2j-1}=1/2j(2j+1)$, which
over the symmetric ladder sums to $1-\ln2$. The interpolation function is then
\begin{equation}
\mu(x)=\sum_{j\ge1}w_j\,\mu_{n_j}(x),\qquad w_j=\frac{1}{(1-\ln2)\,2j(2j+1)}=0.543,\ 0.163,\ 0.078,\dots
\end{equation}
with every level exposed over the same horizon period, so the deep regime keeps $\mu=\tfrac34x$
exactly. Written in the Torridity form $z/(1+z^{n(z)})^{1/n(z)}$ its exponent runs with the
acceleration:
\begin{center}
\small
\begin{tabular}{@{}lcccccccc@{}}
\toprule
$\log_{10}g$ & $-13$ & $-12$ & $-11$ & $-10.5$ & $-10$ & $-9.5$ & $-9$ & $-8$ \\
\midrule
$n(z)$ & $1.008$ & $1.011$ & $1.018$ & $1.026$ & $1.040$ & $1.065$ & $1.096$ & $1.144$ \\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}
It tends to one in the deep regime, passes $\pi/3$ at $g=1.26\,\az$---draft 1's exponent is its value at
the transition---and never exceeds about $1.16$. The exposure order among the surviving elements,
which carry the boost, is $\pi^2/24(1-\ln2)=1.340$ at low load and falls toward one. With the capture
fraction of \S15.1 the gravity law is
\begin{equation}
g\,\Big[1-\Gamma\big(1-\mu(g/a_c)\big)\Big]=g_{\rm bar},
\end{equation}
which gives $g^2=\az g_{\rm bar}$ in the deep regime at full capture, Newton at every acceleration
without capture, and a constant enhancement $g_{\rm bar}/(1-\Gamma)$ in between (GRUT \S22.9
\cite{GRUT2026}).

\begin{figure}[t]
\centering
\begin{tikzpicture}
\begin{axis}[width=0.8\textwidth, height=5.8cm, xmin=-14, xmax=0, ymin=0.9, ymax=2.0,
  xlabel={$\log_{10} g\ [\mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}]$}, ylabel={running exponent $m$},
  legend pos=south east, legend style={font=\scriptsize, draw=none, fill=white, fill opacity=0.85, text opacity=1},
