% =====================================================================
\section{Cascade Structure and the Closure Weights}\label{sec:cascade}
% =====================================================================
\sD\ Two perpendicular arcs enclose one region, so a chain of $N_a$ arcs encloses
$N_s=N_a-1$. The symmetry-preserving ladder adds two arcs per level, $N_a=2j+1$; the full
ladder is $(N_a,N_s)=(k+2,k+1)$. On the symmetric ladder the exponent sequence
\begin{equation}
n_j=\frac{2j+1}{2j}\,2^{-2(j-1)}=\frac{N_a}{N_s}\,2^{-(N_a-3)}
\end{equation}
takes the values $1.5,\ 0.3125,\ 0.0729,\ 0.0176,\dots$ The closure weights
$\kappa_k=1/N_aN_s=1/(k+1)(k+2)$ telescope:
\begin{equation}
\sum_{k=1}^K\kappa_k=\frac12-\frac1{K+2}\to\frac12,\qquad \sum_{k\ge2}\kappa_k=\frac13 .
\end{equation}
\sI\ Identifying the multipole with the sphere count, $\ell=N_s=k+1$, gives
$\kappa_k=1/\ell(\ell+1)$, the form of the Sachs--Wolfe plateau: a correspondence of form, not
a derivation of amplitudes. Every node of a $C_3$ is itself a $C_3$, so depth $d$ holds
$(3^{d+1}-1)/2$ units. \sH\ The unit excess $N_a-N_s=1$ at every level is read as the
directional degree of freedom of space. \sD\ In the dynamic exponent of \S17 the closure fraction
$N_a/N_s$ is the exposure order of a level and the factor $2^{-2(j-1)}$ its scale; a single system is
exposed at a common scale, so the orders enter with the closure weights and the scale factor does
not.

% =====================================================================
\section{Scale Bridge and the Information--Energy Relation}\label{sec:scale_bridge}
% =====================================================================
\sD\ Equation~\eqref{eq:a0} bridges the galactic and cosmic scales by one pure number,
$\az=\atrsd\,cH_0$. Every derived quantity scales with $H_0$, so the geometry fixes ratios,
not $H_0$ itself (Theorem~\ref{thm:counting}).

With $N=2\pi R_H^2/\lP^2$ cells on the effective horizon and the information of an angular
content $\delta$ taken as $I=(\delta/2\pi)N$ \sI, the deficit map~\eqref{eq:defectmass} gives
\begin{equation}
E=Mc^2=\frac{\delta c^4R_H}{8\pi G}=\frac{c^4R_H}{8\pi G}\cdot\frac{2\pi I}{N}
=\frac{\hbar c}{8\pi R_H}\,I .
\label{eq:info_energy}
\end{equation}
$I$ counts nats: for $\dtot=\pi$, $I=N/2=\pi R_H^2/\lP^2$, the Bekenstein--Hawking entropy of
the horizon sphere. The rate $\hbar c/8\pi R_H=\hbar H_0/8\pi$ per nat is one quarter of the
Gibbons--Hawking energy $k_BT_{\rm GH}=\hbar H_0/2\pi$. The energy of the total defect over the
cone volume $V_{\rm cone}=\pi R_H^3/3$ is
\begin{equation}
\frac{E(I=N/2)}{V_{\rm cone}}=\frac{c^4R_H/8G}{\pi R_H^3/3}=\frac{3H_0^2c^2}{8\pi G}=\rho_cc^2 ,
\end{equation}
the full critical energy density (\S24.1). (The draft-1 appendix divided by a hemisphere volume and
obtained half of this; the cone volume is the one used throughout.)

% =====================================================================
\section{Regeneration}\label{sec:regen}
% =====================================================================
\sH\ A vacuum fluctuation of the terminal state sources a potential whose gradient at the cycle
scale is $\ac$, i.e.\ $x=1$, where the active fraction is $f_1(1)=\tfrac12$ (or
$f_3(1)=37/64$ for the three-arc law). The half-activated state seeds displacement from the
vacuum, which the flow of \S7 returns to the vacuum while structure forms. The cycle
terminal state $\to$ fluctuation $\to$ $g\sim\ac$ $\to$ activation $\to$ structure $\to$
terminal state is a model of self-closure, not yet a dynamical calculation.

% =====================================================================
\section{Dark Sector}\label{sec:dark}
\emph{Reading note.} This section and the next describe the radial excitation of the scalar field as a
condensate of dark-matter particles, the condensate reading. In the rotation reading of \S21.1 the cosmic
cold share is the phase held at its rate, pressureless dust with no particle, and the particle mass scale,
solitonic cores and particle bounds do not apply to it; the radial mode and its potential stand as derived. \S21.7 belongs to the rotation
reading.

% =====================================================================

\subsection*{21.1\quad The rotation of the universe and the local baryonic coupling}
\sH\ The universe rotates as a whole, with a fixed frequency: slow, everywhere and coherent. The rotation
lives in the phase $\theta$ of the conjugate pair, which winds around the $C_3$ defects; the winding $n$ is
an integer and the global frequency $\omega_0=c\sqrt{\lambda_1}=0.827H_0$ is fixed by the $C_3$
eigenvalue. \sD\ The rotation is internal: the phase turns in time and space does not rotate, its flow
$\partial_\mu\theta$ being a gradient with zero vorticity, so the CMB bound $(\sigma_V/H)_0<4.7\times10^{-11}$
\cite{Saadeh2016} is met exactly. On the closed horizon the windings of a single-valued complex field sum to
zero and the $C_3$ symmetry sets each to zero, so a global frame dragging is excluded; the extra gravity is
carried by the rotation's medium bound around the baryons (GRUT \S23). Alone it gives $v_0=\omega_0R_H\sin\chi$, not a flat curve. The baryons modulate the
frequency, $\omega=\omega_0+\delta\omega_b$, without changing $n$ or $\omega_0$: they change the local
rate at which the phase is sampled. The modulated phase carries a momentum density; held at its rate by the constraint, the rotation's
medium is pressureless and binds around the baryons, and with the coupling $\Gamma_b(g_b/a_c)$,
\begin{equation}
v(r)=[1-\Gamma_b]\,\omega_0R_H\sin(r/R_H)+\Gamma_b\,v_{\rm flat},
\end{equation}
flat where the acceleration is weak and the tiny global mode, ordinary Newtonian behaviour, where it is
strong. There is no dark particle and no new force: the observed extra gravity is the rotation's medium bound
around the baryons, tuned, not generated, by them (GRUT \S23 \cite{GRUT2026}). \sP\ With the windings zero,
the local inertial frame shows no rotation against the quasar frame \cite{Szapudi2021};
a slowly rotating universe is independently viable \cite{Szigeti2025,Godel1949}. The coupling has two parts. \sD\ The metric part: on the curved background the phase equation forces
a uniform coordinate frequency, and the baryons modulate the locally measured one,
$\Gamma_b^{(g)}=-\Phi/c^2$, the redshift of the core. The geometric part, by matching to the field equation:
$\Gamma_b\to(\az/g_b)^{1/2}$ in the deep regime, so $v_{\rm flat}^4=\az GM_b$. \sH\ Its source is the cone each
baryonic system carries: deficit $\pi$ at every scale, so an equilateral axial section, with slant
$L_\pi=8GM/c^2$ from the defect relation of Section~\ref{sec:mass_defect} (it returns $R_H$ for the horizon's
cone); the coupling length is $\ell=\sqrt{GM_b/\az}=\sqrt{L_\pi c^2/8\az}$ (GRUT \S49.5--49.7). \sD\ Beyond the crossover the
field of an isolated mass is two-dimensional Poisson gravity, $g=2GM/(L_{\rm red}r)$; with the crossover at $\az$,
$L_{\rm red}=2\ell$ and $L_{\rm red}^2=L_\pi R_{\az}$, where $R_{\az}=c^2/2\az$ is the radius of the horizon whose surface
gravity is $\az$, so $v_{\rm flat}^4=\az GM_b$ follows (GRUT \S49.7). \sP\ The zero point lies $0.7\sigma$ from
gas-rich galaxies, $\az=1.24\pm0.14$ \cite{McGaugh2011}; the law acts where the rotation's modulation is bound, so wide
binaries, which bind none, are Newtonian \cite{Banik2024}; applied to the total field it would give the Solar System
a quadrupole $16\sigma$ above Cassini's. The anomaly current of the
rotating phase grows only on scales of $2{,}181$ Hubble radii, so magnetic fields are set by ordinary
dynamos at equipartition, $\sqrt{\mu_0\rho}\,v_t\simeq5\ \mu$G (GRUT \S49.9). \sD\ The cosmic share is the rotation held at
its rate by a constraint, $\int\sqrt{-g}\,\lambda(X-X_0)$: pressureless dust with zero sound speed, no particle and
no vacuum energy \cite{ChamseddineMukhanov2013,Scherrer2004} (GRUT \S23.1).

% =====================================================================
\subsection*{21.2\quad Vacuum}
\sD\ The potential $V=\tfrac12(\Xii-2)^2+V_0$ has its minimum on the ring $\Xii=\phi^2=2$, with
$d^2V/d\Xii^2=1$ and, as a function of $\phi$, $V''(\sqrt2)=6\phi^2-4=8$. The vacuum stress is
$-\epsV V_0\,g_{\mu\nu}$, an energy density $\epsV V_0$ with $w=-1$.

\sD\ The vacuum ring has a geometric meaning. $|\Phi|=\sqrt2$ is the director circle of the unit circle that carries the
three generators' phases $e^{2\pi ik/3}$: from every point of the vacuum the generators' circle subtends a right angle, the apex angle of the
light cone $h=r$. Taking each generation's amplitude as the rise of its generator's invariant when the pair is added,
$A_k=|e^{2\pi ik/3}+\Phi|^2-1=\Xii+2\sqrt\Xii\cos(\theta-2\pi k/3)$, the triple has $\sum_kA_k^2/(\sum_kA_k)^2=(\Xii+2)/3\Xii$, which is
Koide's $\tfrac23$ exactly at the vacuum, and the charged leptons read $\Xii=2.000013\pm0.000031$: the lepton cone of the companion theory is
this vacuum (GRUT \S42.8 \cite{GRUT2026}).

\subsection*{21.3\quad The vacuum constant must vanish}
\begin{theorem}\label{thm:V0}
\sD\ With the potential scale fixed by the dark-matter mass~\eqref{eq:epsV}, a vacuum
constant $V_0$ of order one overshoots the observed vacuum energy by more than $10^{21}$; the
dark energy cannot be carried by $V_0$.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
At $\dtot=\pi$, $\fd^2=1.513\times10^{43}$ N. For the draft-1 calibration $L_*=0.180$ pc,
$\epsV=\fd^2/L_*^2=4.9\times10^{11}\ \mathrm{J\,m^{-3}}$, so $V_0=2$ gives
$9.8\times10^{11}\ \mathrm{J\,m^{-3}}$ against $\rho_\Lambda c^2=5.2\times10^{-10}\
\mathrm{J\,m^{-3}}$, a factor $1.9\times10^{21}$. The Lyman-$\alpha$ bound (\S21.6) shortens
$L_*$ and raises $\epsV$ above $1.9\times10^{16}\ \mathrm{J\,m^{-3}}$, so $V_0<3\times10^{-26}$.
\end{proof}
TRSD therefore takes $V_0=0$, the choice of \S29, and the vacuum energy is carried by the
horizon defect (\S24.1).

\subsection*{21.4\quad Radial mode}
\sD\ By~\eqref{eq:couplings} and Theorem~\ref{thm:norm} the radial fluctuation has
\begin{equation}
\mdelta=\frac{\sqrt8\,\hbar}{c\,L_*},
\label{eq:mdelta}
\end{equation}
and the phase is massless.

\subsection*{21.5\quad Self-interaction}
\sD\ The nonrelativistic coupling of a real scalar with quartic $\lambda$ is
$g_\chi=\lambda\hbar^3/8\mdelta^2c$ \cite{HOTW2017}. With the physical
coupling~\eqref{eq:lambda},
\begin{equation}
g_\chi=\frac{3\pi}{2\delta}\,\frac{\hbar^3}{\mP^2c}\ \stackrel{\delta=\pi}{=}\ \frac{3\hbar^3}{2\mP^2c}
=1.24\times10^{-95}\ \mathrm{J\,m^3},
\label{eq:gchi}
\end{equation}
independent of $\mdelta$. The draft-1 value $3\hbar^3/2\mdelta^2c$ used the field-unit coefficient
$12$ in place of the physical $12\,\mdelta^2/M_{\rm P}^2$ and was larger by
$(\mP/\mdelta)^2\sim10^{100}$. In a halo of density $10^{-21}\ \mathrm{kg\,m^{-3}}$ the
self-interaction energy $g_\chi n$ is $2\times10^{-9}$ of the gradient energy at 1 kpc: the
condensate is effectively free, as in fuzzy dark matter.

\subsection*{21.6\quad Scale duality and the mass bound}
\sI\ (I6) The dark-sector length is the arc removed by the deficit of a mass $M$,
$L_*=\delta R_H=8\pi GM/c^2$. With~\eqref{eq:mdelta},
\begin{equation}
M\,\mdelta=\frac{\sqrt8}{8\pi}\,\mP^2 .
\label{eq:duality}
\end{equation}
\sC\ $M=1.5\times10^{11}M_\odot$ gives $\mdelta c^2=1.00\times10^{-22}$ eV and $L_*=0.180$ pc
(the draft-1 calibration). \sS\ The Lyman-$\alpha$ forest requires $\mdelta>2\times10^{-20}$ eV
\cite{RogersPeiris2021}, so $M<7.5\times10^8M_\odot$ and $L_*<9.0\times10^{-4}$ pc: the
$10^{-22}$ eV benchmark is excluded and the calibration moves to dwarf-galaxy masses.

\sD\ The bound applies to the radial mode. It assumes \cite{RogersPeiris2021} a scalar that is
all the dark matter and evolves linearly as a free field of mass $m$. Here (i) the condensate is
the whole of $\Omega_c$ in the budget (\S24.5); (ii) about the ring the potential is
$4\eta^2+2\sqrt2\,\eta^3+\tfrac12\eta^4$ in field units ($\eta$ the displacement over $\fd$), whose
anharmonic terms shift the oscillation frequency by $\Delta\omega/\omega=-\tfrac38\eta^2$, below
$4\times10^{-5}$ for the initial displacements $\eta\sim10^{-3}$--$10^{-2}$ that give the relic
density, and falling as $a^{-3}$ thereafter---far from the large-misalignment regime that changes
the transfer function \cite{Leong2019}; (iii) the self-coupling is gravitational in strength
(\S21.5); and (iv) a radial oscillation at fixed phase does not populate the massless Goldstone
mode. The linear transfer function is therefore that of the canonical free scalar. If the
condensate were only a fraction of the dark matter the bound would weaken
\cite{Kobayashi2017,MixedFDM2026}. The
relic density follows from misalignment \cite{HOTW2017}; the required initial displacement is
$10^{-3}$--$10^{-2}$ of $\fd$ across the allowed masses \cite{GRUT2026}.

\subsection*{21.7\quad The dark sector as one geometry}
 This subsection formalizes a proposal made for this release. Dark energy is spacetime, the geometry itself; what is called
dark matter is the same spacetime in its excited state, bound to baryonic structure; and what lensing infers as dark matter is the
dilation---the excess of the inferred mass over the baryonic mass, smallest at the centre, growing toward the edge, and varying from system
to system. There is spacetime and there are baryons: dark matter is not a separate entry. The vacuum is the capacity of the final horizon
(\S24.11), the cosmic share is the rotation held at its rate (\S21.1), and the gravity law is the threshold response (\S11, \S17); each
statement follows from them.

\sD\ \emph{Spacetime, unexcited and excited.} The spin's quantum is $\hbar\omega$ with $\omega=H_\infty$, and the charge of the final horizon
is $L_\infty=\hbar N_\infty/4\pi$ (eq.~\eqref{eq:spin-charge}): $N_\infty/4\pi$ quanta, whose energy is the Hubble mass of the final horizon,
$c^5/2GH_\infty$. Spread over its volume they are the vacuum,
\begin{equation}
n_*\,\hbar H_\infty=\rho_\Lambda c^2,\qquad n_*=\frac{N_\infty/4\pi}{\tfrac{4\pi}{3}R_\infty^3}=\frac{3}{8\pi\lP^2R_\infty},
\label{eq:trsd-held}
\end{equation}
the capacity of \S24.11 counted in quanta, three per area step $8\pi\lP^2$ per final radius. Dark energy is spacetime \emph{unexcited}: the
spin's quanta held by the horizon, $w=-1$, constant by the Bianchi identity. The rotation carries quanta of the same kind, each one quantum of
the spin ($j=1$, eq.~\eqref{eq:rot-quantum}), and that is spacetime \emph{excited}: dust, $n\propto a^{-3}$ by the rotation's Noether current,
$w=0$, cold on every scale, clustering and binding to baryonic structure. One stress tensor holds both,
\begin{equation}
\boxed{T^{\rm dark}_{\mu\nu}=\hbar H_\infty\big(n\,u_\mu u_\nu-n_*\,g_{\mu\nu}\big)=2X_0\big(\lambda\,u_\mu u_\nu-\lambda_*\,g_{\mu\nu}\big),
\qquad 2\lambda_*=\frac{\fd^2(\pi)}{\dgen}=\frac{3c^4}{8\pi G},}
\label{eq:trsd-dark-one}
\end{equation}
with $u_\mu=\partial_\mu\theta/\sqrt{X_0}$, $X_0=\Lambda/3=R_\infty^{-2}$, and $\lambda=n\hbar c^2/2H_\infty$ the multiplier of the constraint of
\S21.1. In the normalization of the defect map the vacuum is the generator-defect tension, $\dgen^{-1}\fd^2(\pi)=2\lambda_*$, over the square of
the final radius: draft 1's $\dgen^{-1}\fd^2(\pi)/R_H^2$, which \S24.1 showed to be the critical total today, is the vacuum exactly at the
final horizon, where the total and the vacuum coincide. The rotation's tension today is $\lambda_0=2.80\times10^{42}$~N against
$\lambda_*=7.22\times10^{42}$~N, in the ratio $1/2.580$ of the resonance budget. Quanta, energy and information---$2\pi$ nats per quantum
(\S24.15)---are proportional in both states, so the split is the same in the three currencies of \S24.4. The inventory is spacetime, $0.684$
of the critical density unexcited and $0.265$ excited, $0.949$ together, and the baryons, $0.050$, with the neutrinos and the radiation.

\sD\ \emph{The equation of state of the whole.} From~\eqref{eq:trsd-dark-one},
\begin{equation}
\boxed{w_{\rm dark}=-1+\Theta,\qquad \Theta=\frac{n}{n+n_*}=\frac{\rho_\chi}{\rho_\chi+\rho_\Lambda},}
\label{eq:trsd-wdark}
\end{equation}
the excited fraction. This is the proposal's $w=-1+\Theta$, with $\Theta$ derived rather than posited. Over cosmic time
$n/n_*=(\Omega_c/\Omega_\Lambda)\,a^{-3}$, so $\Theta(a)=1/[1+(a/a_{\rm eq})^3]$ with $a_{\rm eq}=(\Omega_c/\Omega_\Lambda)^{1/3}$: a step in $\ln a$ from $1$
to $0$, centred on dark-sector equality. At the resonance budget (\S24.16) equality falls at $z=0.372$, the dark sector alone stops decelerating
($w_{\rm dark}=-\tfrac13$) at $z=0.728$, and today $\Theta_0=0.279$, $w_{\rm dark,0}=-0.721$. Locally $\Theta=n(\mathbf x)/[n(\mathbf x)+n_*]$:
$\Theta\simeq1$ in haloes, $0.07$ in voids at $\delta=-0.8$, and $\tfrac12$ at the vacuum's own density, $\rho_\chi=\rho_\Lambda$. At the cosmic
ratio that is a baryon density $(\Omega_b/\Omega_c)\,\rho_\Lambda$, $0.65$ protons per cubic metre, $2.58$ times the mean: the proposal's
threshold in $\rho_b$, located. A region is bound inside its zero-acceleration radius, where the mean density is $2\rho_\Lambda$, at matter
overdensity $2\Omega_\Lambda/\Omega_m=4.33$; there $\Theta\ge0.63$.

\sD\ \emph{Excited once, gathered since.} The excitation is not made where baryons are dense. The two states are separately conserved: $n_*$
by the capacity and the Bianchi identity, $n$ by the Noether current, and nothing in the action exchanges energy with the vacuum ($V_0=0$,
\S21.3; the constraint adds none, \S24.15). Nor could the vacuum in a halo's own volume supply it: inside $r_{200}$ the mean density is
$292\,\rho_\Lambda$, so the vacuum there is $0.34\%$ of the halo, and the dark density near the Sun, $0.4\ \mathrm{GeV\,cm^{-3}}$, is
$1.2\times10^5\rho_\Lambda$. The excitation was set once, at the beginning, as the integration constant of \S24.16, and gravity has gathered it
onto baryonic structure since: the infall configuration of \S15.2, $\Omega_c/\Omega_b$ times the baryons. With each state conserved the
expansion is the resonance budget's; no distance, growth rate or parameter moves. \sS\ Gravity measures only the total dark stress-energy
\cite{Kunz2009}, and one multiplier field for both components was proposed in \cite{LimSawickiVikman2010}; here the multiplier carries no
potential, and the vacuum is the horizon's, tied to the dust by the one constant $X_0$. \sM\ Fits that allow an exchange find at most a mild
one: with DESI DR2 the simplest coupling is preferred at slightly more than $1\sigma$ \cite{PanIDE2025}, and a non-parametric reconstruction
finds a coupling that reverses sign with time, at about $2\sigma$ at either end \cite{LiZhang2025}. The prediction of none stands or falls with
$w=-1$ (register row 9).

\sD\ \emph{The dilation.} Lensing and dynamics infer mass with the flat relation between field and source, Poisson's equation: a field
$g$ at radius $r$ is read as the mass $gr^2/G$. The dilation is the ratio of the inferred mass to the baryonic mass,
$\mathcal S\equiv M_{\rm inf}(<r)/M_b(<r)=g/\gbar$, and the excess $(\mathcal S-1)M_b$ is what is called dark matter. The geometry does not respond
flatly: in a relaxed system the response is the field equation~\eqref{eq:aqual}, $\mu g=\gbar$ exactly in spherical symmetry (\S13), and in
the Torridity form whose exponent runs with the acceleration (\S17) the response is a cosine,
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\mathcal S=\frac1\mu=\frac{1}{\cos_n\theta},\qquad \tan\theta=\frac{\az}{g},\qquad \cos_n\theta\equiv\big(1+\tan^n\theta\big)^{-1/n},}
\label{eq:trsd-dilation}
\end{equation}
exact at every acceleration, with $n=n(z)$ the dynamic exponent of \S17. Take the local field $g$ and the horizon's acceleration $\az$ as the
two components of one field, tilted by $\theta$ from the local direction. Projecting the measured field through the tilt gives the baryons'
field, $\gbar=g\cos_n\theta$; the flat inference takes no tilt and reads the whole measured field as the baryons', so the mass it infers is
dilated by the secant. Equivalently $g^2=\gbar\,(g^n+\az^n)^{1/n}$: the measured field is the geometric mean of the baryons' field and the
whole field---two projections through one angle, as the angular reduction of \S11 projects the load and then its radial response. In the Euclidean plane, $n=2$, the cosine is the ordinary one, $\mathcal S=\sqrt{1+\az^2/g^2}=1/\cos\theta$ exactly: the proposal's
form, whose values $2$, $3.86$ and $11.5$ at $\theta=60^\circ$, $75^\circ$ and $85^\circ$ are the dilation where the field has fallen to $0.58$, $0.27$ and
$0.087\,\az$. (The proposal's first table attached these angles to the heights $R/2$, $0.87R$ and $0.98R$ of a sliced sphere, where $1/\cos$ is
$1.15$, $2.03$ and $5.03$.) The cascade sets the exponent between $1.01$ and $1.10$ across galaxies (\S17), near the $\ell^1$ plane,
$\mathcal S\simeq1+\az/g$; at those tilts the dilation is $2.67$, $4.67$ and $12.4$. A fixed exponent near the Euclidean one is too sharp at the
transition: the rotation curves put $2\pi/3$ at $\Delta\chi^2/S^2=397$ (\S17).

\emph{Smallest at the centre, growing toward the edge.} Where $g\gg\az$, $\theta\to0$ and $\mathcal S\to1$: Newton. Where $g\ll\az$, around an
isolated mass, $\mathcal S\simeq\az/g=r/\ell$ with $\ell=\sqrt{GM_b/\az}$ the coupling length of \S21.1. Around $10^{11}M_\odot$ of baryons,
$\ell=11.0$ kpc and the dilation is $2$ at $16$ kpc, $4$ at $39$ kpc and $11$ at $116$ kpc---the range the proposal gave---and $91$ at $1$ Mpc; at
$\gbar=0.1$, $1$, $10$ and $30\,\az$ it is $3.67$, $1.58$, $1.067$ and $1.019$ (GRUT \S22.9). \emph{The tilt is the horizon's:}
$\az=(\sqrt3/\pi^2)\,cH$ \eqref{eq:a0}, and at the present $(2/3\pi)\,c^2\sqrt{\Lambda/3}$ (\S24.15). Without a horizon $\az=0$, $\theta=0$ and
$\mathcal S=1$ at every radius: baryons alone, nothing to call dark matter. The dilation is the effect of dark energy, the geometry, on the
lensing of the baryons---the proposal's statement, derived.

\emph{It varies:} through each system's field, $\theta$ rising outward; through $n(z)$; through capture, $\mathcal S=1/[1-\Gamma(1-\mu)]$
(\S17), which is $1$ for the Solar System and wide binaries (\S15.1); and through the relaxation state (\S15.2), a relaxed galaxy carrying the
dilation of the threshold law and an unrelaxed cluster its gathered share. \sM\ Weak lensing extends the radial acceleration relation by two
decades of baryonic acceleration, in agreement with the MOND-type law, yet early- and late-type galaxies of the same stellar mass differ by at
least $6\sigma$ \cite{Brouwer2021}, which no function of the acceleration alone can give; clusters depart from the galactic relation
\cite{Eckert2022}, one CLASH analysis finding the deep slope at a scale seventeen times higher \cite{Tian2020}. \sP\ The dilation differs between
systems at the same acceleration as their capture and relaxation differ (register row 33).

\emph{What carries it.} \sD\ In general relativity light bends only by energy and momentum, and spacetime's excitation carries them, as a
gravitational wave does, without any matter. The dilation looks like mass and is no matter: in a relaxed system the excited geometry is
distributed exactly as the phantom density of the field equation, $(4\pi G)^{-1}\nabla\cdot[(1-\mu)\nabla\Phi]$ (GRUT \S46.6)---the mass the flat
inference sees and no matter supplies---and since the completion lenses by the dynamical potential (\S27), the dilation of lensing and of the
rotation curves is one. \emph{How far it grows.} \sO\ If the dilation draws only on the excitation bound to a system, it stops where that is
spent, at the $r_t$ of \S15.1: $59$ kpc around $10^{11}M_\odot$ holding its cosmic share. \sM\ Weak lensing finds the circular velocities of
isolated galaxies flat with no clear decline to $1$ Mpc \cite{Mistele2024}, a dilation near $90$ there: either an isolated galaxy binds $30$--$100$
times its baryons in excited spacetime, or the dilation is not drawn from the bound store; the data so far favour a dilation that keeps growing
(register row 34).

\sD\ \emph{The dilation on the sky.} Lensing sees the dilation through a second one of the same form, the slant of each shell to the line of
sight:
\begin{equation}
\Sigma(b)=2\int_b^\infty\rho(r)\,S_r(b)\,dr,\qquad S_r(b)=\frac{r}{\sqrt{r^2-b^2}}=\frac1{\cos\psi},\qquad \int_0^\infty\Sigma(b)\,2\pi b\,db=M,
\label{eq:trsd-abel}
\end{equation}
with $\psi$ the angle between the line of sight and the shell's normal where it crosses: $1$ at the centre of the shell's disc, rising to the
limb. For a thin shell of surface density $\sigma$ this is the proposal's $\kappa=(\Sigma_{\rm surf}/\Sigma_{\rm crit})\,S$ for each crossing, and
$2\sigma S_R(b)/\Sigma_{\rm crit}$ for the two. Over the sky each crossing's mean is $2$, so a closed shell's mean surface density is $4\sigma$:
Cauchy's surface-to-shadow ratio, which the proposal's first form took as the mean dilation, and with its base the deficit-$\pi$ cone of every
system (\S21.1), with its equilateral axial section, gives $3$. The four is TRSD's own: a ball is the union of cones of height $R$ from its
centre to its surface, $V=\tfrac13RA$, and the cone of \S24.3 stands on the shadow, $\tfrac13R\,\pi R^2$, so the closure
$M_{\rm Hubble}/M_{\rm total}=4$ is Cauchy's ratio. The last equation of~\eqref{eq:trsd-abel}, since $\int_0^r2\pi b\cdot2S_r\,db=4\pi r^2$, says
the sky's slant redistributes toward the limbs and keeps the total, so the excess lensing measures is the field's dilation, carried onto the
sky intact; in an aperture the cylinder takes in the outer shells, by $\pi/2$ on the isothermal plateau beyond $r_*$ (\S24.7). Nor is the
excess one number: $6.35$ for the universe as a whole, $1+\Omega_c/\Omega_b$, reached at $R_{200}$ in clusters, and growing without a fixed mean
in galaxies. At the splashback radius, where the infalling streams of the excited geometry turn round, the density drops sharply
\cite{MoreDiemerKravtsov2015}, and the slant turns that edge into the steepening measured around clusters \cite{Chang2018}.

\sM\ \emph{The Bullet Cluster.} The lensing peaks of 1E\,0657$-$56 lie within $1\sigma$ of the galaxies' light and $8\sigma$ from the gas, the
dominant baryonic component, $29$--$48$ times the stars at the gas peaks; at the subcluster $\kappa=0.20\pm0.05$ at the brightest galaxy and
$0.02\pm0.06$ at the gas peak, where the visible mass is $1.8$ times larger \cite{Clowe2006}. \sD\ A dilation computed from the baryons present
would follow the gas; so would the phantom density of the field equation alone (\S27). The dilation follows the excited geometry that carries
it, and that is collisionless: with $X=X_0$ its flow is geodesic, $u^\nu\nabla_\nu u_\mu=\nabla_\mu(\partial\theta\cdot\partial\theta)/2X_0=0$; it is
pressureless, and the constraint contains no interaction, so it scatters only gravitationally, $\sigma/m=0$. A cluster, about four crossing
times old, holds its infall configuration (\S15.2), and a merger of two is the passage of two excited geometries through each other with
their galaxies, while the gas is shocked and held back: the galaxies sit where the dilation is large and the gas where it is small, as the
proposal had it. A system holding its cosmic share is dilated by $1+\Omega_c/\Omega_b=6.35$, $f_b=0.158$, with the derived baryon ratio
(\S24.5). \sM\ The X-COP clusters reach the cosmic baryon fraction at $R_{200}$ \cite{Eckert2022}, and in the Bullet Cluster the
dark-plus-galaxy convergence is about seven times the gas's \cite{Clowe2006}. \sP\ $\sigma/m=0$, against $\sigma/m<0.63\ \mathrm{cm^2\,g^{-1}}$
($95\%$) from eleven double-radio-relic mergers \cite{Jee2026}; no offset between the excess and the galaxies; and the splashback edge of cold
matter around clusters. The merger is worked in \S15.3. Register rows 30--32 and 41--42.

\sD\ \emph{The dilation field.} The proposal's second form writes the dilation as a field, $S=e^{-\psi}$, with $S\simeq1-2\Phi/c^2$ in the
weak field: the refractive index of the geometry. With $\gamma=1$ (\S27) light obeys Fermat's principle in the optical metric
$d\ell_{\rm opt}=S\,d\ell$, so $S$ dilates the measured path, delays the light by $\int(S-1)\,d\ell/c$ and bends it by $\nabla_\perp\!\int\ln S\,d\ell$. It is
the conformal factor of the optical metric, not of spacetime, as the proposal says; what it records is spacetime's dilation along the light path:
in the weak field time is dilated by $1-\Phi/c^2$ and space by $1-\Phi/c^2$, and light, crossing both, is slowed by their product,
$S\simeq1-2\Phi/c^2$---half from time and half from space, the two halves that double the bending of light over Newton's. Hence the names:
$S$ is the dilation field and $\mathcal S$, its gradient against the baryons', the dilation. Its value differs from one by $2|\Phi|/c^2$, a few parts per
million in galaxies and $10^{-5}$--$10^{-4}$ in clusters; its gradient carries the field, $\nabla\ln S=2\mathbf g/c^2$. So the dilation above is a
ratio of gradients, $\mathcal S=\partial_r\ln S/\partial_r\ln S_b$, with $S_b$ the index the baryons alone would make. In these variables the
field equation~\eqref{eq:aqual} reads
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\nabla\cdot\Big[\mu\Big(\frac{c^2|\nabla\ln S|}{2\ac}\Big)\,\nabla\ln S\Big]=-\frac{8\pi G}{c^2}\,\rho_b,\qquad R_{\az}\,|\nabla\ln S|=\frac{g}{\az},}
\label{eq:trsd-dilation-field}
\end{equation}
with $R_{\az}=c^2/2\az=3.91\times10^{26}$ m the radius of the horizon whose surface gravity is $\az$ (\S21.1), and with capture as in \S17. Where
the path's dilation changes by more than one $e$-fold per $R_{\az}$ the geometry is stiff, and the equation is the proposal's
$(\nabla^2-\mu_{\rm Y}^2)\ln S=-(8\pi G/c^2)\rho$ at $S\simeq1$; where it changes more slowly, the geometry softens and dilates further. The
proposal's self-coupling therefore acts through the gradient: the value of $\ln S$ is a few parts per million throughout a galaxy, while its
gradient in units of $1/R_{\az}$ runs over decades. Outside a source the proposal's solution $S=\exp[(2GM_S/c^2)\,e^{-\mu_{\rm Y}r}/r]$ is exact
for a point source. Inside, Gauss fixes the gradient, $\partial_r\ln S=-2GM_S(<r)/c^2r^2$, while the value adds the shells outside $r$. The
screening scale has one value in the rotation reading, the horizon's, $\mu_{\rm Y}=1/R_\infty$; it changes $\kappa$ by less than $10^{-7}$ inside
$1$ Mpc. \sP\ The lensing excess has no exponential cut inside the horizon (register row 35).

\sD\ \emph{The switch.} The proposal's coupling $\alpha(\rho)=\alpha_0(\rho/\rho_c)^n/[1+(\rho/\rho_c)^n]$ is the capture fraction of \S15.1. A
system of radius $R$ in a field of dispersion $\sigma$ binds $\Gamma=\operatorname{erf}X-(2X/\sqrt\pi)\,e^{-X^2}$ of the excited geometry streaming past it,
with $X^2=\rho/\rho_c$ and $\rho_c=3\sigma^2/4\pi GR^2$. So $\Gamma\simeq(4/3\sqrt\pi)(\rho/\rho_c)^{3/2}$ below $\rho_c$ and $\Gamma\to1$ above it: a switch
with $n=3/2$ and $\alpha_0=1$, derived, whose $\rho_c$ is set by the system's size and the field around it, not by nature. A galaxy forming in
the cold cosmic field captures fully; the Sun at its MOND radius has $\rho/\rho_c=4.5\times10^{-6}$ and $\Gamma=7\times10^{-9}$. The switch gates
the excess, not the baryons: what has captured nothing lenses as its baryons, never as nothing. \sD\ In the second form's galaxy estimate the
exponential is $1+O(10^{-6})$, so the whole excess would be $\alpha_0$. A constant $\alpha_0$ at high density is shared by the laboratory, the Sun
and the stars, and is absorbed into $G$. A coupling that rises with density puts the excess where it is absent, in galaxy centres and the Solar
System, and removes it where it is largest, in the outskirts and in low-surface-brightness galaxies \cite{McGaugh2016,Lelli2016}. $\alpha\to0$
would switch off the gas's own gravity, and a shock compresses gas. What the second form asks of $\alpha$ in the Bullet Cluster---galaxies
lensing, gas not---the collisionless excited geometry gives (above).

\sD\ \emph{Scales and the background.} The proposal's last part asks that each scale be the coarse-grained output of those below and the
fixed input of those above. An intermediate scale $B$ is fixed by two outer scales $A\cong C$ jointly, as neither their product nor their union,
and the background solves its own consistency condition. The background's condition is the closure law~\eqref{eq:closure}: the contents' defects
summed are the horizon, $\sum r_s=R_H$, and the horizon in turn sets, through $\az$, the dilation of everything inside it---above and below at
once, with no external stage. The rule that does not change with scale is the spinning-arc rule, $\omega R=c$, obeyed by the final horizon,
black holes and every particle's sphere (\S24.14--24.15): the fixed point of the coarse-graining. And the middle scale is the geometric mean of
the outer two, their midpoint on the logarithmic scale:
\begin{equation}
\ell_P^2=\tfrac12\,r_s\lambda_C,\qquad \ell^2=\frac{GM}{\az}=r_s\,R_{\az},\qquad g^2=\gbar\,\az\ \ (\text{deep regime}).
\label{eq:trsd-geometric-means}
\end{equation}
The Planck length lies between the gravitational and quantum lengths of every mass (\S24.12). The coupling length lies between the system's own
horizon and the horizon whose surface gravity is $\az$: $7{,}200$ AU for the Sun, between $3$ km and $13$ Gpc, and $8.5$ kpc for the Milky Way's
baryons. The measured field lies between the baryons' field and the horizon's, and at every acceleration $g^2=\gbar(g^n+\az^n)^{1/n}$. \sO\ A
sheaf or renormalization-group formulation of the coarse-graining is not supplied.

\sD\ \emph{The dilation on the unbound web.} A homogeneous universe has no gradient of the dilation field, so $\mathcal S=1$ and the
background---the Friedmann equation, the sound horizon, the CMB's $H_0=67.35$---cannot move. The distance ladder measures the expansion inside
our own neighbourhood, $0.023<z<0.15$, and continuity gives the rate inside a region of mean contrast $\delta$ growing at $f=d\ln\delta/d\ln a$:
\begin{equation}
\frac{H_{\rm loc}}{H}=1-\frac{f\,\delta}{3}.
\label{eq:trsd-local-H}
\end{equation}
The local distance network's $73.50\pm0.81$ \cite{H0DN2026} needs $f|\delta|=0.275\pm0.036$; undilated growth, $f=0.528$, needs a region $52\%$
underdense. The web is deep: its rms peculiar field, $\tfrac12\Omega_mH_0^2\sigma(R)R$, is $0.0010$--$0.0024\,\az$ from $12$ to $500$ Mpc and
$0.0079\,\az$ at a point, whose baryons' share the law dilates $28.9$-fold, at most $4.5$ times the cosmic dilation $1+\Omega_c/\Omega_b$ that the
share already supplies. The field cannot tell $8\,h^{-1}$ Mpc ($0.0019\,\az$) from $300$ Mpc ($0.0014\,\az$), so a dilation set by it alone raises
$S_8$ to $0.89$ for the smallest boost, $6.4\sigma$ above DES Y6's $0.789\pm0.012$ \cite{DESY6}: excluded. \sD\ The dilation keeps a clock's
causes. A clock runs at $\Theta=N\sqrt{1-v^2/c^2}$, $N^2=1-r_s/R-R^2/r_\Lambda^2$ (GRUT \S43B \cite{GRUT2026}), slowed by its well, its motion
and the horizon; in a bound system the dilation is the horizon's tilt of the light path, on the unbound web its setting of the clocks.
\emph{When:} the horizon sets a region's clocks only beyond its balance surface, where its mean density is below $2\rho_\Lambda$: the universe
as a whole from $q=0$, $z=0.630$, the onset of acceleration; a region $20\%$ underdense today from $z=0.713$, one $80\%$ overdense from
$z=0.378$. At recombination, with matter at $3\times10^8$ times $2\rho_\Lambda$ and the baryons moving with the photon fluid at $0.45c$, it is
off. \emph{Where:} a region holds the medium where its well slows time more than the medium's peculiar motion does, $v^2<2|\Phi|$, the
capture fraction, with the potential carrying the scale the field lacks: rms $|\Phi|/c^2=0.9\times10^{-6}$ at $12$ Mpc and $1.7\times10^{-5}$ at
$300$ Mpc, flat beyond the equality horizon $1/k_{\rm eq}=96$ Mpc. All of the motion counts---the flows the dilation drives and the haloes'
virial motion, $264\ \mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$ over $77\%$ of the mass---and the law acts on the field the matter moves in, so the dilation exceeds
the share where $\Gamma>0.873$. Dispersion and cut close on themselves at $\sigma_{1D}=494\ \mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$ and $68$ Mpc, with boosts
$1.6$--$4.2$ above it; no input remains. \emph{Light:} de Sitter is conformally flat, so the horizon dilates clocks and rulers by one factor,
$\tilde g_{\mu\nu}=\Omega^2g_{\mu\nu}$. The optical index, their ratio, is unchanged, a photon picks up $\Omega$ only at its two ends, and only
massive bodies feel the pull:
\begin{equation}
\frac{\tilde g_{ij}}{|\tilde g_{00}|}=\frac{g_{ij}}{|g_{00}|},\qquad \ddot{\mathbf x}=-\nabla\Phi-c^2\nabla\ln\Omega .
\label{eq:trsd-web-conformal}
\end{equation}
Had light crossed the web dilated, the late Sachs--Wolfe signal would rise $4{,}200$-fold at $\ell=10$: excluded. As derived it falls, to
$0.28$, $0.54$ and $0.71$ of the background's at $\ell=2$, $10$ and $20$. \sM\ With the cut, $\sigma_8$ rises by $0.16\%$ today and less at the
lensing epochs, cosmic shear is unchanged from $\ell=100$ and CMB lensing from $L=40$: DES Y6 stays $2.8\sigma$ below, as for the background,
and the lensing amplitude at the framework's own neutrino mass, $S_8^{\rm CMBL}=0.822$, is the $0.825^{+0.015}_{-0.013}$ measured jointly by ACT,
SPT and Planck \cite{Qu2026}, leaving no excess for a negative neutrino mass \cite{GreenMeyers2024}; the floor's pull rests on DESI's BAO matter
density \cite{DESI2025}. The Cosmicflows-4 bulk flows, $387\pm28$ and $419\pm36\ \mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$ within $150$ and $200\,h^{-1}$ Mpc, below
$0.03\%$ and $0.003\%$ in $\Lambda$CDM \cite{Watkins2023}, fall at the $78$th and $90$th percentiles of the dilated web's distribution, and the
$628\pm82\ \mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$ dipole of the $120$--$160\,h^{-1}$ Mpc shell \cite{NusserTully2026} at its $98$th. \sP\ The ladder's $H_0$ is the background's. A clock or ruler is shifted by $|\Phi|/c^2\lesssim10^{-5}$, so the dilation cannot make a
ladder read $9\%$ more, and a local void cannot carry it either: read as a matter deficit, the K-band luminosity density, $3.09\pm0.19$ below
$z=0.07$ against $5.40\pm0.27$ beyond $z=0.09$ in $10^8\,h_{70}L_\odot\,\mathrm{Mpc^{-3}}$ \cite{Keenan2013}, is a void $37\%$ deep whose interior
would expand at $74.2\pm0.6$, but the deficit ends by $z\approx0.09$, beyond it the rate would fall back by $8.3\%$, and the supernovae show no
change of $H_0$ across $0.023<z<0.15$ to $0.60\%$ \cite{Kenworthy2019}. The dilation widens the local rate's scatter to $0.88\%$ on $300$ Mpc
($0.41\%$ in $\Lambda$CDM). The framework therefore has one expansion rate, $67.35$, and predicts that distance ladders calibrated alike
converge on $67.3$ within that scatter: the network lies $6.1\sigma$ away, SH0ES $4.8\sigma$, the red-giant and carbon-star ladder $1.5\sigma$;
JWST distances to the supernova hosts settled at the network's value at $5\sigma$ would falsify it. \sH\ GRUT as built
keeps the web undilated; the dilation as geometry rather than store, the branch isolated galaxies' lensing favours (register row 34), lets
the web take it from the horizon on a clock's terms. Unaffected either way: TM1 and $\az(z)$ (GRUT \S53.9 \cite{GRUT2026}; register rows 36--37).

\sD\ \emph{What this changes.} Derived: dark energy as spacetime unexcited and the cosmic share as spacetime excited~\eqref{eq:trsd-dark-one},
with no separate dark matter, the vacuum the generator-defect tension over the square of the final radius; $w=-1+\Theta$ with the vacuum's
density as the threshold~\eqref{eq:trsd-wdark}; the dilation $\mathcal S=1/\cos_n\theta$, $\tan\theta=\az/g$~\eqref{eq:trsd-dilation}, $1$ at the
centre and $r/\ell$ toward the edge, set by the horizon and varying with capture and relaxation, its excess carried by the excited geometry as
the phantom density; the dilation field as the index of the optical metric, its gradient carrying the law~\eqref{eq:trsd-dilation-field}; the
switch as the capture fraction; the sky's slant, which keeps the total~\eqref{eq:trsd-abel}, and the closure's four as Cauchy's ratio; the
Bullet Cluster's offset from the collisionless excitation; and the background as the closure law, with the middle scales as geometric
means~\eqref{eq:trsd-geometric-means}. \sO\ How far the dilation grows is open (register row 34). Unchanged: the background expansion, which has no gradient and so no
local dilation, and with it the CMB's $H_0$, TM1 and the acceleration scale at $z\simeq1$. Changed in the proposal's reading: the
growth of the web above $68$ Mpc after $z=0.63$, on clocks and not on light~\eqref{eq:trsd-web-conformal}, which carries the measured bulk flows
and lowers the late Sachs--Wolfe signal, with $S_8$ and CMB lensing at the background's values. Not changed: the distance-ladder $H_0$.
Corrected: the first form of this paragraph, which held the test run's tensions
unchanged---only the background is; its second form, which read the ladder's $H_0$ as the rate inside a local void, against the supernova
Hubble diagram, and cut the web's dilation at $29$--$80$ Mpc by the motions it counted; the heights of the proposal's first table, and its single mean dilation
$\langle\mathcal S\rangle=4$, which is Cauchy's ratio, not the measured excess; in the second form, the excess assigned to the value of the dilation
field rather than its gradient, a coupling that rises with density rather than with capture and multiplies the baryons rather than the
excess, and the enclosed-mass form of $\ln S$ inside a source.

% =====================================================================
\section{Quantum Condensate Limit}\label{sec:condensate}
% =====================================================================
\sS\ For occupation $N\gg1$ the field is replaced by its coherent expectation value, and the
nonrelativistic envelope $\chi_{\rm cl}=(\hbar/\sqrt{2\mdelta})[\psi e^{-i\mdelta c^2t/\hbar}+{\rm c.c.}]$
obeys the Gross--Pitaevskii equation
\begin{equation}
i\hbar\,\partial_t\psi=-\frac{\hbar^2}{2\mdelta}\nabla^2\psi+\mdelta\Phi\,\psi+g_\chi|\psi|^2\psi ,
\qquad \rho_\chi=\mdelta|\psi|^2 ,
\label{eq:gpe}
\end{equation}
with $g_\chi$ from~\eqref{eq:gchi}. Quantum pressure
$Q=-(\hbar^2/2\mdelta^2)\nabla^2\sqrt{\rho_\chi}/\sqrt{\rho_\chi}$ supports cores of size the
de Broglie length. For $10^{-22}$ eV the reduced Compton length is $0.064$ pc and
$\lambda_{\rm dB}=2\pi\hbar/\mdelta v=0.60$ kpc at $200\ \mathrm{km\,s^{-1}}$; at the
Lyman-$\alpha$ bound, $2\times10^{-20}$ eV, $\lambda_{\rm dB}=3.0$ pc, so the predicted solitonic
cores are parsec-scale.

\section{The Coupled System}\label{sec:coupled}
% =====================================================================
\sD\ The condensate~\eqref{eq:gpe}, the field equation with the three-arc law,
\begin{equation}
\nabla\cdot\big[\mu_n(|\nabla\Phi|/\ac)\,\nabla\Phi\big]=4\pi G\big(\rho_b+\mdelta|\psi|^2\big),
\end{equation}
and the phantom density $\rho_{\rm ph}=(4\pi G)^{-1}\nabla\cdot[(1-\mu_n)\nabla\Phi]$ form the
coupled system; the effective dark matter is $\mdelta|\psi|^2+\rho_{\rm ph}$. \sD\ The covariant
completion is general relativity with the conjugate-pair scalar held at its rotation rate by
$\lambda(X-X_0)$ (GRUT \S23.1 \cite{GRUT2026}); for bound systems its weak-field limit is the field equation
above, through the polarization law of the bound medium (\sI).

\section{Cosmological Implications}\label{sec:cosmo}
% =====================================================================

\subsection*{24.1\quad Flatness}
\sD\ The mass of a ball of radius $R_H$ at the critical density is
$M_{\rm Hubble}=\tfrac43\pi R_H^3\rho_c=c^3/2GH_0$, and its Schwarzschild radius is exactly
$R_H$. Conversely, $2GM/c^2=R_H$ forces $\rho=\rho_c$, which for a Friedmann universe is
$\Omega_{\rm tot}=1$. \sI\ draft 1 wrote the same density as the generator-defect tension,
$\dgen^{-1}\fd^2(\pi)/R_H^2=3c^4/8\pi GR_H^2=\rho_cc^2$, and called it $\rho_{\rm DE}$; it is
the total, of which the vacuum is the fraction $\Omega_\Lambda$. Eliminating $H_0$ with
$\az=\atrsd cH_0$,
\begin{equation}
\rho_cc^2=\frac{\pi^3}{8}\,\frac{\az^2}{G},\qquad \rho_\Lambda c^2=\Omega_\Lambda\,\frac{\pi^3}{8}\,\frac{\az^2}{G}.
\end{equation}
Planck and BAO give $\Omega_K=0.0007\pm0.0019$ \cite{Planck2020}.

\subsection*{24.2\quad Horizon totals}
\sD\ Over the cone volume $V_{\rm cone}=\pi R_H^3/3$:
\begin{equation}
E_{\rm total}=\frac{c^5}{8GH_0},\qquad M_{\rm total}=\frac{c^3}{8GH_0},\qquad
I_{\rm total}=\frac N2=\frac{\pi c^5}{\hbar GH_0^2}\ \text{nats},
\end{equation}
that is $1.916\times10^{69}$ J, $2.132\times10^{52}$ kg and $1.929\times10^{122}$ nats
($2.783\times10^{122}$ bits) for $H_0=73.04$, with $N=3.858\times10^{122}$. The information
of a direction is taken as the full azimuth, $I_F=N$, twice the scalar content $I_E=N/2$
\sI, and the corresponding force per unit length of horizon is
\begin{equation}
F=\frac{\hbar c}{8\pi R_H^2}\,I_F=\frac{\hbar c\,N}{8\pi R_H^2}=\frac{c^4}{4G}=3.026\times10^{43}\ \mathrm N ,
\end{equation}
the maximum force of one horizon \cite{Gibbons2002}. Solving $2\pi R_H^2=N\lP^2$ for $G$ gives
$G=2\pi c^3R_H^2/\hbar N$: this is the definition of $N$ read backwards, not an independent
determination of $G$ (the draft-1 ``agreement with CODATA to $0.001\%$'' is withdrawn as circular).

\subsection*{24.3\quad The Hubble-sphere mass}
\sD\ The critical energy density over the full ball gives
\begin{equation}
E_{\rm Hubble}=\frac{c^5}{2GH_0}=7.664\times10^{69}\ \mathrm J,\qquad
M_{\rm Hubble}=\frac{c^3}{2GH_0}=8.527\times10^{52}\ \mathrm{kg}\quad(H_0=73.04),
\end{equation}
with $r_s=R_H$. The ratio $M_{\rm Hubble}/M_{\rm total}=4$ is the ratio of the sphere to the
cone of height $R_H$, and it is the closure of Theorem~\ref{thm:closure}. Multiplying the
fractions of \S24.5 by $M_{\rm Hubble}$ gives the component masses in the astronomical
convention: baryons $4.254\times10^{51}$, condensate $2.315\times10^{52}$, all matter
$2.740\times10^{52}$ and vacuum $5.787\times10^{52}$ kg.

\subsection*{24.4\quad Three currencies}
\sD\ Mass, energy and information are one amount at fixed exchange rates:
\begin{gather}
\frac{E_{\rm total}}{M_{\rm total}}=c^2,\qquad
\frac{E_{\rm total}}{I_{\rm total}}=\frac{\hbar H_0}{8\pi}=9.932\times10^{-54}\ \mathrm{J\,nat^{-1}},\\
\frac{M_{\rm total}}{I_{\rm total}}=\frac{\hbar H_0}{8\pi c^2}=\frac{M_\star}{8\pi}=1.105\times10^{-70}\ \mathrm{kg\,nat^{-1}},
\end{gather}
with the natural mass unit $M_\star=\hbar H_0/c^2=2.777\times10^{-69}$ kg ($H_0=73.04$).

\subsection*{24.5\quad The cosmic budget}
\emph{Reading note} I5 is withdrawn: with the present at the resonance the dark ratio is $2.580$ (\S24.16). The $5/2$ budget
is kept here in full, and the numbers of this version are quoted at it so that each can be traced.

\sI\ (I5) The dark-energy-to-condensate ratio is identified with the defect power ratio of
\S25, $\Omega_\Lambda/\Omega_c=C_3/C_2=5/2$. \sD\ The baryon ratio is draft 1's product of the scale coefficient and the Torridity exponent,
$\Omega_b/\Omega_c=\atrsd\,n$. The exponent is dynamic (\S17), and the universe reads it at the load
of its own horizon: the surface gravity $g_H=c^2/2R_H=R_H/2t_H^2$, the size over twice the square of
the age, which in units of the scale is the pure number $z_H=\pi^2/2\sqrt3=2.8491$ \sI. There
$n=1.06540$, and
\begin{equation}
\frac{\Omega_b}{\Omega_c}=\frac{\sqrt3}{\pi^2}\,n\Big(\frac{\pi^2}{2\sqrt3}\Big)=0.18697,
\end{equation}
$+0.3\sigma$ from Planck; draft 1's $\sqrt3/3\pi$, with $n=\pi/3$, lay at $-1.2\sigma$. With $\Omega_\Lambda/\Omega_c=5/2$ (the
identification I5, withdrawn in \S24.16) the budget was $\Omega_c=0.27123$, $\Omega_b=0.05071$, $\Omega_\Lambda=0.67806$,
$\Omega_m=0.32194$, and matching the acoustic angle gave $H_0=67.12$ (GRUT \S25.7 \cite{GRUT2026}); the framework's
budget is the resonance budget of \S24.16, and every current number is quoted there. The calibrated budget of draft 1,
with $\mathcal W=1+\sqrt3/3\pi+5/2=(21\pi+2\sqrt3)/6\pi=3.683776$,
\begin{gather}
\Omega_c=\frac{6\pi}{21\pi+2\sqrt3}=0.27146,\qquad
\Omega_b=\frac{2\sqrt3}{21\pi+2\sqrt3}=0.04989,\\
\Omega_\Lambda=\frac{15\pi}{21\pi+2\sqrt3}=0.67865,\qquad
\Omega_m=0.32135 .
\end{gather}
\sP\ Against Planck 2018 ($\Omega_m=0.3153\pm0.0073$, $\Omega_\Lambda/\Omega_c=2.589\pm0.088$,
$\omega_b/\omega_c=0.1864\pm0.0022$) \cite{Planck2020} that budget's pulls were $+0.9\sigma$, $-1.0\sigma$ and
$+0.3\sigma$. Fitting the CMB acoustic angle at it with CAMB \cite{Lewis2000} gave
\begin{equation}
H_0=67.12\pm0.06\,(\theta_*)\pm0.04\,(\nu)\ \mathrm{km\,s^{-1}\,Mpc^{-1}},
\end{equation}
$0.4\sigma$ below Planck's $\Lambda$CDM value and $5.7\sigma$ below SH0ES
\cite{Riess2022}, with $\omega_m=0.14504$ ($+1.9\sigma$) and $\omega_b=0.02275$ ($+2.5\sigma$). The baryon
ratio agrees with Planck's; the absolute baryon density does not, because $\Omega_\Lambda/\Omega_c=5/2$
raises $\Omega_b$ and $\Omega_c$ together, by $2.4\%$ at fixed ratio. \sP\ The CMB measures $\omega_b$
from the acoustic peak heights: a full-spectrum fit of the budget that excludes $\omega_b=0.02275$ at
$5\sigma$ removes the identification I5. A calibrated Planck-like fit of the full spectra (GRUT \S25.9
\cite{GRUT2026}) disfavours $5/2$ at $4.6\sigma$ with the baryon ratio kept, preferring $\Omega_\Lambda/\Omega_c=2.61\pm0.02$:
close to, but short of, the $5\sigma$ threshold; the full Planck likelihood decides, and the baryon ratio passes. With the measured ratio in place of $5/2$ the budget
reproduces Planck's densities, $\omega_b=0.02239$ and $\omega_c=0.1198$, and lies $1.9\sigma$ from DESI's matter density, as
$\Lambda$CDM does (GRUT \S25.10). The 2026 DESI Lyman-$\alpha$ full-shape analysis, independent of the CMB,
measures $\Omega_m=0.325\pm0.018$ and, with a nucleosynthesis prior, $H_0=66.5\pm1.3$
\cite{DESI2026lya}: $-0.2\sigma$ and $+0.5\sigma$ from the budget. The budget also fixes $q_0=-0.517$, the onset of acceleration at
$z=0.615$, and the epoch at which the horizon energy grows at exactly the maximum power
$c^5/4G$, $z=0.017$ \cite{GRUT2026}.

\subsection*{24.6\quad The dual of the Hubble mass}
\sD\ Evaluating the duality~\eqref{eq:duality} at $M_{\rm Hubble}$,
\begin{equation}
m_Uc^2=\frac{\sqrt8}{8\pi}\,\frac{\mP^2c^2}{M_{\rm Hubble}}=\frac{\sqrt2}{2\pi}\,\hbar H_0=0.2251\,\hbar H_0 ,
\end{equation}
$3.5\times10^{-34}$ eV for $H_0=73.04$: the dual of the whole horizon has the Hubble mass scale.
(draft 1 quoted $\sim10^{-33}$ eV.)

\subsection*{24.7\quad The transition radius}
\sP\ Around a mass $M$ the acceleration equals $\ac$ at
\begin{equation}
r_*=\sqrt{\frac{GM}{\mu(1)\,\ac}},\qquad \mu_1(1)=0.4206,\quad \mu_2(1)=0.4623,\quad \mu_3(1)=0.4790 .
\end{equation}
For $H_0=67.35$ and $n=1.75$ ($\mu(1)=0.4557$): $0.060$ pc for the Sun, $1.9$ kpc for $10^9M_\odot$, $19$ kpc for $10^{11}M_\odot$ and $0.60$ Mpc for $10^{14}M_\odot$. \sD\ The phantom mass per unit radius,
$dM_{\rm ph}/dr$, rises monotonically across $r_*$ to its deep-regime plateau; computed for
$\mu_1$ and $\mu_3$, it has no maximum, so there is no ring-shaped excess. The lensing
signature is the transition itself: stacked excess surface density turns from Newtonian to
isothermal ($\rho_{\rm ph}\propto r^{-2}$) at $r_*$. This replaces the draft-1 ``horizon resonance
ring''.


\subsection*{24.8\quad Limits and the freeze}
\sD\ Every dimensionful quantity is a pure number times a monomial in $c$, $\hbar$ and $R_H$ and a power
of $N$ (\S26). Three kinds of bound follow: constant limits, with no horizon in them ($c$, $F$, the force
$c^4/4G$ between two bodies, the power $c^5/4G$); ceilings and floors, which contain the horizon (the
lengths $R_H$ and $\lP$, the masses $c^2R_H/2G$ and $\hbar/cR_H$, the forces $F$ and $\hbar c/R_H^2$),
whose ratios are $\sqrt{N/2\pi}$, $N/4\pi$ or $N/2\pi$; and clock readings, built from a particular event,
which fall as $1/t$. Since $N=2\pi R_H^2/\lP^2\propto H^{-2}$ and $H\to H_\infty=H_0\sqrt{\Omega_\Lambda}$
for $w=-1$,
\begin{equation}
\frac{N(t)}{N_\infty}=\Omega_\Lambda(t)
\end{equation}
exactly: the universe holds $68.4\%$ of its final cells, $N_\infty=6.63\times10^{122}$, and the horizon
stops at $17.56$ Gly. The horizon energy grows at the maximum power $c^5/4G$ when
$\Omega_\Lambda=\tfrac23$, $0.38$ Gyr ago, and the aeon's conformal time is finite, $4.327\,c/H_0=62.8$ Gly,
of which $73.4\%$ has elapsed---$0.7310\pm0.0062$ from the DESI Lyman-$\alpha$ $\Omega_m$
\cite{DESI2026lya}, $+0.5\sigma$ (GRUT \S48.1--48.5 \cite{GRUT2026}). \sP\ All three require $w=-1$ and fail
with evolving dark energy at $5\sigma$ \cite{DESI2025}.

\subsection*{24.9\quad Constants and clock readings}
\sD\ The cells do not stretch: with a fixed cell count $G\propto R_H^2$ would drift at
$2(1+q)H=6.5\times10^{-11}\ \mathrm{yr^{-1}}$, against the $(7.1\pm7.6)\times10^{-14}\ \mathrm{yr^{-1}}$ of
lunar laser ranging \cite{HofmannMuller2018}, so the Planck length is fixed and $N\propto R_H^2$. The
fine-structure constant is not a clock reading, $\dot\alpha/\alpha=(1.0\pm1.1)\times10^{-18}\ \mathrm{yr^{-1}}$
\cite{Lange2021}, but it sets when light escapes: in the Saha balance for hydrogen, whose binding energy is
$\tfrac12\alpha^2m_ec^2$, $d\ln(1+z_{\rm rec})/d\ln\alpha=2.07$ and $d\ln(t_0/t_{\rm rec})/d\ln\alpha=3.54$.

\subsection*{24.10\quad First starlight}
\sH\ If the first starlight marks the present, $t_\star=\alpha t_0$: cosmic dawn, the onset of the
global 21-cm coupling, at $100.6$ Myr, $z=29.6$, $46.4$ MHz; stated between physical epochs,
$t_\star=\alpha t_{\rm mp}=97.9$ Myr, $z=30.2$, $45.6$ MHz. \sP\ Coupling detected below $45.5$ MHz
excludes it. A $3\%$ timing gives the age, $t_0=137.036\,t_\star$, and $H_0$ to
$\pm2.0\ \mathrm{km\,s^{-1}\,Mpc^{-1}}$ independently of the distance ladder; with the condensate it
requires $m_\delta\gtrsim4\times10^{-19}$ eV. A chance match within today's estimated range has probability
$0.53$; within $3\%$, stated in advance, $0.04$ (GRUT \S48.8--48.10). \sH\ With the present selected by the resonance
(\S24.13), the relation no longer selects it; it relates two physical epochs, cosmic dawn and the resonance, $t_\star=\alpha\,t_{\rm res}$, and
needs no observer: at the resonance budget (\S24.16) $100.6$ Myr, $z=29.6$, $46.4$ MHz, the $\pm3\%$ window $45.5$--$47.3$ MHz. It is not
derived, since the onset depends on the primordial amplitude and the first stars. No starlight before it is known: the earliest confirmed
galaxy is at $z=14.44$ \cite{Naidu2026}, and the claimed $z\simeq32$ candidate is a Y dwarf in the Galaxy \cite{Liu2026}.
\sD\ A frequency is a number of cycles per second, so the onset is read as a ratio of counts, the cycles received per
cycle emitted, $\nu_\star/\nu_{21}=1/(1+z_\star)$ with $\nu_{21}=1420.405751768$ MHz, and the second cancels. With the present at the resonance,
\begin{equation}
\frac{\nu_\star}{\nu_{21}}=\Big[\frac{\sqrt{4\pi^2-27}}{3\sqrt3}\,\sinh\Big(\alpha\,\mathrm{artanh}\,\frac{3\sqrt3}{2\pi}\Big)\Big]^{2/3}=\frac{1}{30.81}
\end{equation}
($46.10$ MHz; $1/30.61$, $46.41$ MHz, with radiation). The birefringence counts turns of the same phase weighted by $\alpha$,
$\beta=(\kappa\alpha/2\pi)\,H_\infty(t_0-t_{\rm rec})$ (\S24.14, \S25.3), so $\alpha$ cancels between the two, and with it the present's phase:
\begin{equation}
\boxed{H_\infty t_\star=\frac{2\pi}{\kappa}\,\beta=\frac{\pi}{3}\,\beta,\qquad
\frac{\nu_\star}{\nu_{21}}=\Big[\sqrt{\frac{\Omega_m}{\Omega_\Lambda}}\,\sinh\frac{\pi\beta}{2}\Big]^{2/3}.}
\label{eq:dawn-twist}
\end{equation}
\sP\ The measured $0.277^\circ\pm0.057^\circ$ \cite{Eskilt2026} places the onset at $42.7^{+5.6}_{-6.0}$ MHz ($z=32.2$), $0.6\sigma$ from the
closed form; it moves as $\beta^{0.66}$, so a twist known to $\pm0.01^\circ$ places it to $\pm1.0$ MHz. An onset and a twist off~\eqref{eq:dawn-twist}
by $5\sigma$ together exclude the hypothesis with the rotation's birefringence. No clock, no $\alpha$ and no Hubble constant enter; the
relation tests the hypothesis and does not derive it (GRUT \S48.8 \cite{GRUT2026}).

\subsection*{24.11\quad The capacity of the horizon}
\sD\ With $V_0=0$ (\S21.3) and cells that do not stretch, the dark energy is the energy of the
horizon's finite capacity,
\begin{equation}
\rho_\Lambda=\frac34\,\frac{\rho_P}{N_\infty}=1.130\times10^{-123}\rho_P,\qquad \rho_P=\frac{c^7}{\hbar G^2},
\end{equation}
exactly: the number of the cosmological constant problem \cite{Weinberg1989} is the inverse of the
final cell count. A vacuum of Planck density would exceed the horizon's content by exactly $4N/3$ and put
it inside its own Schwarzschild radius; gravity couples to every field alike, so the horizon condition
caps the vacuum energy of all fields together at the critical density \cite{CKN1999,Li2004}. In the
information--energy relation of Section~\ref{sec:scale_bridge}, each bit of the final horizon carries
$\hbar c/8\pi R_\infty=\tfrac14k_BT_\infty$, with $T_\infty=\hbar H_\infty/2\pi k_B=2.18\times10^{-30}$ K.
\sD\ The value of $N_\infty$ is the boundary datum (\S26: no internal condition fixes it). The suppression of
high-frequency vacuum modes follows mode by mode: a field theory in the horizon is consistent only up to
the wavenumber at which its zero-point energy reaches the critical density,
$k_{\max}=(6\pi)^{1/4}/\sqrt{\lP R_H}$, a wavelength of $0.14$ mm \cite{CKN1999} (GRUT \S48.11).


\subsection*{24.12\quad The closure law}
\sD\ By the defect map, $\delta=8\pi GM/(c^2R_H)=4\pi r_s/R_H$, every energy inside the horizon is a defect on it,
and the horizon is a closed surface, $\chi=2$. Gauss--Bonnet fixes the total, as in $2{+}1$ gravity, where a closed
universe has total deficit exactly $4\pi$ \cite{DJtH1984,Brill2003}:
\begin{equation}
\sum_ir_{s,i}=\frac{\chi}{2}\,R_H,\qquad \Omega_{\rm total}=\frac{\chi}{2}=1,
\label{eq:closure}
\end{equation}
a topological form of the flatness derived above (Planck: $1.0007\pm0.0019$). The $\mathbb Z_2$ cut of the deficit
$\pi$ halves energy, defect and information together; iterated, it gives the scale ladder $E_n=E_H/2^n$ from the
Hubble mass, $E_H=c^4R_H/2G$, down to one Planck cell at $n=\log_2(4\pi R_H^2/\ell_P^2)=408.5$, whose energy
$\hbar H_0/8\pi$ is the energy per bit of the information--energy relation. Its fixed point is the Planck energy,
where the gravitational and quantum lengths cross, $r_s\lambda_C=2\ell_P^2$; the dark-energy scale sits three
quarters of the way down; and independently formed masses show no preference for rungs (GRUT \S50 \cite{GRUT2026}).

\subsection*{24.13\quad Now: where the horizon meets the final rate}
\sH\ The rotation of the horizon's phase (\S21.1) is held at a constant rate, and the one constant rate of the late universe
is the final expansion rate $H_\infty$; the rotation turns at $H_\infty$. \sD\ The lowest mode of the $C_3$ cycle on the
horizon (Section~\ref{sec:C3}) has frequency $c\sqrt{\lambda_1}=(3\sqrt3/2\pi)H(t)$, which falls as the universe expands, and it
meets the rotation's rate once:
\begin{equation}
\frac{3\sqrt3}{2\pi}\,H_{\rm now}=H_\infty,\qquad \Omega_\Lambda({\rm now})=\Big(\frac{H_\infty}{H_{\rm now}}\Big)^2=\frac{27}{4\pi^2}=0.6839 .
\label{eq:now}
\end{equation}
That moment is the present: the future's rate meeting the present horizon. It fixes when now is, $t_{\rm now}=0.786/H_\infty$,
and with the baryon ratio of the budget it gives $\Omega_\Lambda/\Omega_c=2.580$ in place of $5/2$, with the neutrinos counted inside $\Omega_m$
as everywhere in the budget (release 1.6.0 left them out and quoted $2.568$); the full budget is \S24.16. Passing through resonance turns
the relative phase by $\pi$, so the present is a sign flip of that phase, as the exchange of time and radius is at a black
hole's horizon and the gluing of one aeon's future to the next one's past is at the reset. Planck gives
$\Omega_\Lambda=0.6847\pm0.0073$, $0.1\sigma$ from~\eqref{eq:now}; the full-spectrum fit with the derived baryon ratio prefers
$2.611\pm0.024$, $1.3\sigma$ (GRUT \S36.7, \S25.11 \cite{GRUT2026}). The identification of the rotation's rate with $H_\infty$ was made with
the measured value in view and is recorded as a hypothesis, tested as the CMB and BAO sharpen.
\sD\ The constraint makes the rate a constant of the solution, so it can be read in the final state, where the only scale is
$H_\infty$. \sI\ The rate is the final horizon's own frequency, its surface gravity over $c$, $\kappa_\infty/c=H_\infty$: the period of its
thermal circle, with $T_\infty=\hbar H_\infty/2\pi k_B$, and the lowest conformal mode on its spatial sphere of radius $c/H_\infty$ both give
it (GRUT \S36.8 \cite{GRUT2026}). The equilibrium of the phase with the final horizon is the premise not yet derived from the
action. \sD\ It is derived up to an integer by the quantum of the spin, and the integer is measured to be one (\S24.15).

\subsection*{24.14\quad The spinning-arc principle}
\sD\ (A hypothesis in release 1.4.0; derived for the final horizon in \S24.15.) The horizon is not a static sphere: it is swept out by C-arcs spinning about perpendicular axes, vertical and
horizontal, crossing one another (the geometric origin of Section~\ref{sec:axiom}). The spin is physical. Nothing acts on the arcs, so their rate is fixed, and on a
horizon, a surface receding at the speed of light, the arcs' rims move at $c$:
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\omega R=c .}
\label{eq:spin-arc}
\end{equation}
\sD\ \emph{The cosmic sphere.} The universe's horizon grows toward the radius at which the fixed spin's rims reach light
speed, and no further: $R_\infty=c/\omega$, so $H_\infty=\omega$ and
\begin{equation}
\Lambda=\frac{3\omega^2}{c^2} .
\end{equation}
The cosmological constant is the signature of the spin. The internal rotation is the spin itself (I7, derived in \S24.15), so it turns at
$H_\infty$, with no equilibrium premise, and the present is the moment the current horizon's $C_3$ cycle has slowed to the
spin, $\Omega_\Lambda=27/4\pi^2$ (\S24.13).

\sD\ \emph{The wobble meets the spin.} The threefold wobble of the arcs, which creates the three generator defects
(Section~\ref{sec:axiom}; its count is the beat of \S2.4), is the horizon's $C_3$ mode: its frequency $(3\sqrt3/2\pi)\,c/R$ falls as the horizon grows, while the spin is fixed.
The present is the moment the wobble falls into step with the spin.

\emph{The spin is measured.} The birefringence of the CMB is the spin's phase accumulated since last scattering,
$\beta=\kappa\alpha\,\omega(t_0-t_{\rm rec})/2\pi$ (\S25.3), so the measured angle measures the spin: $0.277^\circ\pm0.057^\circ$
gives $\omega=(0.73\pm0.15)H_0$, against $H_\infty=0.827H_0$ from the expansion, $0.6\sigma$. Equivalently the twist of the oldest
light measures the cosmological constant, $\beta=(\kappa\alpha/2\pi)\sqrt{\Lambda c^2/3}\,(t_0-t_{\rm rec})$.

\sD\ \emph{The quantum sphere.} The same rule holds for a particle. A sphere swept at frequency $\omega$ has radius $c/\omega$;
with energy $\hbar\omega=mc^2$ that radius is $\hbar/mc$, the reduced Compton wavelength, and with the zero-point energy
$\hbar\omega/2$ it is half that. The internal clock de Broglie gave every particle, at $mc^2/\hbar$ \cite{deBroglie1925}, and the
light-speed trembling Schr\"odinger found in the electron's motion, at $2mc^2/\hbar$ and radius $\hbar/2mc$
\cite{Schrodinger1930,Hestenes1990}, are this rule at the smallest scale. From the electron to the edge of the universe
each closed sphere is swept by arcs whose rims move at light speed, and the Hubble quantum at the foot of the ladder,
$\hbar H_\infty$, is the cosmic sphere's quantum of spin (\S24.12).

\subsection*{24.15\quad The spin derived}
\sS\ In the final state the vacuum alone remains ($V_0=0$, \S21.3; $w=-1$), and the horizon is the de Sitter horizon of radius
$R_\infty=c/H_\infty$. With $\varrho$ the distance from a static observer, its thermal form, static time $T\to-i\tau$, is the
four-sphere of radius $R_\infty$ \cite{GibbonsHawking1977},
\begin{equation}
X_0+iX_4=\sqrt{R_\infty^2-\varrho^2}\;e^{iH_\infty\tau},\qquad \vec X=\varrho\,\hat n,\qquad X_0^2+X_4^2+|\vec X|^2=R_\infty^2 .
\end{equation}
\sD\ Static time is the angle of a turn, and the turn is the spinning-arc picture exactly. Each line of sight, from a horizon
point through the observer to the opposite horizon point, is a C-arc of radius $R_\infty$ spinning about the axis through its ends;
lines of sight at right angles spin about perpendicular axes, and all of them cross at the observer. A point at distance $\varrho$
moves at $c\sqrt{1-\varrho^2/R_\infty^2}$, the rate of a static clock there: light speed on the rim, which is the observer, and rest at
the horizon. At proper distance $\sigma$ from the horizon the plane of the turn is $\sigma^2(H_\infty d\tau)^2+d\sigma^2$, which closes
without a conical defect only for the period $2\pi/H_\infty$: smoothness where the arcs meet the horizon fixes the rate. Hence
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\omega=H_\infty,\qquad \omega R_\infty=c,\qquad \Lambda=\frac{3\omega^2}{c^2},\qquad k_BT_\infty=\frac{\hbar\omega}{2\pi} .}
\label{eq:spin-derived}
\end{equation}
\sS\ The zeroth law makes the rate the same over the whole horizon \cite{BCH1973}, and the Bianchi identity, with matter separately
conserved, forces $\partial_\nu\Lambda=0$; in unimodular gravity $\Lambda$ is the constant conjugate to the four-volume
\cite{HenneauxTeitelboim1989}. \sD\ Nothing in the action exchanges energy with the vacuum ($V_0=0$; the rotation's constraint adds
none, \S21.1), so the spin does not change. It is the boundary datum read as a rate, $\omega\lP/c=\lP/R_\infty=\sqrt{2\pi/N_\infty}
=9.66\times10^{-62}$ (\S26).

\emph{The charge.} \sS\ The entropy of a Killing horizon is $2\pi$ times the Noether charge of its Killing field at unit surface
gravity \cite{Wald1993}. \sD\ For a horizon of radius $R_H$ turning at its own rate $c/R_H$ that charge is the Hubble mass
carried around the horizon at light speed, $L=M_{\rm Hubble}\,cR_H=\hbar N/4\pi$, and
\begin{equation}
I_{\rm total}=\frac{2\pi L}{\hbar},\qquad E_{\rm total}=\frac{\omega L}{4}=\frac{\hbar\omega}{8\pi}\,I_{\rm total},\qquad \omega=\frac{c}{R_H}:
\label{eq:spin-charge}
\end{equation}
the information--energy relation~\eqref{eq:info_energy} is the energy of the turn, and its $8\pi$ is one turn, $2\pi$, shared over
the four cones of the closure, $M_{\rm Hubble}=4M_{\rm total}$ (\S24.3). \sS\ A body with mechanical spin $s$ has the lower entropy
bound $2\pi\sqrt{(ER/c)^2-s^2}/\hbar$ \cite{Hod2000}. \sD\ The horizon meets the Bekenstein bound with equality,
$2\pi E_{\rm Hubble}R_H/\hbar c=\pi c^5/\hbar GH_0^2=I_{\rm total}$ (\S24.2), so it cannot spin mechanically: its spin is the turn of its time, and the universe's rotation is internal (\S21.1). Read as a spin, the Bekenstein
bound is $S\le2\pi L_{\max}/\hbar$ with $L_{\max}=ER/c$, reached only on a rim moving at light speed; with $I=2\pi L/\hbar$ and
$E=\omega L$ the horizon saturates it exactly when $\omega R=c$.

\emph{The quantum.} \sD\ The turn is an angle of period $2\pi$, so its charge is $L=n\hbar$: $I=2\pi n$ nats, $N=4\pi n$, an area
step $8\pi\lP^2$ ($4\pi\lP^2$ of effective area, the surface of one Planck sphere, which carries $\hbar$), and $\hbar\omega$ per quantum.
\sS\ This is the spacing Bekenstein proposed \cite{Bekenstein1974} and Maggiore found in the quasinormal modes \cite{Maggiore2008};
here it is the quantum of the spin. The same holds for every horizon: for a black hole the turn's rate is $c^3/4GM$, $846$
rad\,s$^{-1}$ at $60\,M_\odot$, and the LIGO--Virgo ringdowns give the area quantum $15.6^{+20.5}_{-13.3}\,\lP^2$ \cite{Laghi2021},
$+0.5\sigma$ from $8\pi$. For a particle the rule $L=E/\omega$ gives $\hbar$ at the Compton radius and, at the zero-point energy
$mc^2=\tfrac12\hbar\omega$, $\hbar/2$ at the trembling radius $\hbar/2mc$ \cite{Hestenes1990}.

\emph{What it predicts.} \sI\ (I7) The phase of \S21.1 turns with the spin, $X_0=\Lambda/3$: one radian per $e$-fold of the final
expansion. The birefringence gives $\omega/H_\infty=0.88\pm0.18$.

\emph{The quantum of the rotation} \sD\ The quantum of the spin derives I7 up to an integer. On the rotation the
constraint vanishes, so the dust's energy is its Hamiltonian, $E=p_\theta\dot\theta=\Omega_0Q$, with $Q$ the momentum conjugate to the phase;
the phase is an angle, so $Q=n\hbar$ and the dust comes in quanta of rest energy $\hbar\Omega_0$, the Josephson--Anderson relation
\cite{Anderson1966}. \sS\ A comoving quantum carries a Killing energy equal to its rest energy, and every one but the observer's own leaves the
static patch; energy carried across the cosmological horizon raises its entropy by $\delta E/k_BT_\infty$ \cite{GibbonsHawking1977}. \sD\ That
entropy steps by $2\pi$, so the horizon takes up energy only in multiples of $\hbar\omega$, and
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\Omega_0=j\,H_\infty,\qquad X_0=j^2\,\frac{\Lambda}{3},\qquad j=1,2,3,\dots}
\label{eq:rot-quantum}
\end{equation}
\sM\ The birefringence, with $\kappa=6$, puts $j=1$ at $0.7\sigma$ and excludes $j=2$ at $6.2\sigma$ and $j=0$ at $4.9\sigma$; with the present
at the resonance, $\Omega_\Lambda$ gives $0.9994\pm0.0053$. So $j=1$: each quantum of the rotation is one quantum of the spin, at the floor mass
$\hbar/cR_\infty$, and I7 is derived, with the integer measured. The dust's density is the constraint's multiplier, which carries no gradient
energy, so the quantum sets no Jeans length and the dust stays cold on every scale. A scalar condensate in equilibrium with a rotating black
hole is locked the same way, $\omega=m\Omega_{\rm H}$ with $m$ an integer \cite{HerdeiroRadu2014} (GRUT \S36.14 \cite{GRUT2026}). \sP\ With the present at the resonance of \S24.13 the phase
turned since the beginning is $\theta_0=\tfrac23\,\mathrm{artanh}(3\sqrt3/2\pi)=0.7857$ rad ($0.78545$ with radiation; it lies within
$5\times10^{-5}$ of $\pi/4$, a coincidence noticed after the computation, with a chance of $0.15\%$), and the birefringence angle is
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\beta=\frac{2\alpha}{\pi}\,\mathrm{artanh}\,\frac{3\sqrt3}{2\pi}=0.3137^\circ ,}
\label{eq:beta-closed}
\end{equation}
$+0.6\sigma$ from $0.277^\circ\pm0.057^\circ$ \cite{Eskilt2026}, with no Hubble constant in it. The acceleration scale is set by the
cosmological constant alone, $\az=(\sqrt3/\pi^2)\,cH_{\rm now}=(2/3\pi)\,c^2\sqrt{\Lambda/3}$, $1.149\times10^{-10}\ \mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$ with
Planck's $\Lambda$: the spin fixes the factor of order one in Milgrom's $\az\approx c^2(\Lambda/3)^{1/2}$ \cite{Milgrom1999}
(GRUT \S36.10--36.13 \cite{GRUT2026}).

\subsection*{24.16\quad The budget at the resonance}
\sD\ The amount of the cosmic share is an integration constant of the rotation's constraint (\S21.1), and in a flat universe with a constant
vacuum $\Omega_\Lambda(t)=H_\infty^2/H(t)^2$ exactly: the constant decides when the resonance falls, not the budget there. With the rotation at the
spin (\S24.15) and the present at the resonance (\S24.13, the opening principle), $\Omega_\Lambda=27/4\pi^2$ whatever that constant is, and
flatness, the derived baryon ratio and the neutrino masses fix the rest. Computed with CAMB with the acoustic angle matched, on the scale of
the budgets of GRUT \S25.10:

\sP\ The resonance budget lies $1.3\sigma$ below the CMB's preferred ratio, $+0.7\sigma$ in $\omega_b$, $+0.2\sigma$ in $\omega_c$, and $+2.2\sigma$
and $-2.1\sigma$ from the DESI DR2 and DES-Y5 matter densities, where $\Lambda$CDM's own CMB fit lies; the material variances of the $5/2$
budget, the baryon density at $+2.5\sigma$ and DESI at $+3.0\sigma$, were the identification's. It gives $\eta=6.16\times10^{-10}$, $+0.5\sigma$ from the

and $73.4\%$ of the conformal time elapsed. \sD\ Hence I5 is withdrawn: the dark ratio is not the defect power ratio, whose value $5/2$ stays the
single-triple limit of the sky pattern (\S25). The value $2.568$ of release 1.6.0 left the neutrinos out of $\Omega_m$. The earlier numbers of this
version are quoted at the $5/2$ budget, so that each can be traced (GRUT \S25.11 \cite{GRUT2026}).

\subsection*{24.17\quad The acceleration scale tracks the horizon}
\sD\ The critical acceleration is one activation per period of the current horizon's lowest $C_3$ mode (I2, Section~\ref{sec:C3}), so
$\ac\propto H(t)$ and $\az(z)=\az E(z)$ with $E=H(z)/H_0$; the spin is fixed, and the two meet only at the resonance. The relations that
equate them, $\az=(2/3\pi)\,c^2\sqrt{\Lambda/3}$ and $\beta=\tfrac92\alpha\,\az t_0/c$, hold at the present, where they are tested, and the load
$z_H=\pi^2/2\sqrt3$ of the baryon ratio is a pure number only because $\az$ tracks $H$. \sM\ The radial acceleration relation of $79$
galaxies at $0.33<z<1.44$ \cite{Ciocan2026} has a scale rising from $1.99$ to $2.71\times10^{-10}\ \mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$ across four bins near
$z=0.47$--$1.27$, so a constant scale is excluded; $\az E(z)$ gives $1.49$--$2.40$. The data lie $13$--$33\%$ above and rise by $1.36$ where
$H(z)$ rises by $1.61$, with the survey's own fit extrapolating to $1.00\pm0.04$ at $z=0$. \sP\ A constant $\az$, or one off $\az E(z)$ at $5\sigma$
in a single method spanning $z=0$ to $1.5$, falsifies the reading (GRUT \S36.15 \cite{GRUT2026}).

\subsection*{24.18\quad The present in Planck cells}
\sH\ The resonance fixes the present in the expansion history (\S24.13); the horizon's size in Planck lengths is the framework's one boundary
datum (\S26). The hypothesis H4 of GRUT \S48.12 \cite{GRUT2026} fixes it by the electric coupling:
\begin{equation}
\ln\frac{R_H}{\lP}=\frac1\alpha+\ln8\pi+\frac{3\pi}{2}\,\alpha ,
\label{eq:trsd-present-cells}
\end{equation}
the horizon's solid angle over the double cover of the fold, and the three arcs times the pair's quarter turn at first order in $\alpha$:
the form of dimensional transmutation, $R_H=8\pi\lP e^{1/\alpha}$, with the coupling run through $1/\alpha$ e-folds from one Planck cell. It
selects the epoch and is not a law for $\alpha$: tied to the growing horizon, $\alpha$ would drift by $2.4\times10^{-13}\ \mathrm{yr^{-1}}$,
against $(1.0\pm1.1)\times10^{-18}\ \mathrm{yr^{-1}}$ from atomic clocks \cite{Lange2021}. \sP\ With CODATA's $\alpha$ it gives
$H_0=67.3765\pm0.0007$ (the error is $G$'s), $0.4\sigma$ from the resonance budget's CMB fit, $67.35\pm0.06$; with the resonance,
$\Lambda\lP^2=(81/256\pi^4)\,e^{-2/\alpha-3\pi\alpha}=2.843\times10^{-122}$ and $\rho_\Lambda/\rho_P=1.131\times10^{-123}$. The constants were read
after the value was known; H4 fails if $H_0$ settles away from $67.38$ at $5\sigma$ or if $\alpha$ varies (register row~38).

\section{The Conical Horizon on the CMB Sky}\label{sec:cmb}
% =====================================================================

\subsection*{25.1\quad Harmonic expansion}
\sD\ Model the three generator defects as point sources of potential on the equator,
$\hat n_j=(\pi/2,\,2\pi(j-1)/3)$, each weighted by $\dgen=\pi/3$:
$\Phi(\hat n)=A\sum_j\dgen\,\delta^{(2)}(\hat n-\hat n_j)$, with Sachs--Wolfe temperature
$\Delta T/T=-\Phi/3$. Then $b_{\ell m}=A\dgen\sum_jY^*_{\ell m}(\hat n_j)$, and
$\sum_je^{-im\phi_j}=3$ for $3\mid m$ and $0$ otherwise. The plane of the defects is
reflection-symmetric, and $Y_{\ell m}(\pi-\theta,\phi)=(-1)^{\ell+m}Y_{\ell m}(\theta,\phi)$,
so
\begin{equation}
b_{\ell m}=0\quad\text{unless}\quad m\equiv0\ (\mathrm{mod}\ 3)\ \text{ and }\ \ell+m\ \text{even}.
\label{eq:selection_rule}
\end{equation}
Summing the addition theorem over pairs of defects gives the closed form
\begin{equation}
C_\ell=\frac{1}{2\ell+1}\sum_m|b_{\ell m}|^2=\frac{3A^2\dgen^2}{4\pi}\Big[1+2P_\ell\big(-\tfrac12\big)\Big],
\end{equation}
so $C_1=0$, and with $P_2(-\tfrac12)=-\tfrac18$ and $P_3(-\tfrac12)=\tfrac7{16}$,
\begin{equation}
\frac{C_3}{C_2}=\frac{1+\tfrac78}{1-\tfrac14}=\frac52 .
\label{eq:C3C2_ratio}
\end{equation}
The quadrupole is pure $(2,0)$ and the octupole pure $(3,\pm3)$ in the defect frame. The
normalization $1/(2\ell+1)$ is essential: the bare amplitude ratio is
$|b_{33}|/|b_{20}|=\sqrt7/2$. Further ratios are $C_4/C_2=0.5625$, $C_5/C_2=1.0938$,
$C_6/C_2=2.1953$.

\subsection*{25.2\quad What the sky can test}
\sS\ For a Gaussian full sky the cosmic variance of $C_\ell$ is $\sqrt{2/(2\ell+1)}$, so the
ratio $C_3/C_2$ carries an irreducible $83\%$ ($1\sigma$) uncertainty. The observed ratio also
depends on the component-separation map and mask. The draft-1 comparison of the number
$2\pi^{\pi/3}/3+\tfrac1{12}=2.2940$ \sH\ with an observed $2.2887$ is therefore not a test
at the stated $0.23\%$: the sky constrains $C_3/C_2$ only to within a factor of about two.
The value $5/2$ is a property of the assumed three-point source configuration; it becomes an
observational prediction only after projection through the radiation transfer function, which
is not yet done.

The selection rule~\eqref{eq:selection_rule} is testable. About an axis $\hat n$, the share of
power in the admissible modes has, under isotropy, mean
$f_\ell=|\mathcal M_\ell|/(2\ell+1)=0.111,\,0.182,\,0.231,\,0.133,\,0.176,\,0.211,\,0.143$
for $\ell=4,\dots,10$; a defect component of fraction $\varepsilon$ raises it to
$\varepsilon+(1-\varepsilon)f_\ell$. The parity-odd admissible set is an internal null
channel. \sM\ The search at $\ell=4$--$10$ about the octopole axis on the Planck Commander map
returned no excess over $f_\ell$: $\varepsilon$ is consistent with zero, so by criterion F7
the point-source imprint is not detected. The pattern after projection through the radiation
transfer function---smoothed, not point-like---is the next target.

\subsection*{25.3\quad Handedness}
For a real sky $a_{\ell,-m}=(-1)^ma_{\ell m}^*$, so $|a_{3,3}|=|a_{3,-3}|$ in every frame. The
draft 1 handedness prediction $|a_{3,+3}|\neq|a_{3,-3}|$ is impossible and is withdrawn. A handed
structure appears instead in the phase of $a_{33}$ and in the parity-odd $EB$ correlation,
where the Planck data show a cosmic birefringence angle $\beta=0.34^\circ\pm0.09^\circ$
\cite{EskiltKomatsu2022}; \sD\ in the rotation reading its size is
$\beta=\kappa\alpha\,\Omega_0(t_0-t_{\rm rec})/2\pi=0.052^\circ\kappa$, with $\kappa=\sum N_cQ^2=3+3=6$ the anomaly coefficient of the
two cone triples, the charged leptons and $(c,b,t)$: $\beta=0.312^\circ$, $0.6\sigma$ above the joint value
$0.277^\circ\pm0.057^\circ$ \cite{Eskilt2026}, and its sign is the sense of the rotation (GRUT \S49.12).
\sD\ The rotation's rate is also the acceleration scale's: $a_c=c\,\Omega_0/2\pi$ and $a_0=\tfrac43a_c$, so
$\Omega_0=(3\pi/2)\,a_0/c$, and eliminating it,
\begin{equation}
\beta=\frac{3\kappa\alpha}{4}\,\frac{a_0t_0}{c}=\frac{9\alpha}{2}\,\frac{a_0t_0}{c}\qquad(\kappa=6).
\label{eq:beta-a0}
\end{equation}
Galaxy rotation curves predict the polarization rotation of the CMB: the radial acceleration relation's
$a_0=(1.20\pm0.24)\times10^{-10}\ \mathrm{m\,s^{-2}}$ \cite{McGaugh2016} with $t_0=13.80$ Gyr gives
$\beta=0.33^\circ\pm0.07^\circ$, $0.6\sigma$ from the measured angle. Milgrom's coincidence $2\pi a_0\approx cH_0$
\cite{Milgrom1983} ties $a_0$ to the expansion; this ties it, through $\alpha$, to the polarization of the
oldest light (GRUT \S49.13). With the rate set by the spin and the present at the resonance, the angle takes the closed form
$\beta=(2\alpha/\pi)\,\mathrm{artanh}(3\sqrt3/2\pi)=0.3137^\circ$ (\S24.15).
