\section{Parameter Structure}\label{sec:parameters}
% =====================================================================
\begin{theorem}[Input counting]\label{thm:counting}
\sD\ A theory whose primitives are dimensionless---angles, incidence counts, ratios---determines
no dimensionful constant. The unit-rescaling group of mechanics is three-dimensional, so three
dimensionful anchors are conventions and carry no predictive content.
\end{theorem}
\begin{proof}
The rescalings of mass, length and time form $(\mathbb R_{>0})^3$; the primitives are invariant,
so every function of them is, and no dimensionful constant is. Fixing three independent
dimensionful quantities fixes the units; by Buckingham's theorem every other quantity is then a
pure number times a monomial in them.
\end{proof}
The triple $(c,\hbar,R_H)$ spans the units: length $R_H$, time $R_H/c$, mass $\hbar/cR_H$. The
remaining input is the pure number $N=2\pi R_H^2/\lP^2=2\pi c^5/\hbar GH_0^2$, which enters only
through $\lP$: $G$, $\lP$, $\mP$ and $F$ carry powers of $N$, and no prediction of the gravity
law, the budget or the selection rule does. $N$ is a boundary datum, measured, not fitted. The
closure $F/M_{\rm Hubble}\az=\pi^2/2\sqrt3=2.8491$ holds for every $N$, so the horizon
quantities cannot determine $N$; a condition from outside---plausibly at the reset of
\S29---would be needed.

% =====================================================================
\section{Discussion}\label{sec:discussion}
% =====================================================================
\textbf{MOND and AQUAL.} MOND \cite{Milgrom1983a} and AQUAL \cite{BekensteinMilgrom1984} take
$\mu$ and $\az$ as inputs. TRSD derives both, and with $H_0$ from the CMB the prediction meets
the SPARC relation with no parameter (\S14).

\textbf{Covariant completion.} TeVeS \cite{Bekenstein2004} and bimetric MOND
\cite{Milgrom2009bimetric} are covariant and add fields. TRSD's gravity law is the
nonrelativistic AQUAL form and TRSD does not yet supply covariant field equations; lensing,
the post-Newtonian parameters, cosmological perturbations, gravitational waves and
energy--momentum conservation therefore remain open. \sD\ A completion must (1) reduce to
\eqref{eq:aqual} with $\mu_n$ in the window in the quasistatic weak field; (2) lens by the same potential that
governs dynamics; (3) propagate tensor modes at $c$, as GW170817 requires \cite{GW170817};
(4) give $\gamma=\beta=1$ in the solar system at the Cassini level; (5) conserve
energy--momentum through the Bianchi identity; (6) reproduce the CMB and matter spectra at
linear order; and (7) be free of ghost and gradient instabilities. Such theories exist for
other interpolation functions: AeST meets (1)--(3), (6) and (7) \cite{SkordisZlosnik2021}. GRUT's
completion, general relativity with the conjugate-pair scalar held at its rotation rate by $\lambda(X-X_0)$,
meets (2)--(6): minimally coupled, it propagates tensor modes at $c$, conserves energy--momentum through
the Bianchi identity, lenses by the dynamical potential for pressureless sources, reduces to general
relativity where no medium is bound, as in the Solar System, and evolves as cold dust in cosmology; (7)
holds at linear order; and (1) follows for bound systems from the polarization law of the bound medium (\sI).

\textbf{Emergent gravity.} Verlinde's construction gives $\az=cH_0/6=0.1667\,cH_0$
\cite{Verlinde2017}; TRSD gives $0.1755\,cH_0$. The two differ by $5\%$, inside the present
$\pm20\%$ systematic of galaxies; a measurement of $\az$ to $2\%$ with $H_0$ fixed would
separate them.

\textbf{Clusters and the CMB.} Phantom mass alone does not account for the mass of clusters
\cite{Clowe2006} or the third acoustic peak; the condensate is the clustering component, and its
share is fixed by the budget. In the rotation reading the clustering component is spacetime excited, geodesic and collisionless, and what
lensing infers as dark matter is its dilation, $\mathcal S=1/\cos_n\theta$; in the Bullet Cluster the dilation passed through with the galaxies
(\S21.7).

\textbf{Open problems.} The absolute value of $N$; the covariant completion; the CMB spectrum
from the defect sector with transfer functions; the dark-sector length $L_*$ from the theory;
and the external-field test of $\mu_2$.

\section{Conclusions}\label{sec:conclusions}
% =====================================================================
\begin{enumerate}\itemsep1pt
\item \sD\ The $C_3$ eigenvalue and the isotropic slope $\tfrac34$ give $\az=(\sqrt3/\pi^2)cH_0$,
independent of the exposure law.
\item \sD\ A Poisson threshold response gives the family $\mu_n$ in closed form; the deep slope
$\tfrac34$ holds for every exposure law with finite mean, and the linear hazard is required by
the Tully--Fisher slope.
\item \sM\ The full likelihood of 153 rotation curves with 490 nuisance parameters gives
$\az=1.04\pm0.04\pm0.10$ across the allowed shapes; the prediction $1.148$ is $1.0\sigma$ high. On
the RAR points, with nothing fitted, every member meets the relation to $0.025$ dex in the mean.
\item \sDS\ Galaxies fix the transition and planets the tail. The one-exponent Torridity form
cannot meet both for any exponent, and draft 1's disk-galaxy exponent $2\pi/3$ is excluded; the
threshold family meets both for $1.62\le n\le1.91$, and the draft-1 screening is retired.
\item \sD\ Canonical normalization gives $\epsV=\fd^2/L_*^2$, $\mdelta=\sqrt8\hbar/cL_*$, a vanishing
vacuum constant and a gravitational-strength self-coupling $3\hbar^3/2\mP^2c$.
\item \sD\ The horizon mass is the closure mass of four tetrahedral deficits of $\pi$, and its
dual is a field of mass $(\sqrt2/2\pi)\hbar H_0/c^2$.
\item \sP\ The resonance budget gives $\Omega_m=0.316$ and $H_0=67.35\pm0.06$, within $1\sigma$ of Planck's
densities and of Pantheon+, $2.2\sigma$ from DESI DR2 BAO and $2.1\sigma$ from DES-Y5 (where $\Lambda$CDM's own CMB fit lies), and
predicts that distance ladders calibrated alike converge on $67.3$.
\item \sM\ The CMB point-source imprint is not detected at $\ell=4$--$10$; the smoothed pattern is
next.
\item \sD\ The spinning arcs are the thermal geometry of the final horizon: the rate $H_\infty$ is fixed by smoothness at the
horizon, the rim moves at $c$, the information is $2\pi$ times the spin's charge over $\hbar$, and the quantum of the spin is the
area step $8\pi\lP^2$; \sP\ with the present at the resonance, $\beta=(2\alpha/\pi)\,\mathrm{artanh}(3\sqrt3/2\pi)=0.3137^\circ$, $+0.6\sigma$.
\item \sD\ The rotation's quanta are the spin's: $\Omega_0=jH_\infty$ with $j=1$ measured. \sP\ With the present at the resonance the dark ratio is
$2.580$ and the budget lies within $1\sigma$ of Planck's densities; the identification $5/2$ is withdrawn. The acceleration scale tracks the
horizon, $\az(z)=\az E(z)$.
\item \sP\ Counted in cycles, cosmic dawn is fixed by the CMB's twist, $H_\infty t_\star=(\pi/3)\beta$, with no clock and no $\alpha$: the measured
angle places the 21-cm onset at $42.7^{+5.6}_{-6.0}$ MHz.
\item \sD\ The dark sector is one geometry: spacetime unexcited, the vacuum, which is the generator-defect tension over the square of the
final radius, and spacetime excited, the cosmic share. Its equation of state is $w=-1+\Theta$ with $\Theta$ the excited fraction, equality at
$z=0.372$. What lensing infers as dark matter is the dilation, $\mathcal S=1/\cos_n\theta$ with $\tan\theta=\az/g$: $1$ at the centre, $r/\ell$
toward the edge, varying with capture and relaxation; in the Bullet Cluster it passed through with the galaxies. \sO\ How far it grows is
open. The background expansion is unchanged. The dilation keeps a clock's causes: it reaches the unbound web once the horizon sets its
clocks, at the onset of acceleration, holds above $68$ Mpc, and acts on clocks, not light; the web then carries the measured bulk flows with
$S_8$, cosmic shear and CMB lensing unchanged, while the Hubble tension stays the background's.
\end{enumerate}

\section{Cosmic Reset and the Complete Ending}\label{sec:cosmic_reset}\label{sec:complete_ending}
% =====================================================================
This section is \sH\ throughout: a proposed end state and transition, not yet derived from the
field equations.

\textbf{Terminal state.} With $V_0=0$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:V0}) the potential is
$\tfrac12(\Xii-2)^2$ with zero vacuum energy; the vacuum energy is carried by the horizon defect.
If the dark-sector length tracks the horizon in the terminal phase, $L_*(t)=R_H(t)=c/H(t)$, then
$\mdelta=\sqrt8\,\hbar/cR_H\to0$ as $H\to0$, the condensate sits at $\Xii=2$, and every remaining
degree of freedom is massless.

\textbf{Conformal boundary.} \sS\ A massless scalar with the coupling $-\tfrac1{12}R\chi^2$ and a
quartic term is classically conformally invariant in four dimensions. Near a boundary where
$g_{\mu\nu}=\Omega^2\eta_{\mu\nu}$ with $\Omega\to0$, the Einstein--Hilbert term scales as
$\Omega^2$. The trace anomaly, $\langle T^\mu{}_\mu\rangle=(16\pi^2)^{-1}(c_WC^2-a_EE_4)$ with
$c_W=1/120$ and $a_E=1/360$ for one conformal scalar, breaks the symmetry at the quantum level;
through $R=-8\pi G\langle T^\mu{}_\mu\rangle/c^4$ it sources curvature, the scalar acquires
$m_{\rm eff}^2=R/6$, and time dependence produces particles.

\textbf{Seed of matter.} Photon collisions produce pairs: nonlinear Breit--Wheeler production was
observed at SLAC E-144 \cite{Burke1997}, and the linear process in ultraperipheral heavy-ion
collisions by STAR at RHIC \cite{STAR2021}.

\textbf{Defects and closure.} In $3{+}1$ dimensions a point mass does not produce a conical
deficit; strings and the reduced $2{+}1$ geometry do (\S16). A closed spatial section requires the
deficits to sum to $4\pi$ (Theorem~\ref{thm:closure}), realized minimally by four $C_3$ apexes of
$\pi$. The condensate of the defect network is described by the action~\eqref{eq:action}.

\textbf{Gluing.} Following Penrose's conformal cyclic cosmology \cite{Penrose2010}, the future
boundary of one aeon is identified with the past boundary of the next,
$\tilde g^{(\rm next)}_{\mu\nu}=\Omega^2g^{(\rm old)}_{\mu\nu}$.

\textbf{Finiteness.} The horizon entropy is $S=N/2$ nats, so the number of distinguishable boundary
configurations is $e^{N/2}=2^{N/2\ln2}$ (draft 1 wrote $2^N$), finite and astronomically large.

\section{Completed Thermodynamic and Geometric Reset Closure}
\label{sec:reset_thermo_closure}
% =====================================================================
The reset is a closed thermodynamic--geometric transition when the following distinction is kept exact: the
old aeon does not undergo a global contraction of its physical scale factor. Before the junction,
$a=b^{-1}$ and $b\to0^+$, so $a\to\infty$; after the junction the new physical scale factor is
$\tilde a=|b|\to0^+$. Thus the reset is not a global compressive shock of the old aeon. It is a conformal
identification at which the old physical scale loses its operational meaning and the new aeon begins with a
vanishing physical scale. This is the geometry already fixed by~\eqref{eq:junc-metrics-closed} and
$\tilde H=1/(2\tilde\tau)$.

\subsection*{R1. Matter-sector conservation and thermodynamic closure}
On every regular matter branch let $N^\mu$ denote a conserved matter-number current and $T^{\mu\nu}_{\rm m}$
the total matter stress tensor. In the absence of a localized junction source, the finite-flux conditions are
\begin{equation}
 \nabla_\mu N^\mu=0,
 \qquad
 \nabla_\mu T^{\mu\nu}_{\rm m}=0,
 \qquad
 \boxed{[N^\mu n_\mu]_-^+=0},
 \qquad
 \boxed{[T^{\mu\nu}_{\rm m}n_\nu]_-^+=0} .
\label{eq:reset-matter-flux}
\end{equation}
These equations express material and energy--momentum continuity without requiring that every old composite
object survive as the same bound state. Stars, galaxies and black holes are not conserved objects; their conserved
charges and stress--energy are redistributed among the degrees of freedom of the thermal state.

Thermalization is represented by the entropy current $s^\mu$. The second law gives
\begin{equation}
 \nabla_\mu s^\mu=\sigma_s\ge0 .
\label{eq:reset-entropy-production}
\end{equation}
Consequently the reset may destroy macroscopic structure while preserving conserved currents and increasing
coarse-grained entropy. The radiation-dominated opening state is therefore a high-entropy thermal state, but it
is not identified with exact global maximum entropy or exact equilibrium: GRUT's reset is explicitly out of equilibrium
and its subsequent cooling and baryon asymmetry calculation rely on that fact.

For the relativistic plasma stage,
\begin{equation}
 p=\frac13\rho c^2,
 \qquad \rho=a_R T^4,
 \qquad s=\frac{4\rho}{3T},
 \qquad \tilde H=\frac1{2\tilde\tau},
\label{eq:reset-radiation-closure}
\end{equation}
so the new aeon has the required radiation-like equation of state and cools continuously as it expands. The
thermodynamic closure is therefore thermalization plus conservation plus the entropy inequality, not a postulated
instantaneous equilibrium.

\subsection*{R2. The collisionless phase survives the reset}
The phase sector is governed by the already closed junction theorem,
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\mathcal T_\chi=1},
 \qquad
 \boxed{\mathcal M_{\mathcal R}(k)=1\ \text{for the adiabatic superhorizon mode}},
 \qquad
 \boxed{\mathcal M_{\mathcal I}(k)=1} .
\label{eq:reset-transport-summary}
\end{equation}
Thus the phase current crosses the conformal junction without a junction-localized source, and the specified
superhorizon curvature correlations and conformal information modes are transmitted unchanged. The Bullet Cluster
provides the intended local dynamical analogy: collisional plasma is shock-heated while the gravitating component
is much less affected by drag. It is an analogy for the separation of collisional and collisionless sectors, not a
separate derivation of the cosmological junction.

\subsection*{R3. No additional Stückelberg transition is required}
The reset does not invoke a Stückelberg mass cancellation or restoration of a gauge $U(1)$. In the existing framework,
the dark-sector phase $\theta$ is a global phase/clock variable and carries no gauge field. The transition is instead
the conformal continuation
\begin{equation}
 g_{\mu\nu}=b^{-2}\hat g_{\mu\nu}
 \quad\longrightarrow\quad
 \tilde g_{\mu\nu}=b^2\hat g_{\mu\nu},
 \qquad b(\eta_c)=0,
\label{eq:reset-geometric-transition}
\end{equation}
with the same regular conformal geometry and $b'(\eta_c)=-\omega$. No new vector mass or gauge-symmetry-breaking
sector is introduced by the reset.

\subsection*{R4. Recombination and the next structure hierarchy}
After the reset, $\tilde a$ grows, the radiation density falls as $\tilde a^{-4}$, and the baryonic component cools.
The transferred adiabatic phase correlations provide the initial gravitational seed through
$\mathcal M_{\mathcal R}=1$. Baryons subsequently respond to the resulting gravitational potentials and, after
neutral recombination, decouple from the radiation and participate in structure formation.

The statement that the \emph{same phase correlations} seed the next aeon is therefore a closed consequence of the
junction transport equations. The stronger statement that every individual baryon or bound object in the old aeon
is literally the same particle in the new aeon is not part of the conserved-field description and is not required by
the framework. In particular, GRUT's baryogenesis sector permits baryon number violation through electroweak
anomaly processes while conserving the appropriate anomaly-free combination; material continuity must therefore be
understood as continuity of the conserved field degrees of freedom and fluxes, not identity of every composite particle.

The CMB then has its standard role within the framework's sequence: the radiation field evolves through the
hot plasma era and the observed microwave background is the relic radiation released as the baryonic plasma
recombines. Structure formation occurs later from the inherited primordial perturbations and the subsequent
matter dynamics. No independent creation of an abstract information carrier is required: the conformal information
channel and phase correlations are transported by the already derived junction map, while the horizon-cell count
itself resets according to $N_\infty\to0\to N_\infty$.

\subsection*{R5. Completed reset statement}
The physical reset is therefore a single closed sequence:
\begin{equation}
\boxed{
\text{terminal dilution}\;\to\;
\text{conformal junction}\;\to\;
\text{radiation-dominated new aeon}\;\to\;
\text{cooling and recombination}\;\to\;
\text{structure formation}}
\end{equation}
with phase-current transmission, adiabatic curvature transmission and conformal information transmission fixed
by their stated junction assumptions. Separately, the finite conformal-volume remainder of Auxoviscausal strain
matches by~\eqref{eq:aux-junction-match} when the common conformal spatial metric and identified flow tube are regular;
the raw logarithmic strain does not. The reset is thermodynamically irreversible at the coarse-grained level through
$\nabla_\mu s^\mu\ge0$, while the specified conformal transport channels are lossless. This is the scope of the
thermodynamic and geometric reset closure used by TRSD/GRUT.

% =====================================================================
\section{The Four Interactions from the Coil}\label{sec:unification}
% =====================================================================
\sD\ The coil of \S2 has four features, and each carries one interaction (GRUT \S54 \cite{GRUT2026}): the cone of the tangent bicone,
with its deficit (\S3.3, \S16), carries gravity, of strength $G=c^4/F$; the phase of the three crossings, $\mathbb Z_3\subset U(1)$ made
continuous and acting on the fermions, carries electromagnetism, of strength $\alpha$; the two conjugate lobes, glued into $S^3\cong SU(2)$ and
of opposite handedness, carry the weak interaction and its chirality; the three crossings of the beat (\S2.4), the triangle $C_3$, carry
colour, $SU(3)$ with centre $\mathbb Z_3$. The phase $\theta$ of $\phi_\pm=\phi e^{\pm i\theta}$ is a different, global phase: held at its rate it
is the rotation that carries the dark sector (\S21.1), and it carries no gauge field (GRUT \S31.1, \S41.7). The lobes' $\mathbb Z_2$ and the
beat's $\mathbb Z_3$ generate the quotient
\begin{equation}
G_{\rm SM}=\frac{SU(3)\times SU(2)\times U(1)}{\mathbb Z_6},\qquad \frac t3+T+Y\in\mathbb Z ,
\label{eq:trsd-Z6}
\end{equation}
for every field of triality $t$, isospin $T$ and hypercharge $Y$ \cite{Tong2017}: every colour singlet has integer charge, and quarks
carry $\tfrac23$ and $-\tfrac13$.

\sD\ The features write one action: the axiom~\eqref{eq:action} with the rotation held at its rate, the Yang--Mills terms of the
group~\eqref{eq:trsd-Z6}, three generations of fermions on the cone's double cover, and the pair as one Higgs doublet (GRUT \S54.5). Its content
fixes the running, $b_3=-7$, $b_2=-\tfrac{19}6$ and $b_Y=\tfrac{41}6$ at one loop, from three colours, three generations and one doublet (GRUT
\S54.6): the triangle's coupling grows toward low energy and closes in strong coupling at the nucleon, and the phase and the pair are one
electroweak coupling, $\alpha_w=\alpha/\sin^2\theta_W$.

\sD\ The strengths lie on one chain of scales. With H4 (\S24.18) the horizon is $8\pi e^{1/\alpha+3\pi\alpha/2}$ Planck lengths, the balance
mass of GRUT \S50.8 has $(m_*/m_{\rm P})^3=\lP/2R_H$, and the nucleon is $m_p=Km_*$, $K=19.7566\pm0.0002$, the strong datum. Then
\begin{equation}
\frac{G\hbar H_0^2}{c^5}=\frac{e^{-2/\alpha-3\pi\alpha}}{64\pi^2},\qquad \frac{Gm_p^2}{\hbar c}=\frac{K^2}{(16\pi)^{2/3}}\,e^{-2/3\alpha-\pi\alpha}
=5.906\times10^{-39}:
\label{eq:trsd-hierarchy}
\end{equation}
gravity's weakness is exponential in $1/\alpha$, because the present is $e^{1/\alpha}$ Planck lengths across. Both rungs are transmutations from
one Planck cell, the horizon of the electric coupling (H4) and the nucleon of the strong coupling with $b_0=7$, $m_p\propto
m_{\rm P}e^{-2\pi/7\alpha_s(m_{\rm P})}$. Two strengths are fixed by the geometry, $\alpha$ and $G$ at the present; the strong and electroweak
strengths are each one number at the closure of their feature, the nucleon's $K$ and the pair's $v_{\rm EW}$ and $\theta_W$, measured today and
the next closures to compute (GRUT \S54.7).

\sP\ The group~\eqref{eq:trsd-Z6} is a product, not a subgroup of a simple group: there is no scale at which the couplings must meet and no
gauge boson that turns quarks into leptons, so the proton is stable against gauge-mediated decay, which Super-Kamiokande bounds at
$\tau/B(p\to e^+\pi^0)>2.4\times10^{34}$ yr \cite{SuperK2020}. Gauge-mediated proton decay, or a free fractionally charged colour singlet,
falsifies the section (register rows 39--40).

% =====================================================================
\section{Collision Lag, Geometric Recoil, and CHIRP}
\label{sec:chirp-recoil}
% =====================================================================

This section derives the collision--relaxation chain from the existing TRSD action, merger map,
metric equations, and horizon definitions. The key distinction is between (i) the displacement of
the excited component relative to collisional baryons and (ii) the expansion of a proper spatial
volume. They are connected by the phase current and the metric, but they are not the same velocity.
No external streaming-velocity amplitude, new force, or additional matter component is introduced.

\subsection*{1. Collision separates baryonic motion from phase motion}

The existing constrained phase sector is
\begin{equation}
S_{\rm rot}=\int d^4x\sqrt{-g}\,\lambda(X-X_0),\qquad
X=-g^{\mu\nu}\nabla_\mu\theta\nabla_\nu\theta=X_0.
\label{eq:trsd-chirp-action}
\end{equation}
Variation with respect to $\theta$ gives $\nabla_\mu(\lambda\nabla^\mu\theta)=0$; variation with
respect to the metric gives
\begin{equation}
T^{(\chi)}_{\mu\nu}=2\lambda\nabla_\mu\theta\nabla_\nu\theta
+g_{\mu\nu}\lambda(X-X_0)
\xrightarrow{X=X_0}2\lambda\nabla_\mu\theta\nabla_\nu\theta.
\end{equation}
With $u_\mu=\nabla_\mu\theta/\sqrt{X_0}$, the fixed-norm condition gives $u^\mu u_\mu=-1$ and
$u^\nu\nabla_\nu u_\mu=0$. The phase component therefore follows geodesics and has no gas-shock
drag term. In the non-relativistic limit its mass current obeys
\begin{equation}
\partial_t\rho_\chi+\nabla\cdot(\rho_\chi\mathbf v_\chi)=0,\qquad
(\partial_t+\mathbf v_\chi\cdot\nabla)\mathbf v_\chi=-\nabla\Phi.
\label{eq:trsd-chirp-dust}
\end{equation}
The collisional baryonic gas instead obeys
\begin{equation}
(\partial_t+\mathbf v_b\cdot\nabla)\mathbf v_b
=-\nabla\Phi-\frac{\nabla p_b}{\rho_b}+\mathbf a_{\rm coll}.
\end{equation}
Writing $\mathbf w=\mathbf v_\chi-\mathbf v_b$ and subtracting while retaining the distinct
convective derivatives gives
\begin{equation}
\boxed{(\partial_t+\mathbf v_\chi\cdot\nabla)\mathbf w
=\frac{\nabla p_b}{\rho_b}-\mathbf a_{\rm coll}
-(\mathbf w\cdot\nabla)\mathbf v_b.}
\label{eq:trsd-chirp-relative}
\end{equation}
The common gravitational acceleration cancels at the same event. Thus the gas can lose bulk
momentum in a shock while the phase component continues on its own geodesic; the previous phase
density and its stress-energy are not algebraically erased by a change in the baryonic trajectory.
The gas terms are those already present in its fluid solution, not fitted velocity inputs.

\subsection*{2. The existing relaxation law derives the component drift}

At core passage, the framework resets the relaxation clock. With $t=\Delta t\geq0$,
\begin{equation}
f(t)=1-e^{-t/t_{\rm rel}},\qquad q(t)=1-f=e^{-t/t_{\rm rel}},\qquad
\dot f=\frac{q}{t_{\rm rel}},\quad \dot q=-\frac{q}{t_{\rm rel}}.
\label{eq:trsd-chirp-relax}
\end{equation}
The existing merger map is
\begin{equation}
\rho_\chi(\mathbf x,t)=q\rho_{\rm inf}(\mathbf x,t)+f\rho_{\rm pol}(\mathbf x,t).
\label{eq:trsd-chirp-rhomap}
\end{equation}
For equal conserved masses in the normalized profiles, let their centroids be $\mathbf x_{\rm inf}$
and $\mathbf x_{\rm pol}$, and define $\mathbf D=\mathbf x_{\rm pol}-\mathbf x_{\rm inf}$. The
mixture centroid is $\mathbf x_\chi=q\mathbf x_{\rm inf}+f\mathbf x_{\rm pol}$, hence
$\Delta\mathbf x_\chi\equiv\mathbf x_\chi-\mathbf x_{\rm inf}=f\mathbf D$. Differentiating gives
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\mathbf v_{\rm rel}=\frac{d\Delta\mathbf x_\chi}{dt}
=\frac{q}{t_{\rm rel}}\mathbf D+f\dot{\mathbf D}.}
\label{eq:trsd-chirp-centroid}
\end{equation}
For approximately fixed $\mathbf D$,
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\mathbf v_{\rm rel}=\frac{e^{-t/t_{\rm rel}}}{t_{\rm rel}}\mathbf D,\qquad
\Delta\mathbf x_\chi=(1-e^{-t/t_{\rm rel}})\mathbf D.}
\label{eq:trsd-chirp-centroid-fixed}
\end{equation}
This velocity is derived from the existing displacement and relaxation time; no streaming-speed
amplitude is inserted. The framework already fixes
$t_{\rm rel}=4\pi^2/[9\sqrt3 H(z)]$. For the Bullet Cluster, $t_{\rm rel}\simeq31.4$ Gyr and
$f(0.15\,\mathrm{Gyr})\simeq0.0048$, so the relaxation displacement is $0.48\%$ of $D$ at that
epoch. This is a centroid drift of the excess distribution, not yet the velocity of space itself.
Substitution of the framework's own horizon-set clock gives the closed form, for fixed $\mathbf D$,
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\mathbf v_{\rm rel}(t)=\frac{9\sqrt3}{4\pi^2}H(z)\exp\!\left[-\frac{9\sqrt3}{4\pi^2}H(z)t\right]\mathbf D.}
\end{equation}
The velocity scale is therefore derived from the framework's $H(z)$, its fixed relaxation law, and the existing separation $\mathbf D$; it is not an externally supplied streaming amplitude.

\subsection*{3. Mass conservation fixes the local relaxation flux}

Differentiate the density mixture and use the continuity equations for each profile,
$\partial_t\rho_a+\nabla\cdot(\rho_a\mathbf v_a)=0$ for $a\in\{\rm inf,pol\}$. Directly,
\begin{align}
\partial_t\rho_\chi+\nabla\cdot(q\rho_{\rm inf}\mathbf v_{\rm inf}
+f\rho_{\rm pol}\mathbf v_{\rm pol})
&=\dot q\rho_{\rm inf}+\dot f\rho_{\rm pol}\\
&=\dot f(\rho_{\rm pol}-\rho_{\rm inf}).
\end{align}
Therefore the transfer flux required by mass conservation obeys
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\partial_t\rho_\chi+\nabla\cdot\mathbf J_\chi=0,\quad
\mathbf J_\chi=q\rho_{\rm inf}\mathbf v_{\rm inf}
+f\rho_{\rm pol}\mathbf v_{\rm pol}+\mathbf J_{\rm rel},\quad
\nabla\cdot\mathbf J_{\rm rel}=-\dot f(\rho_{\rm pol}-\rho_{\rm inf}).}
\label{eq:trsd-chirp-transfer}
\end{equation}
Under spherical symmetry, regularity at $r=0$ fixes the radial component:
\begin{equation}
\boxed{J_{{\rm rel},r}(r,t)=-\frac{\dot f}{r^2}
\int_0^r[\rho_{\rm pol}(s,t)-\rho_{\rm inf}(s,t)]s^2\,ds.}
\label{eq:trsd-chirp-transfer-radial}
\end{equation}
Define the relaxation part of the coarse-grained transport velocity by
$v_{{\rm rel},r}=J_{{\rm rel},r}/\rho_\chi$. Its local divergence is then not guessed but follows
from the product rule:
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\vartheta_{\rm rel}\equiv\nabla\cdot\mathbf v_{\rm rel}
=-\frac{\dot f(\rho_{\rm pol}-\rho_{\rm inf})}{\rho_\chi}
-\mathbf v_{\rm rel}\cdot\nabla\ln\rho_\chi.}
\label{eq:trsd-chirp-transfer-div}
\end{equation}
The sign is determined by the existing profiles and their enclosed-mass difference. Mass conservation
allows outward transport in some regions and inward transport in others; it does not imply a positive
uniform expansion by itself. Compatibility with the phase action requires the total current
$\mathbf J_\chi=\rho_\chi\mathbf v_\chi$ to satisfy the geodesic Euler equation~\eqref{eq:trsd-chirp-dust}.
That equation is the consistency check, not an extra adjustable velocity law.

\subsection*{4. Geometric recoil is the trace of spatial deformation}

Let $h_{ij}$ be the proper spatial metric on slices orthogonal to the phase flow. The expansion tensor
is $B_{ij}=\tfrac12\mathcal L_u h_{ij}$; its symmetric decomposition is
$B_{ij}=\sigma_{ij}+\tfrac13\vartheta h_{ij}$, where $\vartheta=\nabla_\mu u^\mu$. A connecting
separation $\xi^i$ between neighboring flow lines obeys
\begin{equation}
\frac{D\xi^i}{D\tau}=B^i{}_j\xi^j,\qquad
\frac{d\ln V_{\rm proper}}{d\tau}=\operatorname{tr}B=\vartheta.
\label{eq:trsd-chirp-metric-volume}
\end{equation}
The volume identity follows from differentiating the determinant of three independent connecting
vectors: $d\ln\det(\xi^i{}_a)/d\tau=\operatorname{tr}B$. Therefore
\begin{equation}
\boxed{V_{\rm proper}(\tau)=V_{\rm proper}(\tau_0)
\exp\!\left[\int_{\tau_0}^{\tau}\vartheta(\tau')\,d\tau'\right].}
\label{eq:trsd-chirp-proper-volume}
\end{equation}
This is the exact geometrical definition of stretching or recoil of a proper spatial volume. It is not
the centroid drift in~\eqref{eq:trsd-chirp-centroid}. In a locally isotropic patch, $h_{ij}=a^2\delta_{ij}$,
$\vartheta=3H$ and the corresponding recession/recoil speed at proper radius $R$ is
\begin{equation}
\boxed{v_{\rm space}(R)=HR=\frac{R}{3}\vartheta.}
\label{eq:trsd-chirp-space-velocity}
\end{equation}
For the relaxation contribution, replace $\vartheta$ by the component $\vartheta_{\rm rel}$ only when
the transfer current is the corresponding part of the phase congruence. The derived $v_{\rm space}$ is
therefore a metric-deformation velocity, not the incorrect force impulse $-t_{\rm rel}\mathbf g$.

\subsection*{5. The existing action supplies the metric response}

Varying the Einstein--Hilbert plus existing field action gives
\begin{equation}
G_{\mu\nu}+\Lambda g_{\mu\nu}=\frac{8\pi G}{c^4}T_{\mu\nu}^{\rm eff},
\label{eq:trsd-chirp-einstein}
\end{equation}
where $T_{\mu\nu}^{\rm eff}$ is the stress-energy of the baryons and the already-present scalar/phase
sector. Thus the post-collision metric is sourced by the surviving excited geometry as well as the
baryonic gas. In the weak-field, linear regime, harmonic gauge gives
\begin{equation}
\Box\bar h_{\mu\nu}=-\frac{16\pi G}{c^4}\,\delta T_{\mu\nu},\qquad
\bar h_{\mu\nu}(t,\mathbf x)=\frac{4G}{c^4}\int\frac{\delta T_{\mu\nu}(t-|\mathbf x-\mathbf x'|/c,\mathbf x')}
{|\mathbf x-\mathbf x'|}\,d^3x'.
\label{eq:trsd-chirp-retarded}
\end{equation}
The retarded solution depends on the source's past light cone, so the metric response is not an algebraic
instruction to erase the pre-collision geometry when the gas shocks. The continuing phase stress-energy
and the retarded metric response are the two existing carriers of the lag. The proper-volume recoil is
computed from that metric through~\eqref{eq:trsd-chirp-metric-volume}; it is not set equal to a guessed
multiple of $g$.

The same weak-field metric also gives the recoil velocity directly from the changing spatial dilation. With $S\simeq1-2\Phi/c^2$ and $\Phi=\Psi$, the spatial metric coefficient is $h_{ij}/a^2\simeq S\delta_{ij}$ to first order, while the physical length factor is $a_s=\sqrt S=e^{-\psi/2}$. This uses $S$ as the weak-field spatial metric coefficient, not as a conformal rescaling of all spacetime. At a proper radius $R$ corresponding to a fixed coordinate separation, to the weak-field order retained here,
\begin{equation}
\frac{\dot a_s}{a_s}=\frac12\partial_t\ln S=-\frac12\dot\psi\simeq-\frac{\dot\Phi}{c^2},\qquad
\boxed{v_{\rm rec}(R,t)=R\frac{\dot a_s}{a_s}=\frac{R}{2}\partial_t\ln S=-\frac{R}{2}\dot\psi\simeq-\frac{R}{c^2}\dot\Phi.}
\label{eq:trsd-chirp-vrecoil}
\end{equation}
This is the spatial-recoil velocity: the time derivative of proper spatial length as the sourced metric changes. It is different from the centroid drift $v_{\rm rel}$ and does not assume the incorrect impulse $-t_{\rm rel}\mathbf g$.

For the leading nonrelativistic phase-density contribution to the retarded potential, write $r=|\mathbf x-\mathbf x'|$ and
\begin{equation}
\Phi_\chi(t,\mathbf x)=-G\int\frac{\rho_\chi(t-r/c,\mathbf x')}{r}\,d^3x'.
\end{equation}
Differentiating at fixed field point, then applying the already-derived continuity equation $\partial_t\rho_\chi+\nabla'\!\cdot\mathbf J_\chi=0$ and integrating by parts (with the existing localized-source boundary condition), gives
\begin{align}
v_{{\rm rec},\chi}(R,t)&=-\frac{R}{c^2}\partial_t\Phi_\chi
=\frac{GR}{c^2}\int\frac{\partial_t\rho_\chi(t-r/c,\mathbf x')}{r}\,d^3x'\\
&=\boxed{\frac{GR}{c^2}\int\mathbf J_\chi(t-r/c,\mathbf x')\cdot\frac{\mathbf x-\mathbf x'}{|\mathbf x-\mathbf x'|^3}\,d^3x'.}
\label{eq:trsd-chirp-vrecoil-current}
\end{align}
This expression derives the leading weak-field recoil from the actual phase current generated by the framework's geodesic motion and relaxation map. The complete relativistic answer retains the full retarded $\delta T_{\mu\nu}$ in equation~\eqref{eq:trsd-chirp-retarded}; the displayed scalar-source integral is its nonrelativistic phase-density contribution. No streaming-speed amplitude is added. The sign and magnitude follow from the source current and the point at which the metric is evaluated.

For geodesic flow, the Raychaudhuri equation gives the evolution of the same expansion scalar:
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\frac{d\vartheta}{d\tau}=-\frac{\vartheta^2}{3}
-\sigma_{\mu\nu}\sigma^{\mu\nu}+\omega_{\mu\nu}\omega^{\mu\nu}
-R_{\mu\nu}u^\mu u^\nu.}
\label{eq:trsd-chirp-raychaudhuri}
\end{equation}
Tracing~\eqref{eq:trsd-chirp-einstein} in the stated stress-energy units gives
$R_{\mu\nu}u^\mu u^\nu=\frac{8\pi G}{c^4}(T_{\mu\nu}u^\mu u^\nu+T/2)-\Lambda$. For the existing pressureless phase
sector, this is fixed by its density; it is not an independent recoil force. The sign of the volume response
is determined by the metric solution, its initial congruence, and the stress-energy. A positive integrated
$\vartheta$ leaves a larger proper volume; a negative one contracts it. This is the mathematical distinction
between a residual spatial stretch and a label for a moving mass peak.

\subsection*{6. Dilation recoil as a domain expansion rate}

In the same weak-field slicing, the isotropic spatial metric including the background scale factor has $dV_{\rm proper}=a^3S^{3/2}d^3x$. Differentiating its determinant gives the expansion rate of a domain $D$:
\begin{equation}
\boxed{H_D\equiv\frac13\frac{d\ln V_{\rm proper}}{dt}=H+\frac12\langle\partial_t\ln S\rangle_D
=H-\frac12\langle\dot\psi\rangle_D\simeq H-\frac{\langle\dot\Phi\rangle_D}{c^2}.}
\label{eq:trsd-chirp-domain-H}
\end{equation}
The brackets denote the average with the domain proper-volume measure $S^{3/2}d^3x$ in this weak-field slicing (equivalent to coordinate-volume averaging at first order in the weak-field perturbation). This is a kinematic identity linking the existing dilation field to proper-volume growth; the field equation and the retarded source-current expression above determine its value. The relaxation centroid alone is not inserted into this equation as a space velocity.

Using the two expansion rates already tabulated in GRUT, $H_{\rm local}=73.04$ and $H_{\rm bg}=67.35\ \mathrm{km\,s^{-1}\,Mpc^{-1}}$, their difference is $\Delta H=5.69\ \mathrm{km\,s^{-1}\,Mpc^{-1}}=1.84\times10^{-19}\ \mathrm{s^{-1}}$. A homogeneous account of this difference would require
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\delta\langle\vartheta\rangle_D=3\Delta H=17.07\ \mathrm{km\,s^{-1}\,Mpc^{-1}}\simeq5.53\times10^{-19}\ \mathrm{s^{-1}},\qquad
\langle\partial_t\ln S\rangle_D=2\Delta H\simeq3.69\times10^{-19}\ \mathrm{s^{-1}}.}
\label{eq:trsd-chirp-H0-target}
\end{equation}
These are derived target values from the framework's stated rates, not fitted recoil inputs. The theory's prediction is the source-current integral's value; it explains the difference only if that calculation returns the required domain average.

\subsection*{7. Horizon information density and the expansion law}

The existing Hubble-horizon definitions give
\begin{equation}
I_H=\frac{\pi c^5}{\hbar G H^2},\qquad
V_H=\frac{4\pi}{3}\left(\frac{c}{H}\right)^3,\qquad
k_BT_H=\frac{\hbar H}{2\pi}.
\end{equation}
Dividing information by volume and eliminating $H$ yields
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\iota_H\equiv\frac{I_H}{V_H}=\frac{3c^2H}{4\hbar G},\qquad
H=\frac{4\hbar G}{3c^2}\iota_H,\qquad
V_H=\frac{9\pi c^9}{16\hbar^3G^3}\,\iota_H^{-3}.}
\label{eq:trsd-chirp-horizon-closure}
\end{equation}
Consequently, the horizon's logarithmic rates obey
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\frac{\dot\iota_H}{\iota_H}=\frac{\dot H}{H},\qquad
\frac{\dot V_H}{V_H}=-3\frac{\dot\iota_H}{\iota_H},\qquad
\frac{\dot T_H}{T_H}=\frac{\dot\iota_H}{\iota_H}.}
\label{eq:trsd-chirp-cooling}
\end{equation}
This is the exact horizon form of the proposed cooling/recoil relation: as $H$ and $T_H$ fall, the
horizon information density falls and its Hubble volume grows. It is not an independently fitted
streaming amplitude. For the flat-FLRW background already used in TRSD,
\begin{equation}
\dot H=-4\pi G\left(\rho+\frac{p}{c^2}\right),
\end{equation}
so
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\frac{\dot\iota_H}{\iota_H}=-\frac{4\pi G}{H}
\left(\rho+\frac{p}{c^2}\right),\qquad
\frac{\dot V_H}{V_H}=\frac{12\pi G}{H}\left(\rho+\frac{p}{c^2}\right).}
\label{eq:trsd-chirp-horizon-rate}
\end{equation}
For $H>0$ and $\rho+p/c^2\geq0$, the Hubble-horizon volume grows while its information density and
Gibbons--Hawking temperature fall. Separately, the proper volume of a comoving FLRW region obeys
$\dot V_{\rm com}/V_{\rm com}=3H$, and the same horizon identity gives
\begin{equation}
\boxed{\frac{\dot V_{\rm com}}{V_{\rm com}}=3H=\frac{4\hbar G}{c^2}\,\iota_H.}
\label{eq:trsd-chirp-comoving-rate}
\end{equation}
Thus the framework derives the exact horizon identities: $H$ is proportional to the Hubble-horizon information density and the Hubble-horizon volume is proportional to its inverse cube. When the solved background has decreasing $H$, the horizon volume grows while its information density and temperature fall. These identities are not a standalone field equation for $H$. Auxoviscausality, derived below, identifies the phase-flow integral of the proper-volume expansion as retained logarithmic strain; Raychaudhuri and Einstein determine its local load-dependent rate. The collision/relaxation sector supplies the local metric response through~\eqref{eq:trsd-chirp-einstein}--\eqref{eq:trsd-chirp-raychaudhuri}; its contribution to volume is the volume average of the resulting $\vartheta$, not the centroid speed substituted for it.

\subsection*{8. The derived recoil chain}

The complete equation chain is
\begin{equation*}
\begin{gathered}
\text{gas collision}\;\Rightarrow\;\text{baryonic momentum loss but geodesic phase flow},\\
f=1-e^{-t/t_{\rm rel}}\;\Rightarrow\;\mathbf v_{\rm rel}=\dot f\mathbf D+f\dot{\mathbf D},\\
\rho_\chi=q\rho_{\rm inf}+f\rho_{\rm pol}\;\Rightarrow\;\nabla\cdot\mathbf J_{\rm rel}=-\dot f(\rho_{\rm pol}-\rho_{\rm inf}),\\
T_{\mu\nu}^{\rm eff}\;\Rightarrow\;g_{\mu\nu}\;\Rightarrow\;\vartheta=\frac{d\ln V_{\rm proper}}{d\tau},\\
I_H,V_H,T_H\;\Rightarrow\;\iota_H\propto H,\quad V_H\propto\iota_H^{-3}.
\end{gathered}
\end{equation*}
No step adds a recoil force or external velocity. The sign and magnitude of a particular system's net proper-volume
change are obtained by solving the already-defined profile/current and metric equations with that system's
initial and boundary data; the framework does not permit assigning a universal positive sign to every local
transfer region independently of those equations.


% =====================================================================
\subsection*{9. Auxoviscausality: retained geometric memory}
\label{subsec:auxoviscausality}

\textbf{Definition and scope.} Auxoviscausality is the phase-flow property by which a local gravitationally sourced
change in proper spatial volume is recorded as accumulated logarithmic deformation rather than being returned to a
reference value by a restoring term. The operational variable is the logarithmic volume strain of a small comoving
flow tube, not a new matter density and not a count of newly created particles or topological cells. This definition
uses the existing fixed-norm phase field and adds no field, coupling, force, or independent velocity.

On a regular branch, let $u^\mu$ be the unit, geodesic, hypersurface-orthogonal phase-flow congruence already defined
in equation~\eqref{eq:trsd-chirp-action}, and write $D\equiv u^\mu\nabla_\mu$ for differentiation along one of its
integral curves. Let $V_{\rm prop}$ be the infinitesimal proper three-volume spanned by three neighboring flow lines,
with reference value $V_*$. The volume-determinant identity already derived in
 equation~\eqref{eq:trsd-chirp-metric-volume} is
\begin{equation}
 D\ln V_{\rm prop}=\vartheta,\qquad \vartheta\equiv\nabla_\mu u^\mu .
\label{eq:aux-volume-identity}
\end{equation}
We therefore define the dimensionless auxoviscausal strain by
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\mathcal A_{\rm aux}\equiv\frac13\ln\!\left(\frac{V_{\rm prop}}{V_*}\right),\qquad
 D\mathcal A_{\rm aux}=\frac13\vartheta.}
\label{eq:aux-volume-law}
\end{equation}
The factor $1/3$ makes $\mathcal A_{\rm aux}$ the logarithmic linear scale associated with a three-volume.
Integrating along the same phase-flow worldline gives the causal-memory form
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\mathcal A_{\rm aux}(\tau)=\mathcal A_{\rm aux}(\tau_0)
 +\frac13\int_{\tau_0}^{\tau}\vartheta(\tau')\,d\tau',\qquad
 V_{\rm prop}(\tau)=V_*e^{3\mathcal A_{\rm aux}(\tau)}.}
\label{eq:aux-memory-integral}
\end{equation}
Only the past segment $\tau'\leq\tau$ on that phase-flow curve contributes: causal order is carried by the
existing phase flow, not by an added nonlocal force or an acausal boundary condition.

\textbf{Derivation of the load equation.} The phase constraint already makes the congruence geodesic and
hypersurface-orthogonal, so its acceleration and vorticity vanish. The existing Raychaudhuri equation,
\eqref{eq:trsd-chirp-raychaudhuri}, then becomes
\begin{equation}
 D\vartheta=-\frac13\vartheta^2-\sigma_{\mu\nu}\sigma^{\mu\nu}
 -R_{\mu\nu}u^\mu u^\nu,
\label{eq:aux-raychaudhuri}
\end{equation}
where $\sigma_{\mu\nu}$ is the shear of the phase congruence. Define the signed Ricci-focusing load by
$\mathcal L_g\equiv R_{\mu\nu}u^\mu u^\nu$, so positive $\mathcal L_g$ is a focusing contribution in Raychaudhuri. Substituting
$\vartheta=3D\mathcal A_{\rm aux}$ into Raychaudhuri yields the covariant second-order memory equation
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{D^2\mathcal A_{\rm aux}+(D\mathcal A_{\rm aux})^2
 +\frac13\sigma_{\mu\nu}\sigma^{\mu\nu}=-\frac13\mathcal L_g.}
\label{eq:aux-load-response}
\end{equation}
This is the rigorous local link to gravitational load supplied by the existing geometry: the Ricci projection enters
through Einstein's equation and anisotropic deformation enters through the already-defined shear. The Ricci projection alone does not capture arbitrary anisotropic or tidal deformation; the phase congruence records that part through its shear.
Positive Ricci load focuses the congruence; the cosmological constant contributes with the opposite sign. The covariant equations use the framework's normalized phase-flow convention (natural units in Raychaudhuri); explicit factors of $c$ are restored in the FLRW source relation. No independent proportionality coefficient between load and strain is introduced.

For a homogeneous, isotropic FLRW branch, $\sigma_{\mu\nu}=0$, $V_{\rm prop}\propto a^3$, and
$\vartheta=3H$. Hence
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\mathcal A_{\rm aux}=\ln\!\frac{a}{a_0},\qquad
 \dot{\mathcal A}_{\rm aux}=H,\qquad
 \mathcal L_g=-3(\dot H+H^2)=-3\frac{\ddot a}{a}.}
\label{eq:aux-flrw}
\end{equation}
With mass density $\rho_m$, pressure $p$, and cosmological constant $\Lambda$ (inverse-length-squared units), the
standard Einstein--Friedmann acceleration equation gives
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\mathcal L_g=4\pi G\!\left(\rho_m+\frac{3p}{c^2}\right)-\Lambda c^2.}
\label{eq:aux-einstein-load}
\end{equation}
Thus the gravitational load drives the expansion history through the existing Einstein/Raychaudhuri system, and
Auxoviscausality records that solved history as $\mathcal A_{\rm aux}$. It does not replace the field equations with
an algebraic proportionality between strain and the instantaneous Ricci projection.

The horizon identities already derived in equation~\eqref{eq:trsd-chirp-horizon-closure} further imply, on the
FLRW branch,
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\dot{\mathcal A}_{\rm aux}=H=\frac{4\hbar G}{3c^2}\,\iota_H,\qquad
 \mathcal A_{\rm aux}(t)-\mathcal A_{\rm aux}(t_0)=
 \frac{4\hbar G}{3c^2}\int_{t_0}^{t}\iota_H(t')\,dt'.}
\label{eq:aux-horizon-link}
\end{equation}
This is the downstream link between the framework's horizon-information density and retained expansion strain;
$\dot V_{\rm prop}/V_{\rm prop}=3H$ remains exactly the previously derived volume law.

\textbf{Retention condition and limits.} Equation~\eqref{eq:aux-volume-law} contains no restoring term of the form
$-\mathcal A_{\rm aux}/\tau_r$. If the expansion rate settles to zero after a period of positive expansion, then
$D\mathcal A_{\rm aux}=0$ and the previously accumulated value remains; it is not driven back to zero. On an
expanding FLRW branch with $H\geq0$, $\mathcal A_{\rm aux}$ is nondecreasing. In a locally contracting region with
$\vartheta<0$, however, the signed geometric strain decreases with the actual proper volume; the present framework
does not artificially forbid real contraction. A strictly one-way microscopic damage/plasticity variable, a yield
threshold, and positive material entropy production do not follow from this geometric identity and would require an
additional irreversible constitutive sector. They are not needed for the Auxoviscausal geometric-memory closure.

\subsection*{9.1. Auxoviscausal memory at the conformal junction}
\label{subsec:auxoviscausal-junction}
The bulk law~\eqref{eq:aux-volume-law} is defined using physical proper volume on a regular aeon branch. It must not be
matched by setting the two limiting values of $\mathcal A_{\rm aux}$ equal: those values diverge with opposite signs
at the stated conformal junction. The existing metric identification~\eqref{eq:junc-metrics-closed} instead supplies a
finite, geometric matching prescription for the conformal-volume remainder, provided the regular conformal spatial
metric is nondegenerate on the junction and the same comoving flow tube is identifiable on its two sides.

Let $\hat h_{ij}$ be the induced spatial metric of the common conformal representative, and let $\hat V$ be the volume
of that same coordinate-labelled tube computed with $\hat h_{ij}$. The physical induced metrics and volumes are
\begin{equation}
 h_{ij}^{(-)}=b^{-2}\hat h_{ij},\qquad h_{ij}^{(+)}=b^{2}\hat h_{ij},\qquad
 V_{\rm prop}^{(-)}=b^{-3}\hat V,\qquad
 V_{\rm prop}^{(+)}=|b|^{3}\hat V .
\label{eq:aux-junction-volumes}
\end{equation}
Using the same reference volume $V_*$ in the branchwise definition therefore gives
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal A_{\rm aux}^{(-)}=-\ln b+\widehat{\mathcal A}_{\rm aux},\qquad
 \mathcal A_{\rm aux}^{(+)}=\ln|b|+\widehat{\mathcal A}_{\rm aux},\qquad
 \widehat{\mathcal A}_{\rm aux}\equiv\frac13\ln\!\left(\frac{\hat V}{V_*}\right).
\label{eq:aux-junction-regularization}
\end{equation}
The finite matching variable is thus fixed by the existing conformal-volume determinant; it is not an additional field or a
new transmission coefficient. Explicitly, under the regularity and tube-identification conditions stated above,
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\lim_{\eta\to\eta_c^-}\!\left(\mathcal A_{\rm aux}^{(-)}+\ln b\right)
 =\widehat{\mathcal A}_{{\rm aux},c}
 =\lim_{\eta\to\eta_c^+}\!\left(\mathcal A_{\rm aux}^{(+)}-\ln|b|\right)},
 \qquad
 \boxed{[\widehat{\mathcal A}_{\rm aux}]_-^+=0}.
\label{eq:aux-junction-match}
\end{equation}
This is the Auxoviscausal junction rule: the divergent isotropic scale contribution is reinitialized by the reciprocal
scale-factor continuation ($-\ln b$ on the old branch and $+\ln|b|$ on the new), while the finite conformal-volume
remainder is matched. It does \emph{not} make the unregularized $\mathcal A_{\rm aux}$ continuous, nor does it imply that
every matter degree of freedom or every microscopic record crosses the boundary.

The scope of the result matters. In exactly homogeneous FLRW for a fixed comoving tube, $\hat V$ is constant, so
$\widehat{\mathcal A}_{\rm aux}$ is only the reference-normalized conformal-volume datum; the accumulated $\ln a$ of
the old aeon is not preserved as a separate finite scalar. In an inhomogeneous branch, the finite remainder can encode
the volume determinant of the regular conformal geometry, but it still is not an independent ledger of the entire past
expansion integral. Retaining that entire integral as an extra cross-aeon state would require a new history variable and
matching dynamics absent from the present action. The derived result here is deliberately narrower: the geometric
volume remainder matches when the existing conformal geometry and the identified flow tube match.

Accordingly, the closed result is the phase-flow law~\eqref{eq:aux-volume-law}, its causal history integral
\eqref{eq:aux-memory-integral}, and its load-source form~\eqref{eq:aux-load-response}. The word
\emph{viscoelastic} is an analogy for retained geometric response here; it is not a claim that the existing
conservative phase action has derived microscopic viscosity or a material hysteresis law.


\subsection*{9.2. Local Auxoviscausal strain field and the Bullet Cluster}
\label{subsec:aux-local-field}

\textbf{Purpose.} The scalar $\mathcal A_{\rm aux}$ is local in space-time: it is evaluated on each worldline of the existing phase-flow congruence. It is not a spatially uniform cosmological number, and it is not an independent field with a freely chosen potential. This subsection specifies the local calculation required for a merging cluster without adding a new force or coupling.

Let $h_{ij}(x,\tau)$ be the induced proper spatial metric on hypersurfaces orthogonal to the phase flow, and let $dV_{\rm prop}=\sqrt{h}\,d^3q$ for a fixed infinitesimal coordinate cell $d^3q$. Relative to the same cell and a reference metric determinant $h_*$, the local strain is
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\mathcal A_{\rm aux}(x,\tau)=\frac16\ln\!\left(\frac{h(x,\tau)}{h_*(x)}\right),\qquad
 D\mathcal A_{\rm aux}=\frac13\vartheta.}
\label{eq:aux-local-determinant}
\end{equation}
The determinant formula is the local form of the proper-volume definition, not an extra field equation. Along each phase-flow worldline the evolution is fixed by
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{D^2\mathcal A_{\rm aux}=-\left(D\mathcal A_{\rm aux}\right)^2
 -\frac13\sigma_{\mu\nu}\sigma^{\mu\nu}-\frac13\mathcal L_g,\qquad
 \mathcal L_g\equiv R_{\mu\nu}u^\mu u^\nu.}
\label{eq:aux-local-evolution}
\end{equation}
This equation fixes the sign convention. $D\mathcal A_{\rm aux}>0$ means the flow-tube volume is currently expanding and the accumulated strain is increasing; $D\mathcal A_{\rm aux}<0$ means it is contracting and the signed strain is decreasing. Positive $\mathcal L_g$ contributes negatively to $D\vartheta$ and focuses the congruence, but it does \emph{not} by itself imply that $\mathcal A_{\rm aux}$ is already negative: the sign of the strain depends on its reference value and prior expansion history. Shear contributes a non-positive term to $D\vartheta$. A cosmological-constant contribution enters through the signed Ricci projection and has the opposite sign to positive matter focusing under the framework's stated Einstein convention.

In the weak-field, flow-aligned, approximately isotropic slicing already used for the dilation, $h_{ij}\simeq a^2 S\delta_{ij}$, so the same determinant gives
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\mathcal A_{\rm aux}(x,t)-\mathcal A_{\rm aux,*}
 =\ln\!\left(\frac{a}{a_*}\right)+\frac12\ln\!\left(\frac{S(x,t)}{S_*}\right),\qquad
 S\simeq1-\frac{2\Phi}{c^2}.}
\label{eq:aux-local-weakfield}
\end{equation}
To first order, the spatially varying part is $\delta\mathcal A_{\rm aux}\simeq-\delta\Phi/c^2$, and its local rate is $D\mathcal A_{\rm aux}\simeq H-\dot\Phi/c^2$ only when the chosen coordinates follow the same congruence to the retained weak-field order. This reproduces the sign in the existing recoil relation $v_{\rm rec}/R\simeq-\dot\Phi/c^2$; it does not justify treating a static potential offset as a continuing expansion rate. For a general anisotropic metric, equation~\eqref{eq:aux-local-determinant}, not the isotropic $S$ approximation, is the definition.

\textbf{Bullet Cluster prescription.} The local strain contrast between a phase-flow tube through a lensing/galaxy peak and a comparison tube through the X-ray gas is
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\Delta\mathcal A_{\rm aux}(\tau)=\Delta\mathcal A_{\rm aux}(\tau_0)
 +\frac13\int_{\tau_0}^{\tau}\!\left[\vartheta_{\rm peak}(\tau')-\vartheta_{\rm gas}(\tau')\right]d\tau'.}
\label{eq:aux-bullet-contrast}
\end{equation}
The two expansion scalars must be computed from the same solved metric and the corresponding phase-flow trajectories. The metric source must include the observed baryonic gas, galaxies, and the framework's existing phase/scalar stress-energy without double-counting any component. The observed lensing convergence and galaxy distribution constrain the gravitating map; X-ray surface brightness and temperature constrain the shocked gas; merger geometry and line-of-sight projection constrain initial and boundary data. The gas is collisional and shock-heated, while the phase congruence is treated according to its existing geodesic equation. The strain is therefore a derived local diagnostic of the solution, not an additional mass component and not a substitute for lensing.

This closes the \emph{definition and sign-consistent evolution equation} for the local Auxoviscausal strain field. A numerical Bullet Cluster strain map is not claimed here: it requires digitized observational maps, a specified merger initial-value reconstruction, and a solved metric/phase-flow solution. In particular, the centroid relaxation fraction $f(\Delta t)$ cannot be inserted as $\vartheta$; centroid drift and proper-volume expansion are different observables. The present equations state exactly what must be computed and what data determine it.

\subsection*{9.3. Cosmological matter, radiation, and thermodynamic closure}
\label{subsec:cosmo-matter-closure}

The cosmological model must evolve physical matter and radiation through changing thermodynamic regimes, not only a background expansion and a pressureless phase sector. This closure uses the standard baryonic, gauge, fermion, Higgs, electromagnetic, and radiation content already admitted by the framework's total action; it introduces no new fundamental field. Its purpose is to make the background and perturbation equations explicit and to prevent the radiation-era relation $p=\epsilon/3$ or the pressureless phase equation of state from being incorrectly applied to every component and epoch.

Write the total source schematically as $T_{\rm tot}^{\mu\nu}=T_b^{\mu\nu}+T_r^{\mu\nu}+T_\chi^{\mu\nu}+T_{\rm other}^{\mu\nu}$, with each existing scalar/defect contribution included exactly once in $T_{\rm other}^{\mu\nu}$. The metric field equation and the Bianchi identity require
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\nabla_\mu T_{\rm tot}^{\mu\nu}=0,\qquad
 \nabla_\mu T_i^{\mu\nu}=Q_i^\nu,\qquad \sum_i Q_i^\nu=0.}
\label{eq:cosmo-sector-conservation}
\end{equation}
The $Q_i^\nu$ terms describe transfer among already-present sectors and must be obtained from their interaction terms; they are not adjustable exchange functions. Where the action contains no interaction, the corresponding transfer is zero. The existing separately conserved phase current remains separately conserved unless the action is explicitly amended and re-derived.

For a homogeneous FLRW background, using energy density $\epsilon_i$ and pressure $p_i$, the continuity equations are
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\dot\epsilon_i+3H(\epsilon_i+p_i)=Q_i,\qquad \sum_i Q_i=0,}
\label{eq:cosmo-fluid-continuity}
\end{equation}
where $Q_i$ is the energy-transfer rate per proper volume in the comoving frame. The total equation of state is $p_i=p_i(\epsilon_i,T,n_j,\{x_j\})$ as appropriate to the regime, rather than a universal constant $w_i$. Radiation in the relativistic limit obeys $p_r=\epsilon_r/3$; a nonrelativistic collisionless phase component obeys its already-derived dust limit; baryons, photons, neutrinos, ionization states, and later neutral gas must use their applicable thermodynamic and kinetic closures.

Composition is tracked by number currents and reaction terms,
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\nabla_\mu(n_j u_j^\mu+J_j^\mu)=R_j,}
\label{eq:cosmo-species-balance}
\end{equation}
where $J_j^\mu$ is a diffusion/transport current and $R_j$ is the net production rate from the specified reactions. The reaction network must conserve every exactly conserved charge; any allowed violation (for example, an anomaly-mediated process) must follow the existing particle-physics sector rather than an arbitrary source. The equation of state, heat capacities, reaction rates, and transport coefficients are microphysical inputs to be sourced from established physics or derived from the framework's particle sector, not new free cosmological forces.

Radiation and relativistic species are evolved with a covariant kinetic equation,
\begin{equation}
 p^\mu\frac{\partial f}{\partial x^\mu}-\Gamma^\alpha_{\mu\nu}p^\mu p^\nu
 \frac{\partial f}{\partial p^\alpha}=C[f],
\label{eq:cosmo-boltzmann}
\end{equation}
where $f(x,p)$ is the distribution function and $C[f]$ is the collision operator for the relevant scattering, emission, absorption, and decay processes. In a fluid/radiation moment treatment, the same physics appears as emissivity, opacity, heat flux, and anisotropic stress; moment truncations must be stated and checked in their regime of validity. For thermalized components the entropy current obeys
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\nabla_\mu s^\mu=\sigma_s\geq0,}
\label{eq:cosmo-entropy}
\end{equation}
with entropy production attributed to actual collisions, diffusion, shocks, chemical reactions, or other specified irreversible processes—not inferred merely from the geometric strain $\mathcal A_{\rm aux}$.

The perturbation problem must use the same closure. In particular, pressure perturbations must separate adiabatic and non-adiabatic parts, $\delta p=c_s^2\delta\epsilon+\delta p_{\rm nad}$, and the calculation must retain anisotropic stress and the relevant radiation/particle hierarchy. The framework's scalar/defect perturbations and the metric response must then be evolved with these matter perturbations to generate its own transfer functions. Using a precomputed $\Lambda$CDM transfer function can remain a comparison baseline, but it is not a derivation of the framework's perturbation predictions.

This is a closed statement of the conservation and kinetic structure, not a claim that every $Q_i$, reaction rate, opacity, or perturbation kernel has already been derived. The model becomes numerically closed only after each component's equation of state, collision/reaction data, and initial conditions are specified and the coupled background and perturbation system is integrated. Low-temperature molecular opacity and cloud-condensate physics are specialized applications of this same matter/radiation machinery, not ingredients to insert unchanged into the early Universe. No numerical cosmological parameter is changed by this patch.


\section*{Update supplement: derivations and the status of the open equations}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Update supplement: derivations and the status of the open equations}
% =====================================================================
This supplement integrates the twenty-principle update ledger while keeping a strict distinction between an algebraic consequence,
a constitutive closure, an interpretation and a genuinely open matching problem. No field, coupling or boundary-transfer coefficient is added.
The merger timeline below is an astrophysical analogy for local collision and relaxation; it is not a derivation of a cosmological junction.

\subsection*{A. The $C_3$ relaxation scale and the exponential response}
From the framework's horizon mode and its one-third-cycle identification,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:update-C3-relax}
\omega_{C_3}=\frac{3\sqrt3}{2\pi}H,\qquad \omega_{C_3}t_{\rm rel}=\frac{2\pi}{3}
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad t_{\rm rel}=\frac{4\pi^2}{9\sqrt3\,H}.
\end{equation}
The numerical coefficient is therefore derived once the stated $C_3$ identification is accepted. The exponential response additionally uses a
first-order relaxation closure, $\dot f=(1-f)/t_{\rm rel}$, with $f(0)=0$. Separation of variables gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:update-f-relax}
\int_0^{f(t)}\frac{df'}{1-f'}=\frac{t}{t_{\rm rel}}
\quad\Longrightarrow\quad f(t)=1-e^{-t/t_{\rm rel}},\qquad q(t)=1-f=e^{-t/t_{\rm rel}}.
\end{equation}
Thus the exponential is the exact solution of the declared relaxation law, but it is not implied by geodesic flow or stress-energy conservation alone.
For a bound system formed at $t_f$ in matter domination, $t_f=2/(3H_f)$ and the framework's formation-epoch value of $t_{\rm rel}$ give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:update-f-age}
\frac{t_{\rm rel}}{t_f}=\frac{2\pi^2}{3\sqrt3}\equiv x_H\simeq3.80,\qquad
f=1-\exp\!\left[-\frac{D_{\rm age}-1}{x_H}\right],\quad D_{\rm age}\equiv\frac{t}{t_f}\ge1.
\end{equation}
For a merger, the clock is reset by definition at core passage and $t$ in~\eqref{eq:update-f-relax} means elapsed proper time after that event.
The cross-scale use of the same $t_{\rm rel}H$ is a framework identification, not an independently established cluster microphysics result.

\subsection*{B. Early-aeon horizon equations and horizon power}
For a spatially flat constant-$w$ epoch, $a\propto\tau^{2/[3(1+w)]}$, hence
$H=2/[3(1+w)\tau]$. With $\mathsf J=1/(2H^2t_P^2)$ and $N=4\pi\mathsf J$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:update-J-early}
\mathsf J=\frac98(1+w)^2\left(\frac{\tau}{t_P}\right)^2,\qquad
N=\frac{9\pi}{2}(1+w)^2\left(\frac{\tau}{t_P}\right)^2.
\end{equation}
Matter ($w=0$) gives $\mathsf J=9(\tau/t_P)^2/8$; radiation ($w=1/3$) gives $\mathsf J=2(\tau/t_P)^2$ and
$N=8\pi(\tau/t_P)^2$. Since $N_\infty=2\pi/(\omega^2t_P^2)$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:update-cell-ratio}
\frac{\widetilde N}{N_\infty}=(2\omega\widetilde\tau)^2\qquad\text{(radiation-dominated start)}.
\end{equation}
The Hubble-horizon energy is $E_H=c^5/(2GH)$. Using $\dot H=-\tfrac32(1+w_{\rm eff})H^2$ gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:update-horizon-power}
\dot E_H=-\frac{c^5}{2G}\frac{\dot H}{H^2}
=\frac34(1+w_{\rm eff})\frac{c^5}{G}
=3(1+w_{\rm eff})P_{\max},\qquad P_{\max}=\frac{c^5}{4G}.
\end{equation}
Consequently the radiation-era value is $4P_{\max}$ and the matter-era value is $3P_{\max}$. These are consequences of the Friedmann power-law epoch and the horizon-energy definition; the interpretation as a literal cosmic collision is separate.

\subsection*{C. Local condensation: weak-field scope}
For $\Phi=-GM/r$ and $r_s=2GM/c^2$, the stated weak-field metric identification gives
\begin{equation}\label{eq:update-local-deformation}
S=e^{-\psi}\simeq1-\frac{2\Phi}{c^2}=1+\frac{r_s}{r}
=1+\Omega\frac{R_H}{r},\qquad \Omega\equiv\frac{r_s}{R_H}.
\end{equation}
If $a_s=\sqrt S$, then to first order in $|\Phi|/c^2$,
$\dot a_s/a_s\simeq-\dot\Phi/c^2$. For inward motion with speed $|\dot r|$, $\dot\Phi=-g|\dot r|$ and therefore
\begin{equation}\label{eq:update-recoil-local}
 v_{\rm rec}(R)\equiv R\frac{\dot a_s}{a_s}\simeq\frac{g|\dot r|}{c^2}R.
\end{equation}
Equations~\eqref{eq:update-local-deformation}--\eqref{eq:update-recoil-local} are weak-field, quasi-static relations. They are not exact strong-field formulae, and $v_{\rm rec}$ is a metric-length rate in this approximation, not a new force or a signal velocity.

\subsection*{D. Hawking temperature and the conditional lifetime coefficient}
With $R_s=2GM/c^2$, $\mathsf J=R_s^2/(2\ell_P^2)=2GM^2/(\hbar c)$ and $S=2\pi\mathsf J$,
\begin{equation}\label{eq:update-Hawking-T}
\frac{d(Mc^2)}{dS}=\frac{\hbar c^3}{8\pi GM}\quad\Longrightarrow\quad k_BT_H=\frac{\hbar c^3}{8\pi GM}.
\end{equation}
For the ideal-blackbody approximation with two photon polarizations, $A=4\pi R_s^2$ and
$\sigma=\pi^2k_B^4/(60\hbar^3c^2)$ give
\begin{equation}\label{eq:update-Hawking-power}
P_{\gamma,\,\rm bb}=A\sigma T_H^4=\frac{\hbar c^6}{15360\pi G^2M^2},\qquad
\dot M=-\frac{P}{c^2}.
\end{equation}
Integrating from $M_0$ to zero yields
\begin{equation}\label{eq:update-Hawking-lifetime}
t_{\rm ev}^{\rm bb}=\frac{5120\pi G^2M_0^3}{\hbar c^4},\qquad
\mathsf J(t)=\mathsf J_0\left(1-\frac{t}{t_{\rm ev}^{\rm bb}}\right)^{2/3},\qquad
\dot{\mathsf J}=-\frac{1}{1920\pi}\frac{c}{R_s}.
\end{equation}
The coefficient $1/(1920\pi)$ is therefore the perfect-blackbody, photon-only coefficient. It is not the exact universal Hawking coefficient: the physical flux depends on greybody transmission factors and on all particle species accessible at the black-hole temperature. The semi-classical derivation also fails when $\mathsf J=O(1)$, so the endpoint remains open.

\subsection*{E. Merger phases as a local analogue, not a cosmic time-lapse}
The observations are interpreted with three physically distinct diagnostics: (i) before first core passage, subclusters approach and their gas is still associated with their own potentials; (ii) during/after core passage, the intracluster plasma shocks and is decelerated while galaxies and the lensing-inferred mass peaks can remain spatially offset; and (iii) later, the disturbed system evolves through orbital motion, shocks, and relaxation. The Bullet Cluster and MACS J0025.4$-$1222 are examples of lensing--gas separation; El Gordo is a high-redshift ongoing merger usually reconstructed as shortly after first core passage, with outgoing/returning solutions; Pandora's Cluster (Abell 2744) is a multi-subcluster collision, not a clean relaxed endpoint. Sources: \href{https://science.nasa.gov/image-detail/hubble-chandra-bulletcluster-stsci-01evvebdtedwkk5j4k58a3j734/}{NASA on the Bullet Cluster}, \href{https://science.nasa.gov/missions/chandra/a-clash-of-clusters/}{NASA on MACS J0025}, \href{https://doi.org/10.1093/mnras/stv1713}{El Gordo dynamical reconstruction}, and \href{https://science.nasa.gov/asset/hubble/pandoras-cluster-abell-2744/}{NASA on Abell 2744}.

These objects are different systems observed at different cosmic times and viewing geometries; they are not successive frames of one merger. Projection and time-since-pericentre degeneracies complicate phase assignments (see the 2026 review, \href{https://doi.org/10.3390/universe12080249}{Merging Galaxy Clusters and the Search for New Physics of Dark Matter}). The data support a collisionless gravitating component relative to the shocked gas under standard lensing/GR interpretations; they do not establish a conformal boundary, a sign flip of the scale factor, or information transfer between aeons.

The phase-current, curvature and conformal-information rows above are closed under their stated assumptions. The Auxoviscausal bulk law is separately closed in Section~\ref{sec:chirp-recoil}; its finite conformal-volume remainder has the conditional matching rule~\eqref{eq:aux-junction-match}. The microscopic origin of the zero of $b$, non-adiabatic/subhorizon transfer, the independent thermal state of the excited share, and a genuinely dissipative plasticity sector remain separate questions.

% =====================================================================
\section*{Junction closure: phase current, curvature perturbation, and conformal information}
\addcontentsline{toc}{section}{Junction closure: phase current, curvature perturbation, and conformal information}
% =====================================================================
The conformal junction is now closed at the level of the three transport channels used by the framework.
The closure does not add a new field, force, or phenomenological transmission coefficient. It follows from the
existing constrained phase action, the smooth conformal metric, and the absence of a junction-localized source
or surface action. The physical metrics remain distinct on the two sides:
\begin{equation}
 g_{\mu\nu}=b^{-2}\hat g_{\mu\nu},\qquad
 \widetilde g_{\mu\nu}=b^{2}\hat g_{\mu\nu},\qquad
 b(\eta_c)=0,\qquad b'(\eta_c)=-\omega\neq0 .
\label{eq:junc-metrics-closed}
\end{equation}
All matching statements below are made in the regular conformal description and then translated back to the
physical branches. This avoids incorrectly identifying the divergent pre-flip volume factor with the vanishing
post-flip volume factor.

\subsection*{J1. Phase norm and geodesic flow}
The phase constraint is
\begin{equation}
 X\equiv-g^{\mu\nu}\partial_\mu\theta\partial_\nu\theta=X_0=\frac{\omega^2}{c^2},
 \qquad u_\mu=\frac{\partial_\mu\theta}{\sqrt{X_0}} .
\label{eq:junc-phase-norm}
\end{equation}
Since the Hessian of a scalar is symmetric,
\begin{align}
 u^\nu\nabla_\nu u_\mu
 &=\frac{1}{X_0}\partial^\nu\theta\,\nabla_\nu\partial_\mu\theta
 =\frac{1}{2X_0}\nabla_\mu\!
 \left(\partial^\nu\theta\partial_\nu\theta\right) \\
 &= -\frac{1}{2X_0}\nabla_\mu X_0=0 .
\end{align}
Thus the phase congruence is exactly geodesic and hypersurface-orthogonal on every regular branch.

\subsection*{J2. Exact phase-current matching and $\mathcal T_\chi=1$}
The phase action already present in the framework is
\begin{equation}
 S_\theta=\int d^4x\sqrt{-g}\,\lambda(X-X_0).
\label{eq:junc-phase-action}
\end{equation}
Variation with respect to $\theta$ gives
\begin{equation}
 \nabla_\mu J^\mu=0,
 \qquad J^\mu\equiv\lambda g^{\mu\nu}\partial_\nu\theta .
\label{eq:junc-current}
\end{equation}
In a conformal-time foliation, the conserved canonical flux density is
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal F_\theta(\eta,\mathbf x)
 \equiv\sqrt{-g}\,J^\eta,
 \qquad \partial_\eta\mathcal F_\theta+\partial_i(\sqrt{-g}\,J^i)=0 .
\label{eq:junc-canonical-flux}
\end{equation}
Integrate the field equation over an infinitesimal Gaussian pillbox around $\Sigma_c:\eta=\eta_c$.
Because the action contains no junction-localized source or surface term, variation of the single global action
requires continuity of the canonical momentum conjugate to $\theta$:
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\left[\sqrt{-g}\,J^\mu n_\mu\right]_-^+=0}
 \qquad\Longleftrightarrow\qquad
 \boxed{\mathcal F_{\theta,+}=\mathcal F_{\theta,-}} .
\label{eq:junc-flux-match}
\end{equation}
This is the required junction condition; it is not an additional phenomenological transmission postulate.

For a homogeneous FLRW branch, $J^\mu=n u^\mu$ and $u^\eta\propto a^{-1}$. Hence
\begin{equation}
 \mathcal F_\theta\propto n a^3V_c,
\label{eq:junc-number-flux}
\end{equation}
where $V_c$ is a comoving volume. Therefore
\begin{equation}
 n_-a_-^3V_c=n_+a_+^3V_c\equiv N_\chi .
\label{eq:junc-number-match}
\end{equation}
On the pre-flip branch $a_-=b^{-1}$ and $n_-\propto b^3$; on the post-flip branch $a_+=b$ and $n_+\propto b^{-3}$.
The same conserved canonical flux is obtained from either side. Consequently the phase-quanta transmission coefficient is
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\mathcal T_\chi\equiv\frac{\mathcal F_{\theta,+}}{\mathcal F_{\theta,-}}=1.}
\label{eq:junc-Tchi}
\end{equation}
Thus the former partial result $n a^3V_c=\mathrm{const}$ on each branch is promoted to an exact cross-junction result,
provided the stated action is the complete junction action and no localized source is introduced.

\subsection*{J3. Curvature perturbation transport and $\mathcal M_{\mathcal R}=1$}
The previous Bardeen-potential argument is not used. The appropriate matching variable is the comoving curvature perturbation
because it is directly the scalar perturbation of the spatial conformal geometry. Write the scalar spatial metric as
\begin{equation}
 \gamma_{ij}=a^2 e^{2\mathcal R}\delta_{ij}+\cdots .
\label{eq:junc-spatial-metric}
\end{equation}
A homogeneous conformal rescaling changes $a$ but not $\mathcal R$. Therefore the same regular conformal metric on the two sides has
\begin{equation}
 \widehat\gamma_{ij}=\widehat a^{\,2}e^{2\mathcal R}\delta_{ij}+\cdots,
 \qquad
 [\widehat\gamma_{ij}]_-^+=0
 \quad\Longrightarrow\quad
 \boxed{[\mathcal R]_-^+=0} .
\label{eq:junc-R-cont}
\end{equation}
There is no junction-localized stress tensor in the stated action, so the conformal spatial geometry is the quantity whose
finite limit is matched. On each regular branch the pressureless adiabatic phase has
\begin{equation}
 \dot{\mathcal R}=-\frac{H}{\rho+p}\,\delta p_{\rm nad}
 +O\!\left(\frac{k^2}{a^2H^2}\right),
 \qquad \delta p_{\rm nad}=0,
\label{eq:junc-R-evolution}
\end{equation}
so the superhorizon adiabatic mode is constant away from the junction. Combining branch conservation with
\eqref{eq:junc-R-cont} gives the exact junction transfer law for that mode:
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\mathcal R_+(k)=\mathcal R_-(k)},
 \qquad
 \boxed{\mathcal M_{\mathcal R}(k)=1}
 \quad\text{(adiabatic, superhorizon mode).}
\label{eq:junc-MR}
\end{equation}
This does not assert that every subhorizon, entropy, anisotropic-stress, or additional-field mode has unit transfer.
Those modes are outside the closed single-component matching sector.

\subsection*{J4. Conformal information transport and $\mathcal M_{\mathcal I}=1$}
The information channel is the conformal, massless sector already identified by the framework. For a conformally invariant scalar,
the rescaled field $\widehat\chi$ has a regular action on the conformal geometry. In the locally flat conformal patch used for the
junction calculation,
\begin{equation}
 S_\chi=-\frac12\int d^4x\sqrt{-\widehat g}\,\widehat g^{\mu\nu}
 \partial_\mu\widehat\chi\partial_\nu\widehat\chi,
 \qquad \widehat\Box\widehat\chi=0 .
\label{eq:junc-chi-action}
\end{equation}
For each Fourier mode, the junction has no delta-function potential because $\widehat g_{\mu\nu}$ is smooth and the action has no
surface term. The variational junction conditions are therefore
\begin{equation}
 [\widehat\chi_k]_-^+=0,
 \qquad
 [\partial_\eta\widehat\chi_k]_-^+=0 .
\label{eq:junc-chi-match}
\end{equation}
Using the same global conformal mode basis on both sides,
\begin{equation}
 \widehat\chi_k=A_k e^{-ik\eta}+B_k e^{ik\eta},
\end{equation}
these two equations form an invertible two-by-two system whose unique solution is
\begin{equation}
 A_{k,+}=A_{k,-},\qquad B_{k,+}=B_{k,-}.
\end{equation}
Hence the local junction Bogoliubov map is
\begin{equation}
 \alpha_k=1,\qquad \beta_k=0,
 \qquad
 \boxed{\mathcal M_{\mathcal I}(k)=1} .
\label{eq:junc-MI}
\end{equation}
The map is unitary, so the von Neumann entropy of the complete conformal quantum state is unchanged by the junction.
This statement concerns the conformal information channel; it does not identify the reset cell count itself with a conserved entropy,
since the framework explicitly has $N_\infty\to0\to N_\infty$.

\subsection*{J5. Closed junction theorem}
Under the framework's existing assumptions—(i) $b(\eta_c)=0$ with a smooth conformal metric, (ii) the fixed-norm phase action,
(iii) no junction-localized source or surface action, and (iv) a conformally invariant massless information carrier—the junction transport
laws are therefore
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{
 \mathcal T_\chi=1,
 \qquad
 \mathcal M_{\mathcal R}(k)=1\ \text{for the adiabatic superhorizon mode},
 \qquad
 \mathcal M_{\mathcal I}(k)=1 .}
\label{eq:junc-closure}
\end{equation}
The conformal junction is consequently transparent to the conserved phase current, to the specified adiabatic curvature mode,
and to the conformal information channel. The former OPEN/partial junction entries are closed at this level.
The separate microscopic origin of the zero of $b$, any material dissipative plasticity law, and the transfer of non-adiabatic or subhorizon
perturbations remain outside this theorem. Auxoviscausal memory is governed by its bulk volume-flow law; its finite conformal-volume remainder has the conditional determinant-matching rule~\eqref{eq:aux-junction-match}, not an independently postulated transmission coefficient.

\subsection*{J6. Gauge quotient and hierarchy: corrected scope}
The global group used by GRUT/TRSD remains
\begin{equation}
 G_{\rm SM}=\frac{SU(3)_C\times SU(2)_L\times U(1)_Y}{\mathbb Z_6},
 \qquad \frac t3+T+Y\in\mathbb Z,
\end{equation}
where $T$ is the $SU(2)$ representation isospin, not $T_3$. The quotient is a global-form statement. Within the framework's
geometric construction, the $\mathbb Z_2$ and $\mathbb Z_3$ features are identified with the quotient, but this identification is a
model derivation rather than a general theorem of the Standard Model.
The absence of a simple unified gauge group means there are no SU(5)-type $X/Y$ gauge bosons and hence no SU(5)-type
\emph{gauge-mediated} proton-decay channel. The quotient does not, however, forbid the gauge-invariant dimension-six operator
$QQQL/\Lambda^2$; such higher-dimensional baryon violation must be separately suppressed.

For the hierarchy, H4 and the footprint relation give
\begin{equation}
 m_*^3=\frac{\hbar^2H_0}{2Gc},
 \qquad
 R_H=8\pi\ell_P\exp\!\left(\frac1\alpha+\frac{3\pi\alpha}{2}\right),
\end{equation}
so
\begin{equation}
 \boxed{\frac{Gm_p^2}{\hbar c}
 =\frac{K^2}{(16\pi)^{2/3}}
 \exp\!\left(-\frac{2}{3\alpha}-\pi\alpha\right)} .
\label{eq:junc-hierarchy}
\end{equation}
Using the framework value $K=19.7566\pm0.0002$ and $\alpha^{-1}=137.036$ gives
$5.9061\times10^{-39}$, consistent with the framework's quoted $5.906\times10^{-39}$. The earlier illustrative value $K=19.759$
would instead give approximately $5.9076\times10^{-39}$; it is not the value used in the released framework.
The hierarchy relation is therefore closed as a conditional consequence of H4 and the footprint balance, not as a consequence of
cluster-merger phenomenology.

\section{Variance analysis}\label{app:variance}
Variance $V=\text{TRSD}-\text{observed}$, pull $V/\sigma$; tiers: on track $|V/\sigma|<1$,
monitor $1\le|V/\sigma|<2$, material $|V/\sigma|\ge2$. Two further labels mark rows that are not
independent tests: \emph{calibration} (a calibrated value against the data it was chosen to
approach) and \emph{consistency} (a comparison with a fit sharing an input). The likelihood row
combines the scaled statistical error with the nuisance-treatment systematic (\S14.3).

calibrated alike converge on $67.3$ (\S21.7); with the $0.88\%$ scatter of the local rate they are $-4.8\sigma$ and $-6.1\sigma$. The withdrawn
$5/2$ budget (I5) had two material variances of its own, $\omega_b$ at $+2.5\sigma$ and DESI's $\Omega_m$ at $+2.8\sigma$; both are gone. Data:
\cite{McGaugh2016,Li2018,PitjevaPitjev2013,Planck2020,Brout2022,DES2024,DESI2025,DESI2026lya,Riess2022}.

\textbf{Bridge 1, matter fraction.} From Planck's ratios the three-component budget gives $0.31426$; the derived baryon ratio moves it by
$+0.00010$ ($12\%$) and the resonance's vacuum ratio $2.5804$ by $+0.00071$ ($88\%$), to $0.31507$; radiation and the neutrino masses give the
budget's $0.3160$, $0.1\sigma$ from Planck's $0.3153$.

\textbf{Bridge 2, Hubble constant.} From SH0ES ($73.04$) the CMB acoustic calibration within $\Lambda$CDM moves $H_0$ by $-5.68$
($99.8\%$) and the resonance budget at fixed $\theta_*$ by $-0.01$, to $67.35$: the ladder's offset is the offset between the two
calibrations, and the budget adds nothing to it.

\textbf{Galactic scale.} The published RAR scale and the rotation-curve likelihood both place the
prediction within $1\sigma$; the nuisance treatment is the dominant uncertainty, and the result
does not depend on the tail order.
The dark-matter calibration of draft 1 ($10^{-22}$ eV) is excluded by the Lyman-$\alpha$ forest and
moved to $\mdelta>2\times10^{-20}$ eV (\S21.6).
